STEP-BY-STEP TEXTURE MODDING:

Step 1: List textures in a bundle (optional)
  python list_textures.py "path/to/bundle"
  -> Shows hash, size, dimensions, format for each texture

Step 2: Extract textures to PNG (optional, for reference)
  python extract_all_textures.py "path/to/bundle" output_folder
  -> Extracts all textures as PNG files

Step 3: Create replacement texture
  - Edit the PNG in your image editor
  - Dimensions and format can be DIFFERENT (tool auto-resizes!)
  - Save as DDS using texconv or GIMP DDS plugin:
    texconv -f DXT5 -m 8 your_texture.png
  - Rename to <hash>.dds (e.g., 01f03948e18b4899.dds)
  - Place in a folder (e.g., my_textures/)

Step 4: Preview changes (optional)
  python replace_textures_v4.py "path/to/bundle" my_textures --dry-run
  -> Shows what would be replaced without modifying

Step 5: Apply replacement
  python replace_textures_v4.py "path/to/bundle" my_textures
  -> Creates .backup file automatically and replaces matching textures
  -> Auto-resizes textures to match original format and EXACT byte size

Step 6: Test in-game
  - Launch Gauntlet
  - Check if textures appear as expected
  - If issues, restore from .backup file

KEY IMPROVEMENTS IN V4:
  - No need to match dimensions - tool auto-resizes
  - No need to match format - tool converts DXT1/DXT5 automatically
  - No need to match file size - tool ensures EXACT byte match
  - Works on ALL bundle types (simple and complex)
  - Preserves streaming data gaps in complex bundles

================================================================================
13. COMPLETE MOD CREATION WORKFLOW (NEW - January 16, 2026)
================================================================================

This section documents how to create complete Gauntlet mods from scratch using
the create_full_bundle_system.py tool.

================================================================================
13A. BUNDLE LOADING WITHOUT MODIFYING bundle_database.data
================================================================================

UNDERSTANDING THE PROBLEM
-------------------------

Gauntlet's resource system normally requires:
1. Bundle file in contents/ folder
2. Entry in bundle_database.data mapping resources to bundles
3. Package definition loaded at game start

However, there are ways to work around this:

METHOD 1: TEXTURE REPLACEMENT (Recommended - No DB Modification)
----------------------------------------------------------------
Instead of adding NEW bundles, REPLACE textures in EXISTING bundles.
The bundle is already registered, so no DB changes needed!

  1. Create your replacement texture as DDS
  2. Name it with the hash of the texture you're replacing: <hash>.dds
  3. Use replace_textures_v4.py to inject into the original bundle
  
  Example:
    # Replace warrior diffuse texture
    python replace_textures_v4.py "contents/0164ad48615ba9ed" my_textures/
    # Where my_textures/ contains: 67111d9e39e1cabc.dds

  Advantages:
    - No bundle_database.data modification
    - No package changes needed
    - Works immediately
    - Easy to revert (restore .backup)

METHOD 2: VERMINTIDE-STYLE MOD LOADING (How Vermintide Does It)
---------------------------------------------------------------
Vermintide 2 uses a custom mod framework with its own bundle format:

  1. .mod file (Lua config) defines mod entry point and packages
  2. mod_bundle contains all resources with signature 0xF0000006
  3. Mod framework (VMF) hooks into game and loads bundles dynamically

The key is the mod framework itself:
  - VMF registers a hook on ResourcePackage.load()
  - Intercepts package loading and injects mod bundles
  - Uses its own resource registry separate from bundle_database

For Gauntlet, this would require:
  - A DLL injection or similar to intercept resource loading
  - Custom bundle loading code that bypasses the normal DB check

METHOD 3: GAUNTLET'S BUILT-IN PACKAGE MANAGER (Experimental)
------------------------------------------------------------
Gauntlet HAS the same PackageManager as Vermintide! In game.lua:

  _G.SPLASH_PACKAGES = {
      "resource_packages/scripts_base",
      "resource_packages/scripts_gui",
      "resource_packages/menu",
      "modules/menu/resources",
  }

The PackageManager.load_package() function:
  local package = Application.resource_package(path)  -- Looks up in DB
  ResourcePackage.load(package)
  ResourcePackage.flush(package)

CRITICAL LIMITATION: Application.resource_package() queries bundle_database!
The ONLY way around this is to either:
  a) Modify bundle_database.data (add_bundle_to_db.py does this)
  b) Hook at the C++ level (requires DLL injection)
  c) Replace resources in existing bundles (METHOD 1)

METHOD 4: ADD TO EXISTING BUNDLE (Hybrid Approach)
--------------------------------------------------
If you must add NEW resources, add them to an EXISTING bundle:

  1. Decompress an existing bundle
  2. Append your new resources to the resource list
  3. Update the entry table and hash table
  4. Recompress the bundle
  5. Update bundle_database.data with the new resources

This modifies both the bundle AND the database, but only touches
one database entry rather than adding a new one.

RECOMMENDED APPROACH FOR GAUNTLET
---------------------------------
Given Gauntlet's architecture, the practical options are:

1. TEXTURE REPLACEMENT - No DB changes, uses proven tools
2. DB MODIFICATION - Use add_bundle_to_db.py (one-time change)
3. DLL/LOADER HACK - Intercept C++ resource loading (complex)

For texture mods: Use Method 1 (replacement)
For new content: You MUST modify bundle_database.data

================================================================================
13B. CREATING EACH FILE TYPE FOR A COMPLETE BUNDLE  
================================================================================

A Gauntlet bundle can contain these resource types:

RESOURCE TYPE SUMMARY
---------------------
Type          | Hash               | Format
--------------+--------------------+----------------------------------
texture       | 0xCD4238C6A0C69E32 | Raw DDS file (no header)
material      | 0xEAC0B497876ADEDF | Binary struct (see below)
unit          | 0xE0A48D0BE9A7453F | Binary mesh data
lua           | 0x997CBC2F3AECB2CF | Raw Lua bytecode or source
package       | 0xAD9C6D9ED1E5E77A | Binary resource list
bones         | 0x18DEAD01056B72E9 | Skeleton data
animation     | 0x931E336D7646CC26 | Animation keyframes
particles     | 0x5EB3F6ECDDDE11CE | Particle system definition
state_machine | 0x333776E4E7A42AD7 | AI/behavior state machine
flow          | 0x897A83FF37DAB3DD | Visual scripting nodes
shader        | 0xF2CC7CC9F7CCE2B2 | Compiled shader

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TEXTURE FORMAT (type hash: 0xCD4238C6A0C69E32)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Gauntlet stores textures as RAW DDS files (starts with "DDS " magic).

Creating a texture for a bundle:
  1. Create/edit image in any image editor (PNG, TGA, etc.)
  2. Convert to DDS with proper format:
     
     texconv -f DXT5 -m 8 -y my_texture.png
     
     Common formats:
       DXT1 - Opaque textures, 4 bits/pixel (no alpha)
       DXT5 - Textures with alpha, 8 bits/pixel
       ATI2 - Normal maps, 8 bits/pixel (better quality)
  
  3. Rename to the path hash: <hash>.dds
  
     Example: warrior_diff.dds -> 67111d9e39e1cabc.dds
     (calculate hash: python create_full_bundle_system.py hash "characters/avatar_warrior/warrior_diff")

DDS REQUIREMENTS:
  - Power-of-2 dimensions (128, 256, 512, 1024, etc.)
  - Include mipmaps (texconv -m generates them)
  - Match original format for replacements

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
MATERIAL FORMAT (type hash: 0xEAC0B497876ADEDF)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Gauntlet materials are BINARY, not text like Vermintide source files!

Binary Material Structure:
  Offset  Type     Description
  ------  -------  -----------
  0x00    uint32   Version (always 0x0A = 10)
  0x04    uint32   Section count
  0x08    uint64   Shader hash (MurmurHash64 of shader name)
  0x10    uint64   Zero padding
  0x18+   ...      Texture slot entries and settings

Each texture slot entry (variable position):
  uint32   Slot index
  uint32   ThinHash (upper 32 bits of slot name hash)
  uint64   Texture hash (MurmurHash64 of texture path)

Common shader hashes:
  "base"           -> 0x0EFE985F44B8E8A9
  "skin"           -> 0x8A9B67B2D8DB8E5A
  "gui"            -> 0x45A6B83292D3C1F0

Common ThinHashes (upper 32 of slot name):
  "diffuse_map"    -> 0xCD4238C6 (from full hash)
  "normal_map"     -> depends on full hash calculation
  "specular_map"   -> depends on full hash calculation

CREATING A NEW MATERIAL (ADVANCED):
  Since Gauntlet materials are binary, you need to:
  1. Extract an existing material as a template
  2. Modify the texture hash values
  3. Re-pack into binary format

  Or use decode_materials.py to understand the structure, then
  create a material builder (not yet implemented).

EASIER APPROACH: Replace texture in existing material's slot by
  replacing the texture itself (Method 1 above).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
UNIT FORMAT (type hash: 0xE0A48D0BE9A7453F)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Units are 3D meshes with material slots and bone bindings.

Binary Unit Structure (summarized):
  - Header with counts (materials, bones, meshes)
  - Material slot table
  - Bone hierarchy
  - Mesh data (vertices, indices)
  - LOD levels

See FINDINGS section 9 for full unit format documentation.

CREATING A NEW UNIT:
  Would require a complete mesh exporter (not yet implemented).
  Current tools only EXTRACT units to OBJ, not create them.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
LUA FORMAT (type hash: 0xA14E8DFA2CD117E2)   [CORRECTED — was 0x997CBC2F3AECB2CF]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Lua scripts are stored as either:
  a) Raw Lua source code (text)
  b) Compiled Lua bytecode (LuaJIT)

Gauntlet appears to use LuaJIT bytecode for shipped scripts,
but the engine can load source code during development.

CREATING A LUA RESOURCE:
  1. Write your Lua script
  2. Calculate path hash:
     python create_full_bundle_system.py hash "lua/my_mod/script"
     
  3. For source (if engine supports it):
     Just include the raw .lua file content
     
  4. For bytecode:
     luajit -b script.lua script.out
     Include the bytecode as resource data

PRACTICAL APPROACH:
  Use the custom/ folder Lua loading system instead!
  The bootstrap.lua framework loads Lua from files, bypassing bundles.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PACKAGE FORMAT (type hash: 0xAD9C6D9ED1E5E77A)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Packages define which resources should be loaded together.

Binary Package Structure:
  Offset  Type     Description
  ------  -------  -----------
  0x00    uint32   Flags (0 = standalone, other = has dependencies)
  0x04    uint32   Resource count
  0x08+   ...      Resource entries

Each resource entry (16 bytes):
  uint64   Type hash
  uint64   Name hash

CREATING A PACKAGE:
  from create_full_bundle_system.py import TYPE_HASHES, murmur_hash64
  import struct
  
  resources = [
      ("texture", "custom/my_texture"),
      ("material", "custom/my_material"),
  ]
  
  data = struct.pack('<II', 0, len(resources))  # flags=0, count
  for type_name, path in resources:
      type_hash = TYPE_HASHES[type_name]
      name_hash = murmur_hash64(path)
      data += struct.pack('<QQ', type_hash, name_hash)

The deps=0 flag is critical for standalone packages - see section 9B.

================================================================================
13C. COMPLETE MOD BUNDLE STRUCTURE
================================================================================

A complete mod bundle for Gauntlet needs:

MINIMUM VIABLE MOD (Texture Replacement Only):
  my_mod/
    textures/
      67111d9e39e1cabc.dds  # Named by hash
  
  python replace_textures_v4.py contents/0164ad48615ba9ed my_mod/textures/
  -> Done! No bundle creation needed.

FULL CUSTOM BUNDLE (Requires DB Modification):
  my_mod_bundle/
    my_package.package      # Binary package resource
    my_texture.texture      # DDS file (renamed)
    my_material.material    # Binary material
  
  Steps:
    1. Create bundle: python create_full_bundle_system.py create_texture_bundle my_mod_bundle -o my_mod
    2. Copy to game: copy my_mod contents\
    3. Register: python add_bundle_to_db.py my_mod
    4. Add to package loading in Lua (requires hook)

VERMINTIDE MOD STRUCTURE (For Reference):
  loremasters-armoury.mod:           # Lua config file
    return {
      run = function()
        new_mod("Loremasters-Armoury", {
          mod_script = "scripts/mods/Loremasters-Armoury/Loremasters-Armoury",
        })
      end,
      packages = {
        "resource_packages/Loremasters-Armoury/Loremasters-Armoury",
      },
    }
  
  Loremasters-Armoury.package:       # Text format (Stingray source)
    texture = [ "textures/*" ]
    material = [ "materials/*" ]
    unit = [ "units/*" ]
    lua = [ "scripts/mods/Loremasters-Armoury/*" ]
  
  Compiled into 3ac73385950a26ea.mod_bundle (signature 0xF0000006)

  KEY DIFFERENCE: Vermintide uses a text-based source format that gets
  COMPILED into mod_bundle. Gauntlet uses pre-compiled binary bundles.

================================================================================
13D. PRACTICAL MODDING RECOMMENDATIONS
================================================================================

FOR TEXTURE MODS:
  Use texture replacement (Method 1). It's the simplest and most reliable.
  No bundle_database.data changes needed.

FOR LUA MODS:
  Use the custom/ folder and bootstrap.lua framework.
  Lua files are loaded from disk, bypassing the bundle system entirely.
  This is how all your existing mods work!

FOR NEW ASSETS (Models, Animations):
  You MUST modify bundle_database.data. Use add_bundle_to_db.py.
  This is a one-time change to the database.

FOR MATERIALS:
  Replace the TEXTURE that a material references, not the material itself.
  This avoids having to deal with the complex binary material format.

HYBRID APPROACH:
  1. Create your textures and replace them in existing bundles
  2. Use Lua hooks (via custom/ folder) to modify game behavior
  3. Only touch bundle_database.data if you need truly new content

================================================================================
14. C++ MOD LOADER ARCHITECTURE  *** NEW May 2026 ***
================================================================================

The Gauntlet mod loader is implemented as a dinput8.dll proxy that gets loaded
by the game at startup via the standard DLL search order. It uses MinHook to
intercept engine calls and inject custom bundles.

Source: GauntletModLoader/ (Visual Studio project)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
14A. DLL INJECTION MECHANISM
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

dinput8.dll proxy:
  - Exports all original DirectInput8 functions (forwarded to system DLL)
  - Custom DllMain() is called before any game code runs
  - On DLL_PROCESS_ATTACH: spawns init thread, hooks engine functions
  - Actual original dinput8.dll forwarded via get_real_directinput8()

Files:
  dllmain.cpp          — DLL entry point, MinHook initialization
  dinput8_proxy.cpp    — DirectInput8 export forwarding

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
14B. RESOURCE HOOK ARCHITECTURE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Key C++ source files:
  GauntletModLoader/src/resource_hooks.cpp   ~1900 lines — core injection logic
  GauntletModLoader/src/bundle_manager.cpp   — bundle database patching

INITIALIZATION PATH:
  1. DllMain → CreateThread → InitThread()
  2. InitThread() waits for game module to be fully loaded
  3. Calls ResourceHooks::Initialize()
  4. Hooks: CanGetResource, BundleDatabase_Open, and related functions

BUNDLE INJECTION (confirmed working):
  - PatchBundleDatabase() at line 1092 in resource_hooks.cpp
  - Calls BundleManager::InjectBundles() at line 1096
  - NOT dead code — fully active injection path

PREFLIGHT SYSTEM:
  - RunSmokeTest() at line 1141: calls g_originalCanGetResource() to verify
    that the package path resolves before attempting injection
  - Uses preflight transaction objects to batch injection without corrupting
    the live database if any entry fails

CONSOLE SYSTEM:
  - Separate in-game console overlay (IMGUI-based)
  - Receives print/warn/error output from engine Lua context
  - Text wrapping + copyable output (ctrl+C to copy selection)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
14C. BundleManager::InjectBundles
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Located in: GauntletModLoader/src/bundle_manager.cpp

Flow:
  1. Reads list of custom bundles from mod config
  2. Decompresses each custom bundle body
  3. Parses bundle TOC (using corrected layout: N+1 entries, 20-byte res-0 hdr)
  4. For each resource in the custom bundle:
     a. Checks if resource already exists in game database (skip if so)
     b. Appends resource entry to in-memory patched database copy
  5. Engine's BundleDatabase_Open hook redirects to patched copy

Database groups:
  - Native game database uses 189-entry groups per bundle (77 bundles)
  - Custom bundles get their own group appended after all native groups

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
14D. IMPORTANT FINDINGS ABOUT BUNDLES VIA C++ LAYER
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

BundleManager::InjectBundles IS reachable:
  - Previously believed to be dead code
  - Confirmed called from resource_hooks.cpp line 1096 inside PatchBundleDatabase()

c2 field validation:
  - Engine does NOT validate c2 in package resource entries at runtime
  - c2=0 is safe for all custom bundles

Native bundle loading:
  - Native bundles load normally via the standard BundleDatabase_Open path
  - Custom bundles injected after native database is parsed — no interference
  - The 20-byte res-0 header anomaly is handled correctly in the C++ parser

================================================================================
9H. LEVEL RESOURCE FORMAT  *** NEW -- May 2026 ***
================================================================================

Type hash: 0xE7F01A5344F7C5A3  (MurmurHash64 of "level")
Sample file: Exports/samples/level_24abcdf4b1fb4d4a.level  (1136 bytes)
Tool: resource_tools/level_tools.py  (selftest: PASS, byte-exact)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9H.1 OVERALL LAYOUT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  HEADER (128 bytes):  16 x { uint32 field_a, uint32 field_b }

  Entry [0]:  { entities_offset, entity_count }
              e.g. { 128, 6 } = 6 entity records starting at byte 128

  Entry [1]:  { 1, unknown_size }  -- format version marker

  Entries [2..15]:  mixed section offsets and sentinel values
    - When field_a == field_b: sentinel marking empty/end sections
    - Other entries point to sub-sections within the file

  Known section offsets in the sample:
    @128  entity records  (count x 80 bytes)
    @608  resource path / layer name config section
    @720  small sub-table (64 bytes; likely LOD/section lookup)
    @784  empty
    @824  lighting / render environment (256 bytes; 4x4 identity matrices)
    @1080 index/offset back-references
    @1088 sentinel (field_a == field_b)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9H.2 ENTITY RECORDS (80 bytes each)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Entities with template_hash != 0 are PLACED UNITS:

    Offset  Size  Field           Description
    ------  ----  -----           -----------
     0       8    entity_id       uint64: unique instance hash (0 = anonymous)
     8       8    template_hash   uint64: MurmurHash64 of unit/prefab name
    16       8    flags           uint64: unknown, usually 0
    24      12    position        float[3]: world XYZ position
    36       8    _pad_0          unknown floats (usually 0)
    44      12    rotation_abc    float[3]: rotation components
    56       4    scale           float: uniform scale (1.0, 8.0, 64.0 observed)
    60       4    scale_w         float: secondary scale component
    64       8    _pad_1          unknown floats
    72       8    _tail_hash      uint64: extra hash/component reference

  Entities with template_hash == 0 are SPECIAL ENTITIES (world/env settings).
  Their @24..71 region stores hashes rather than floats (preserved as raw hex).

  Rotation encoding: three floats, likely a partial basis vector.
  Values observed: (-0.707, -0.707, 1.0), (0.0, 0.0, 0.0)
  NOT a standard quaternion (4 floats). Full decode TBD.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9H.3 RESOURCE CONFIG SECTION  (@608 in sample)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  uint32 descriptors interleaved with Pascal-style null-terminated strings:
    { uint32 str_len_incl_null, char[str_len] null-terminated-string }
    Strings are padded to 4-byte boundary.

  Observed strings:
    'core/rendering/default_outdoor'  -- sky / render environment preset
    'default'                         -- default layer name

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9H.4 EDITING NOTES
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  To MOVE a placed unit:  edit position[0], position[1], position[2]
  To CHANGE a unit type:  edit template_hash to target unit name hash
  To SCALE a unit:        edit scale (float)
  To CHANGE sky preset:   needs full config-section parser (TBD)

================================================================================
9I. ADDITIONAL TYPE HASHES  *** NEW -- May 2026 ***
================================================================================

  Type hashes found in Gauntlet bundles NOT in the original 16-type list:

  Hash                  Identified Type     Notes
  ----                  ---------------     -----
  0x712D6E3DD1024C9C    wwise_bank          16-byte header + raw Ogg Vorbis.
                                            Header: { uint32 data_size, 12x 0 }
                                            Strip 16 bytes to get .ogg audio.
                                            40 entries in audio bundle.

  0x76015845A6003765    wwise_dep           Pure XML, no binary header.
                                            <theme time_signature="4/4" ...>
                                            Wwise music metadata / timing.

  0x92D3EE038EEB610D    unknown (flow?)     Structured binary, ~2780 bytes.
                                            Likely Stingray Flow graph or
                                            state machine. Not yet decoded.
                                            Header: {48,52,19,244,2,396,420}

  0x99736BE1FFF739A4    unknown (navmesh?)  8.5 MB binary blob.
                                            uint64=34 then hash array.
                                            Likely pre-compiled navmesh or
                                            Havok physics world cache.

  PHYSICS / MESH standalone types:
    0x5F7203C8F280DAB8 (physics) -- NOT present in any Gauntlet bundle
    0x48FF313713A997A1 (mesh)    -- NOT present as standalone type
    Both are EMBEDDED inside .unit resources.

================================================================================
15. GAUNTLETMODLOADER RUNTIME STABILIZATION FINDINGS  *** NEW -- May 31, 2026 ***
================================================================================

SESSION RESULT (VERIFIED):
  - No hangs, no crashes, no freezes during custom bundle probe sequence.
  - Bundle loading remained stable across baseline and follow-up probe runs.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
15A. VALID BUNDLE FOLDER CONTRACT (CURRENT LOADER)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Per-bundle folder (mods/bundles/<ModName>/) must contain:
  1) bundle.ini (required)
  2) <bundle_hash_hex> binary bundle file (required)
  3) <bundle_hash_hex>.stream (required only when HasStream=true; otherwise
     loader can create empty companion at target)
  4) manifest.json (optional but validated if present)
  5) vmf.mod (optional but validated if present)

Required bundle.ini fields ([Bundle]):
  - PackagePath
  - ResourceCount (>0)
  - HasStream (true/false)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
15B. VALIDATION GATES NOW CONFIRMED IN PRACTICE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The loader accepts/rejects bundles using these gates:

  1) Package hash parity gate:
     - MurmurHash64(lowercase canonical PackagePath) must match the bundle
       filename (16-char lowercase hex).

  2) vmf.mod consistency gate (if vmf.mod exists):
     - vmf.mod packages[1] must match bundle.ini PackagePath.
     - Mismatch causes bundle skip.

  3) manifest.json consistency gate (if manifest exists):
     - manifest package_path must match bundle.ini PackagePath.
     - manifest resource_count must match bundle.ini ResourceCount.
     - Mismatch causes bundle skip.

  4) Stream gate:
     - HasStream=true requires source .stream file.
     - HasStream=false allows empty .stream creation at target.

  5) Duplicate patch-set guards:
     - duplicate bundle hash and duplicate package hash collisions are rejected
       in preflight patch queue.

Important: Manifest "resources" member/type_hash rows are not deep semantic
validated by loader; runtime payload integrity is still enforced by engine load.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
15C. PACKAGE PATH CANONICALIZATION RULES (LOADER + RUNTIME)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Canonicalization used for lookup/hash stability:
  - Trim whitespace
  - Remove leading '@'
  - Convert '\\' to '/'
  - Collapse duplicate '/'
  - Remove leading './' and leading '/'
  - Lowercase all characters
  - Strip '.package' suffix for package path keys

Practical requirement:
  Keep package paths consistent across bundle.ini, manifest.json, vmf.mod,
  and Lua probe calls.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
15D. RUNTIME LOAD BEHAVIOR (CUSTOM BUNDLES)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Confirmed stable custom load flow:
  - Mods.resource.load(...) queues custom package handles.
  - Queue processing is deferred until native startup settles.
  - Settle gate uses:
      * seen native milestone: resource_packages/scripts_settings
      * native package activity idle window
  - At safe point, custom package load is started via Game.load_package(..., true)
    (synchronous fail-closed behavior).
  - Queue advancement is pumped from high-frequency
    Application.resource_package hook return paths.
  - Reentrancy guards prevent nested queue recursion.

Observed effect in this session:
  - Eliminated previous freeze/hang behavior in active probe flow.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
15E. VMF-STYLE COMPATIBILITY LAYER STATUS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

VMF-style descriptor tooling and checks are now active:
  - tools/generate_vmf_bundle_descriptors.sh generates vmf.mod from bundle.ini.
  - vmf.mod package path validation is enforced when descriptor exists.
  - This catches metadata drift early before runtime load.

Reference note:
  - External VMF mods (e.g., Loremasters-Armoury) are useful for descriptor and
    folder convention comparison.
  - They are not by themselves proof of payload safety for Gauntlet custom
    binary bundles; payload correctness must still be tested in-engine.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
15F. BUNDLE TEST PRIORITY WORKFLOW (CURRENT RECOMMENDED)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1) Regenerate vmf.mod descriptors from bundle.ini.
2) Verify bundle.ini/manifest.json/vmf.mod consistency.
3) Run known-good probe bundle first (baseline).
4) Run suspect bundle probe second.
5) If baseline passes and suspect fails/hangs, treat as payload-level issue,
   not structural metadata issue.

Current status (May 31, 2026):
  - Baseline + follow-up probe run completed with no hangs/crashes/freezes.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
15G. GLB->UNIT PIPELINE FREEZE ROOT CAUSE (May 31, 2026)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Symptom:
  - Package load path succeeded (`Game:load_package`), but game froze when
    loading bundle using generated cube unit payload.

Byte-level mismatch identified:
  - Previous generated unit from `gltf_to_unit.py`:
      * 1,003 bytes
      * vertex stride 32
      * elements: POSITION/NORMAL/UV only
      * material name from DCC mesh (`Cube`)
  - Known-good runtime probe unit:
      * 43,403 bytes
      * vertex stride 56
      * elements: POSITION/NORMAL/BINORMAL/TANGENT/UV
      * engine-style material names (`m_*`)

Pipeline issues confirmed:
  1) Encoder emitted minimalist vertex declaration unlike stable game units.
  2) Encoder carried arbitrary GLB material names that may not resolve to
     runtime material resources.
  3) UV V-coordinate was being flipped twice in GLB path
     (read_glb flip + vertex pack flip).

Fix applied in tooling (`gltf_to_unit.py`):
  - Generate BINORMAL + TANGENT channels (game-like 56-byte layout).
  - Map non-engine material names to safe material key `m_ham` for stability
    tests.
  - Remove read_glb V-flip; keep single V-flip in final vertex write path.

Post-fix validation:
  - New generated cube unit: 1,614 bytes
  - Parser confirms stride 56 + 5 elements + material `m_ham`.
  - Bundle rebuilt/deployed with same package path/hash and same DEV spawn path.

Deeper structural finding after further freeze test:
  - Native `.unit` files do not end at the last parsed mesh block.
  - Sample native units consistently contain non-zero trailing data after mesh
    parsing completes.
  - Smallest observed native trailer is 361 bytes and appears to be a required
    global footer/metadata block for simple static units.
  - Generated units from `gltf_to_unit.py` had `trailing=0`, which is now a
    stronger freeze suspect than vertex layout alone.

Current probe implementation:
  - `mod_bundle_builder.py` now appends a synthesized 361-byte native-style
    footer for generated static units that otherwise end immediately after the
    mesh block.
  - Footer includes the generated unit resource hash at the native footer hash
    slot and uses a minimal native content key/template derived from a simple
    one-mesh game unit.
  - Generated probe unit now validates as:
      * 1,978 bytes total
      * 56-byte stride
      * trailing footer length 361
      * embedded unit hash = `0x7153D7BA6F64F835`
      * material key = `m_sphere`

Open question:
  - The 361-byte footer contains at least one additional content-dependent
    32-bit field that is not yet fully decoded. It correlates with native unit
    family/material usage and may represent a content fingerprint or runtime
    section identifier.

Spawn-stage follow-up (later May 31, 2026):
  - Package load succeeded with the synthesized 361-byte footer, but spawning
    the unit still crashed.
  - Native hash mapping showed the 361-byte footer samples came from
    `effects/*` units, not `gameobjects/*` world units.
  - New probe path keeps a real one-mesh `gameobjects/*` trailer intact
    (`gameobjects/food/ham_plate`, trailing size 2134) and rewrites only the
    mesh vertex data/bounds into a cube-like shape.
  - Resulting probe payload:
      * `cube_from_template.unit`
      * 8660 bytes
      * 1 mesh / 1 material / 2134-byte native world-unit trailer preserved
      * cube bounds = [-0.5, +0.5] on all axes
  - This probe is designed to separate trailer/schema correctness from mesh
    geometry correctness.

Latest validation (later May 31, 2026):
  - The `cube_from_template.unit` probe spawned successfully in-game with NO
    crash.
  - Spawned visual was new (not the previous ham/placeholder), confirming the
    mesh rewrite is taking effect at runtime.
  - Spawned visual was still not an actual cube.

Interpretation:
  - Spawn safety issue is now substantially resolved for this probe path
    (world-unit trailer/schema is accepted by runtime spawn).
  - Remaining problem is content correctness of geometry/topology, not package
    load or basic unit instantiation.
  - Rewriting vertex positions in an arbitrary native mesh shell can produce a
    stable but non-cube result because original index topology/shapes/material
    split remain from the source unit.

Next technical target:
  - Build a true cube topology payload (24 verts / 36 indices with clean face
    groups) while preserving a valid world-unit trailer family, or fully decode
    and regenerate the post-mesh trailer sections for custom topology.

Fallback-like spawn investigation (later May 31, 2026):
  - User observed spawned unit looked like an unrelated in-game unit rather than
    the intended custom cube.
  - Lua path fallback was ruled out:
      * `AddUtility.spawn_unit_path` calls `World.spawn_unit(world, unit_path, ...)`
        directly with the provided full path.
      * No short-lookup fallback is used in this code path.
  - Binary inspection of `cube_from_template.unit` revealed embedded native
    template unit hash bytes for `gameobjects/food/ham_plate`
    (`0x8AEED77B10003A6C`) still present in trailer metadata.
  - The custom unit hash for `units/custom/simple_cube_model_test/cube`
    (`0x7153D7BA6F64F835`) was absent.

Interpretation:
  - Runtime was likely resolving/aliasing by internal unit identity metadata,
    making the spawned object appear to come from native game content even when
    package path and spawn call were custom.

Mitigation applied:
  - `cubify_unit_template.py` now supports optional target unit resource path
    and rewrites embedded template hash occurrences to the target hash.
  - Regenerated `cube_from_template.unit` now has:
      * old template hash occurrences: 0
      * custom hash occurrences: 1
      * preserved one-mesh world-unit trailer size: 2134

Follow-up crash result (same day):
  - Rewriting only the trailing self-identity hash from template
    `0x8AEED77B10003A6C` to custom `0x7153D7BA6F64F835` caused spawn-time crash.
  - Binary diff showed only a single 64-bit trailer field changed (7 byte
    deltas due one identical byte), confirming crash sensitivity to that field.
  - Interpretation: self-identity hash in world-unit trailer is coupled to
    additional metadata/dependency records that were not updated in tandem.
  - Restored stable baseline by reverting to the non-rewritten template hash
    variant (`cube_from_template.unit`), which avoids crash but can still render
    as native-like content.

Dependency-alias probe (active):
  - Working theory: trailer tail hash is a dependency key (likely shared base
    resource identity across unit-adjacent resources), not a free-form unit id.
  - Rewriting that field alone to custom hash crashes spawn due unresolved or
    inconsistent coupled metadata.
  - New experiment keeps stable trailer hash in payload (`ham_plate`), but adds
    a second bundled unit resource at path `gameobjects/food/ham_plate` pointing
    to the same modified `cube_from_template.unit` blob.
  - Bundle now contains both unit entries:
      * `units/custom/simple_cube_model_test/cube`
      * `gameobjects/food/ham_plate`
  - Goal: force trailer dependency resolution to custom bundled payload at the
    referenced hash without mutating crash-sensitive trailer identity fields.

Additional binary evidence (same session):
  - Diff between native `ham_plate` unit and `cube_from_template.unit`:
      * file sizes identical (8660)
      * total byte diffs: 3964
      * diffs concentrated in vertex buffer (3940 bytes) + bbox region (24)
      * trailer bytes unchanged in stable variant
  - This strongly indicates inline mesh data is being modified and consumed,
    rather than a pure path-level fallback replacing the entire unit blob.

Cube-geometry verification probe:
  - Updated cubify transform to snap all axes to cube corners (not dominant-axis
    projection).
  - Verified resulting payload now has:
      * 8 unique vertex positions
      * x/y/z coordinate sets exactly {-0.5, +0.5}
  - Bundle rebuilt/deployed with same stable trailer/dependency-alias setup.
  - If runtime visual still appears non-cube, remaining cause is likely
    dependency/render path behavior (e.g., trailer-coupled external references or
    material/shader interpretation), not vertex coordinate generation.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
15H. .UNIT FILES — WHAT WE NOW KNOW (WORKING FIELD GUIDE)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

This section consolidates the current best understanding of Gauntlet `.unit`
runtime payload behavior from byte-level analysis and live in-game probes.

1) PRACTICAL MENTAL MODEL (REQUIRED FOR MODDING)
------------------------------------------------

Think of a `.unit` as three layers:

  A) Mesh block(s) at file start
     - Vertex buffer, index buffer, shapes, bbox, material strings.
     - This is where visible geometry is encoded.

  B) Post-mesh trailer metadata
     - Variable length, required by runtime behavior.
     - Encodes additional identity/dependency records.

  C) Tail identity record (near file end)
     - Includes an 8-byte hash-like reference field.
     - Not safely editable in isolation.

Key consequence:
  A valid mesh block alone is NOT sufficient for safe runtime spawn.


2) VERIFIED BASE STRUCTURE (MESH BLOCK)
---------------------------------------

Confirmed parser-compatible layout at file start:

  uint32 signature = 0x00010090
  uint32 mesh_count
  repeated per mesh:
    uint32 vertex_buffer_size
    bytes[vertex_buffer_size] vertex_data
    uint64 padding
    uint32 vertex_count
    uint32 vertex_stride
    uint32 element_count
    element_count * 17-byte element descriptors
    12-byte padding
    uint32 total_index_count
    uint32 index_buffer_size
    bytes[index_buffer_size] index_data
    uint32 shape_count
    shape_count * 16-byte shapes
    7 * float32 bbox
    uint32 material_count
    repeated: uint32 name_len + bytes[name]

Observed stable runtime-friendly vertex declaration:
  - stride 56
  - elements: POSITION, NORMAL, BINORMAL, TANGENT, UV


3) TRAILER FACTS (CRITICAL)
---------------------------

Verified across 582 sampled native units:

  - Trailer length is never zero in native samples.
  - Trailer length varies by unit family/content.
  - Simple effects family may use small trailer (361 bytes).
  - World/gameobject units commonly use larger trailers (e.g., 2134 bytes).

Critical coupling behavior:
  - Editing trailer identity hash field alone causes spawn crash.
  - Therefore trailer fields are interdependent and likely reference-coupled.


4) HASH/IDENTITY BEHAVIOR (PROVEN)
----------------------------------

  - `AddUtility.spawn_unit_path` is direct `World.spawn_unit(...)` path call;
    no short-name fallback in that code path.
  - Nevertheless, runtime result can appear native-like if internal unit
    metadata references native identity/dependency hashes.

Observed on `cube_from_template.unit`:
  - Preserved native hash: `0x8AEED77B10003A6C` (`gameobjects/food/ham_plate`)
    in trailer region.
  - Replacing this with custom hash only:
      * changed one 64-bit trailer field
      * produced spawn-time crash

Conclusion:
  - This field is not a free rename slot; it is coupled to other trailer
    records and/or companion resource keys.


5) WORKING/FAILING CONFIGURATIONS SUMMARY
-----------------------------------------

FAIL: minimal generated unit (no trailer)
  - package load freeze/hang risk

FAIL: effect-family 361-byte trailer used for world spawn
  - load may succeed, spawn unstable

PASS (spawn stable):
  - template-derived world-unit shell (`ham_plate` family trailer 2134 bytes)
  - mesh rewritten in-place
  - trailer identity left in stable state

FAIL (spawn crash):
  - same as above, but trailer self-identity hash rewritten to custom hash only

ACTIVE mitigation strategy:
  - keep crash-safe trailer identity
  - add bundled alias unit entry for referenced path/hash
    (`gameobjects/food/ham_plate`) so dependency resolution remains custom-side


6) GEOMETRY PIPELINE LESSONS
----------------------------

Initial issue:
  - vertex remap to dominant axis produced cube-like bounds, but not a real
    closed cube topology.

Improved probe:
  - force positions to true cube corners (8 unique positions, each axis in
    {-0.5, +0.5}).

Latest user-confirmed milestone:
  - Spawn now shows a cube in game.
  - Physics is applied at origin (expected from reused world-unit shell).
  - Remaining issue reported: missing top face.

Top-face response applied:
  - `cubify_unit_template.py` now rewrites index buffer to deterministic cube
    triangles (all 6 faces) and mirrored winding for culling robustness.
  - Bundle rebuilt/deployed with this topology fix.


7) WHAT MUST BE TRUE FOR A SAFE CUSTOM .UNIT TODAY
---------------------------------------------------

Minimum currently validated safe constraints:

  - Use a known-good world-unit trailer family as shell.
  - Keep runtime-accepted trailer identity coupling intact unless all linked
    trailer fields are updated together.
  - Maintain stride/elements expected by native family (56-byte P/N/B/T/UV).
  - Keep package resource entries and path hashes consistent.
  - Validate in game at two stages:
      1) package load stability
      2) world spawn stability + visual correctness


8) OPEN REVERSE-ENGINEERING TARGETS
-----------------------------------

Still required for fully independent custom units:

  - Full decode of world-unit trailer sections (especially 2134-byte family).
  - Identification of all fields coupled to trailer identity hash.
  - Deterministic encoder for trailer metadata (not template reuse).
  - Clean topology/material authoring path without native-shell dependencies.


9) CURRENT RECOMMENDED WORKFLOW (UNTIL FULL DECODE)
---------------------------------------------------

  1) Start from known-good world-unit shell.
  2) Rewrite mesh vertices (and now indices) only.
  3) Keep trailer coupling stable.
  4) Optionally provide alias resources for trailer-referenced paths in same
     package.
  5) Test load, then spawn, then visual correctness.

This is currently the highest-confidence path for iterative model experiments.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
15I. TEXTURE/MATERIAL REVERSE-ENGINEERING (CUBE TEST)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Problem observed:
  - Replacing material `m_ham` with a minimal 52-byte custom material caused
    spawn-time crash in the cube test path.

Root cause (current best):
  - Native ham-family materials in this path are not equivalent to the minimal
    one-slot material used for basic texture tests.
  - They use a larger material payload (observed size 460) with multiple
    embedded texture references and additional structured fields.

Decoded native material evidence (from extracted materials DB):
  - Material hashes tied to ham-family unit refs:
      * `0x8AEED77B10003A6C` (ham_plate-linked material entry)
      * `0xCF33DC549060E425` (ham-linked material entry)
  - Both entries share:
      * size = 460
      * shader_hash = `0xCF752A73093FC934`
      * texture_refs (6 entries), including known paths:
          - `0xDCA96153A4EE0CCF` -> `gameobjects/food/ham_diff`
          - `0x4D843D4F579DBAEF` -> `gameobjects/food/turkey_diff`
          - `0x2016FB7AFDF84214` -> `gameobjects/food/turkey_spec`
      * two additional unresolved texture hashes:
          - `0xBF3B3629FF21B3AF`
          - `0x5E2C73DD85E38712`

Safer texture override strategy (applied):
  - Do NOT replace `m_ham` material blob with simplified material format.
  - Keep native material behavior and override texture resources by path/hash
    that the native material already references.
  - Bundle now overrides:
      * `gameobjects/food/ham_diff`
      * `gameobjects/food/turkey_diff`
      * `gameobjects/food/turkey_spec`
    using custom DDS (`cube_checker.dds`).

Why this is safer:
  - Leaves material shader/layout/slot semantics intact.
  - Changes only texture payload data at known referenced hashes.
  - Avoids crash-sensitive material structure mismatches.

Open texture tasks:
  - Resolve unknown hashes `0xBF3B3629FF21B3AF` and `0x5E2C73DD85E38712`.
  - Confirm whether they are normal/roughness/emissive-type maps and whether
    overriding them is required for full visual control in this unit family.

15J. CUSTOM TEXTURE & MATERIAL LOADING RESEARCH (July 2, 2026)
================================================================================

Empirical investigation into whether custom DDS textures can be loaded
from custom resource packages. Seven controlled experiments conducted
to isolate all variables.

================================================================================
15J.1 EXPERIMENT RESULTS
================================================================================

| Test | Bundle Contents                                      | Result     |
|------|------------------------------------------------------|------------|
| 1    | Unit only (crane_from_template, native m_ham)        | ✅ Spawns  |
| 2    | Unit + homemade 64x64 DXT5 (no mipmaps, no NVTT)     | ❌ Hang    |
| 3    | Unit + native-cloned 128x128 DXT5 (8 mips, NVTT)     | ❌ Hang    |
| 4    | Unit + cloned material + cloned texture              | ❌ Hang    |
| 5    | Unit + cloned material only (no textures)            | ❌ Hang    |
| 6    | Texture-only bundle (native DDS, clean DB, 189 slots)| ❌ Hang    |
| 7    | Unit-only crane bundle (baseline retest)             | ✅ Spawns  |

Test 1/7 baseline:
  - crane_from_template.unit (1,137,588 bytes) spawns in-game
  - Uses native m_ham material applied to geometry
  - Log: "SPAWNED unit: [#ID[825f664e8e6c7eb8]]"
  - Requires: template-based unit with identity hash EB379016DC5FA5BA
    and coupling sentinel FFFFFFFF in trailer

Tests 2,3,6 (texture hang):
  - resource_package() C++ hook shows handle returned successfully
  - Engine stalls during splash loading phase — no crash, no Lua error
  - Texture need not be referenced by any unit/material to cause hang

Tests 4,5 (material hang):
  - Same symptoms as texture hang
  - 460-byte identity clone of m_ham with self-remapped texture hash
    still causes hang even with ZERO texture resources in bundle

================================================================================
15J.2 DISPROVEN HYPOTHESES (conclusively ruled out)
================================================================================

  [x] DDS format: Two correctly-formatted DDS files (homemade 64x64 and
      native-cloned 128x128) produce identical hangs.
  [x] DDS size: 4,224 bytes and 22,000 bytes both hang.
  [x] DDS mipmaps: 1 mip vs 8 mips both hang.
  [x] NVTT/DX10 header: DDS with and without both hang.
  [x] Material-texture dependency: Unreferenced texture still hangs.
  [x] Material cloning: clone_material.py verified byte-correct.
  [x] Materials alone: 460-byte identity clone still hangs.
  [x] Native DDS: Byte-for-byte unmodified native DDS still hangs.
  [x] DB structure: 189-entry fixed allocation + alpha sort — no effect.
  [x] Bundle preamble: Native preamble bytes — no effect.
  [x] Per-resource alignment: Fixed 4-byte padding (necessary but not
      sufficient to resolve hang).

================================================================================
15J.3 UNIT TRAILER — CONFIRMED WORKING
================================================================================

Identical across cube_from_template.unit and crane_from_template.unit:

  trailer-68  8  Identity hash (EB 37 90 16 DC 5F A5 BA)
  trailer-60  4  Coupling sentinel: FF FF FF FF
  trailer-56  8  Zeros
  trailer-48  4  Count field (04 00 00 00)
  trailer-44  8  Zeros
  trailer-36  8  Trailer data (6C 3A 00 10 7B D7 EE 8A)
  trailer-28  8  Zeros

Total trailer: 68 bytes after material name string. Scratch-built units
from gltf_to_unit.py have broken trailer coupling → segfault on spawn.

================================================================================
15J.4 MATERIAL CLONING — VERIFIED CORRECT
================================================================================

Native m_ham (8aeed77b10003a6c, 460 bytes):
  Shader: 0xCF752A73093FC934
  3 texture slots + payload hash 0xDCA96153A4EE0CCF (ham_diff) at offset 0x114
  clone_material.py --remap replaces exactly 1 hash, all other bytes preserved

================================================================================
15J.5 BUNDLE DATABASE — STRUCTURE DECODED
================================================================================

bundle_database.data (version 2):
  - 14,553 records = 77 bundles x 189 entries each
  - Records alphabetically sorted by bundle_stem
  - Each record: flags(4) + name_len(4) + bundle_stem(16) + stream_len(4) + stream_name + null
  - Database extractor: database_extractor/New/data_unbundle.py

================================================================================
15J.6 KEY CORRECTIONS (July 2, 2026)
================================================================================

1. FALSE POSITIVE STRUCK: Line 26 "Custom texture packages load
   successfully" was based on an earlier test (Jan 2026) that was not
   reproducible under controlled conditions. Unit-only is the only
   confirmed working custom bundle type.

2. mod_bundle_builder.py ALIGNMENT BUG FIXED: Package resource (33 bytes)
   caused 1-byte misalignment → corrupted res-1+ headers. Added per-resource
   4-byte padding in _build_bundle().

3. Native units use material strings (e.g. "m_ham") not resource path
   hashes. In native bundles materials share name hash with parent unit.
   Setting up matching hashes in custom bundles did NOT resolve hang.

================================================================================
15J.7 REMAINING HYPOTHESES
================================================================================

1. Package resource format: Native packages use different binary format
   than our 28-byte entry builder (native food_units package is 1228 bytes
   with 245 entries vs our 33 bytes with 1 entry).
2. Texture type hash confirmation needed for 0xCD4238C6A0C69E32.
3. Per-resource metadata (streaming flags, ThinHash) may be required.
4. VMF .mod path as alternative injection method.
5. In-place texture injection via replace_textures_v4.py.

================================================================================
16. ★ CUSTOM ASSETS SOLVED (July 5, 2026) — THE COMPLETE RECIPE ★
================================================================================

END STATE, CONFIRMED IN-GAME: a fully custom unit (crane, 18,841 verts) spawned
via `spawn_unit("units/custom/crane_test/crane")` and RENDERED with a custom
material bound to a custom novel-hash texture (magenta/cyan checker visible).
No native asset overridden. Every resource type — unit, texture, material —
passes real engine bring-in from runtime-loaded custom bundles.

THREE ROOT CAUSES, none of which was the novel hash:

  #1 TEXTURE — missing 4-BYTE TRAILER after the mip chain          (hang)
  #2 UNIT    — missing TRAILING SECTIONS + 32-byte footer          (native crash)
  #3 BINDING — unit binds material via FOOTER HASH, not the string (wrong look)

================================================================================
16.1 TEXTURE RESOURCE FORMAT (root cause #1)
================================================================================

A compiled texture resource (bundle data field, stream_size=0) is:

  [128-byte DDS header][full mip chain, top mip -> 1x1][4-BYTE TRAILER]

THE 4-BYTE TRAILER IS MANDATORY. Survey of all 23 food_units textures:
every single one has exactly +4 bytes after the computed mip chain
(values seen: 00000000, 4a07531f, be0fc23f — 00000000 works).
Without it, the loader worker never posts the opcode-1 completion message
-> resource never completes -> has_loaded stays false -> package never
onlines -> anything waiting on it hangs. THIS WAS "THE WALL" ALL ALONG.

DDS header must match native conventions:
  flags        0x000A1007  (CAPS|HEIGHT|WIDTH|PIXELFORMAT|MIPMAPCOUNT|LINEARSIZE)
  pitchLinear  top-mip linear size
  mips         full chain: floor(log2(max(w,h)))+1  (0 of 23 natives are 1-mip)
  pf_flags     0x4 (FOURCC), fourcc DXT1/DXT5/ATI2
  caps1        0x00401008  (COMPLEX|MIPMAP|TEXTURE)
  reserved1[60:76] NVTT signature: 'UVER' 00000000 'NVTT' 00010200 (v2.1.0)
               (native textures are NVTT-compiled; sig alone did NOT fix the
                hang — the trailer did — but include it to stay native-identical)

Hand-rolled DXT1/DXT5 encoders are ACCEPTED (our checker pixels render fine).

================================================================================
16.2 UNIT RESOURCE FORMAT (root causes #2 and #3)
================================================================================

A native .unit = [mesh section][trailing sections ~2KB][32-byte footer]:

  MESH SECTION (this part gltf_to_unit.py builds correctly):
    sig 0x00010090, mesh_count, vbuf, elements, ibuf, shapes, bbox,
    material_count x {u32 len, name string}
    -> the material STRINGS are SLOT LABELS ONLY (natives use e.g. "m_ham";
       murmur("m_ham") maps to NO resource — it is not the binding!)

  TRAILING SECTIONS (missing from scratch-built units = the crash):
    collision/physics actors ("a_collision"), FlowCallbacks data,
    sub-resource refs {u64 hash, FFFFFFFF sentinel}...
    A unit without these COMPLETES BRING-IN (it's a CPU copy) but the engine
    CRASHES NATIVELY at the later resolve/patch stage walking off the end.
    (This is why unit-only bundles "worked" in earlier tests: the crash
    path only runs when the package fully onlines/flushes.)

  FOOTER (last 32 bytes — root cause #3, the material binding):
    04 00 00 00 | 12 zero bytes | u64 MATERIAL RESOURCE NAME HASH | 8 zero bytes
    Patch unit[len-16:len-8] to bind any material. Every native unit ends in
    zeros; a unit ending mid-string is malformed.

WORKING BUILD PATH (proven):
  1. Extract a native unit as template (used: ham_plate 0x8aeed77b10003a6c,
     8,660 B, trailer 2,134 B, from food_units cd241d5192024482).
  2. glb_to_template_unit.py <template.unit> <mesh.glb> <out.unit> [scale] [label]
     -> splices custom geometry into the template, preserves trailer VERBATIM.
  3. Patch footer hash -> your material resource name hash.
  Template physics/collision/flow come along; pick a template whose material
  shader suits the mesh.

NATIVE NAMING CONVENTION: a unit and its material share the same resource
name (gameobjects/food/ham_plate names BOTH the unit and material resource).
Custom paths (units/custom/..., materials/...) work fine — nothing parses
the path; the footer hash is the only coupling.

================================================================================
16.3 MATERIAL RESOURCE (needed NO format fix)
================================================================================

Our 460-byte clone of ham_plate's material onlined UNMODIFIED — materials
have no trailer quirk. Format recap: 0a000000, u32 count, u64 shader id
(offset 8, e.g. 0xCF752A73093FC934 resolved via always-resident render_config),
texture slot hashes (this material: diffuse @276, normal @288, spec @300),
float params to the end.

Recipe: clone the TEMPLATE UNIT'S OWN material (guarantees shader/vertex
format compatibility), remap the diffuse slot hash -> custom texture name
hash. Normal/spec were left on native ham textures (all-3-slots remap was
never re-tested after the other fixes; single-slot is proven).

================================================================================
16.4 PACKAGE / BUNDLE / INSTALL (all verified working)
================================================================================

PACKAGE RESOURCE BLOB:
  u32 count + count x {u64 type_hash, u64 name_hash, u32 depcount=0} + 0x01
  All natives declare depcount=0 for materials/textures/units. A package
  onlines only when EVERY listed entry completes — keep test packages minimal.

BUNDLE BODY (replace_textures_v4.py parse_bundle/rebuild_simple_bundle/
compress_bundle = authoritative tooling):
  u32 count | 256-byte header region (zeros accepted) | TOC count x {type,name}
  | resources BACK-TO-BACK (no alignment padding), each:
    {u64 type, u64 name, u32 flags=1, u64 stream_size=0, u32 main_size, u32 unk=0}
    + main_size bytes
  Multi-chunk zlib: 64 KB uncompressed chunks, u32 comp_size + zlib each.

INSTALL (C++ dinput8 loader):
  mods/bundles/<Mod>/{<hash>, <hash>.stream (empty OK), bundle.ini, manifest.json}
  <hash> = murmur64A(package_path). bundle.ini ResourceCount and manifest.json
  resource_count/package_path MUST agree or the loader SKIPS the bundle.
  Loader copies to contents/<hash> at boot; restart required for bundle bytes.

TYPE HASHES (murmur64A of type string, seed 0 — verified):
  texture  0xCD4238C6A0C69E32   material 0xEAC0B497876ADEDF
  unit     0xE0A48D0BE9A7453F   package  0xAD9C6D9ED1E5E77A

================================================================================
16.5 LOADING RULES (verified)
================================================================================

- ALWAYS ASYNC: Game:load_package(path, false). flush=true busy-waits
  WaitForSingleObject(INFINITE) on worker completion -> any defect = permanent
  freeze with no log and no dump.
- LOAD SCREEN (StateLoadGame) pumps the streamer: hook on_enter to issue the
  load, poll in pre_update. Install state hooks from a require hook, and use
  rawget(_G,"StateLoadGame") — strict.lua ERRORS on undeclared global reads
  (crashed boot once).
- IN-GAMEPLAY async ALSO completes (Game.update pumps package_manager:update()
  every frame — the material bundle onlined mid-gameplay). The idle MAIN MENU
  does NOT pump. Never load big packages at the menu.
- Game:is_package_online(path): nil = never requested, false = loading,
  true = online. Spawn only when online.
- A failing GPU resource does not error — it HANGS the load pipeline. A
  malformed unit CRASHES natively with no dump. Treat hang-vs-crash as signal:
  hang = texture-class problem, silent crash = unit-class problem.

================================================================================
16.6 THE EXPERIMENT CHAIN THAT CRACKED IT (methodology)
================================================================================

Each test = one game launch; every hypothesis isolated ONE variable:

  1. 1-mip checker, novel hash ................ HANG   (baseline failure)
  2. Full 8-mip checker ....................... HANG   (mips NOT the fix)
  3. 8-mip + NVTT reserved signature .......... HANG   (sig NOT the fix)
  4. HAM'S EXACT BYTES under our novel hash ... ONLINE  << disproved the
     "novel hashes have no render slot" theory in one shot
  5. Ham header + OUR pixels (no trailer) ..... HANG   (pixels innocent)
  6. Ham header + our pixels + 4B TRAILER ..... ONLINE  << TEXTURE SOLVED
  7. Full 4-res bundle (old scratch unit) ..... NATIVE CRASH
  8. Sequenced bisect: A=pkg+unit+tex loads first, B=pkg+material only after
     A onlines. Crash BEFORE "A ONLINE" ....... UNIT is the crasher
  9. Template-spliced unit (ham trailer) ...... A=true, B=true, SPAWNS,
     renders with ham material (footer still ham_plate's)
 10. Footer hash patched to our material ...... CHECKER RENDERS. DONE.

THE GREAT CONFOUND (why this took months): overriding a NATIVE hash "works"
because the engine reuses the already-resident boot copy — custom bytes never
actually go through bring-in. So every byte-format bug stayed invisible until
a truly novel resource forced a real load. (Also explains why the magenta
ham_diff override "onlined but didn't change appearance".)

DEBUGGING PATTERN THAT WORKED: swap known-good native bytes under the novel
name to split name-vs-bytes, then binary-search the byte diff (header vs
pixels vs trailer). All three root causes were plain byte differences vs
native resources. Byte-diff EVERYTHING against natives first; disassemble
second.

================================================================================
16.7 ANSWERS TO 15J.7 "REMAINING HYPOTHESES"
================================================================================

1. Package resource format: our minimal blob (count + 20-byte entries +
   0x01) IS accepted — packages online. Native 1228-byte packages just list
   more entries. NOT the blocker.
2. Texture type hash 0xCD4238C6A0C69E32 CONFIRMED (murmur64A("texture"),
   validated against native TOCs and in-game loads).
3. Per-resource metadata: no extra streaming flags/ThinHash needed in the
   resource headers; the missing metadata was IN THE PAYLOADS (texture
   trailer, unit trailer/footer).
4. VMF .mod path: unnecessary — direct bundles work.
5. In-place replacement (replace_textures_v4.py): still valid for overrides,
   but no longer needed for custom content.

Also corrects 15J.6 #3: "matching hashes did not resolve hang" — correct
observation, wrong conclusion; naming was never the issue, payload bytes were.
And 15J.3/earlier trailer analysis: the "68-byte trailer" observed after the
material string is the tail of the trailing sections + the 32-byte footer;
scratch-built units lacking it crash exactly as predicted there.

================================================================================
17. RESOURCE / BUNDLE NAME RECOVERY (MurmurHash64A)
================================================================================

17.1 THE HASH
  - Every bundle filename, resource name and package path is
    MurmurHash64A(path, seed = 0) over the ASCII bytes of the LOWERCASE path
    (64-bit lanes, little-endian). Confirmed bit-for-bit against the game for
    bundle names, resolved resources and texture references.
  - Names carry NO extension: the build tool strips every trailing extension
    (foundation/scripts/common.lua clean_filepath():
    `filename,count = string_gsub(filename, "%.%w+$", "")` repeated until
    count == 0) and normalises `\` -> `/`.
  - The TOC only stores hashes, so a name is recovered either from a text
    source (Lua / ini / embedded binary strings) or by brute force.

17.2 NAME SOURCES - THE generate_*.lua SCRIPTS ARE THE MAP
  foundation/scripts/generate_*.lua run at build time to auto-generate name
  lists. Each one documents how a resource family is named:

  generate_shader_libraries.lua
      scans `<source>/**/*.shader_source`; name = clean_filepath(file, root);
      sorts the list; rewrites the block between `// <SHADER_LIBRARIES>` and
      `// </SHADER_LIBRARIES>` inside the *render_config* resource as
      `shader_libraries = { ... }`.
      => shader_library_group names are PATHS (`core/rendering/<group>`);
         leaf shader_library names are BARE basenames (`fxaa`, `terrain`,
         `debug_gbuffer`, `beckmann_distribution_lut`, `omni_light_debug_lights`).
      => group basename == the matching leaf library name for many entries
         (`core/rendering/fxaa` <-> `fxaa`).

  generate_elias.lua
      for every melody dir (`resource_packages/elias/<name>`) transcodes
      `<melody>/audio/*.wav` -> `.ogg` -> renamed `.ugg`, then writes
      `<melody>.package` (SJSON) listing every ugg path.
      => ugg resource name = `<melody_dir>/audio/<source-wav-name>` (the `.ugg`
         is stripped). The source .wav file names are NOT shipped and are not
         referenced anywhere in Lua, which is why ugg (144) stays unresolved.
      => confirms ugg = Ogg voice lines (`resource_packages/elias/*_adventure`,
         `*_labyrinth`, `*_arena`).

  Other generators (mine them as vocabulary, they are name tables):
      generate_animation_events_lookup.lua (animation_event names),
      generate_unit_paths / unit_lookup / unit_data_lookup /
      unit_path_to_components (unit paths),
      generate_short_file_paths_lookup / file_lookup / project_path,
      generate_level_package / resource_package / slayer_resource_packages,
      layout lookup (lua/dungeon/layouts/lookup.lua -> quilt/arena paths),
      settings/vo_triggers.lua (voice-over trigger list).

  The render_config resource itself (bundle 1a9c33dcd0301dfd, 27,412 B) embeds
  the full `shader_libraries` array AND the shading-graph node names as plain
  ASCII: a machine-readable vocabulary of render words.

17.3 A NEW LEVER - SCAN RESOURCE BODIES FOR EMBEDDED STRINGS
  scan_bundle_strings.py walks every bundle, parses the resource table, and
  harvests printable ASCII runs from every resource body (173,664 distinct
  plausible strings). Some bodies reference names in text.
  - 42 strings were themselves unsolved resource names, e.g.
      shader_library_group: core/rendering/{additive,base_dx11,base_voxelizer,
        canvas,color_grading,debug_gbuffer,distort_access,fog,
        framebuffer_distort,fxaa,light_bake,mixer,multilayer,player_marker,
        terrain}
      shader_library: fxaa, color_grading, linearize_depth, global_lighting,
        ao_cone_trace, shadow_cutter, omni_light(_debug_lights),
        gbuffer_debug, downscale_depth, ssao_upscale, dof_near_coc,
        beckmann_distribution_lut, terrain_undergrowth, terrain_editor_brush,
        framebuffer_distort, debug_render_voxels, debug_gbuffer, terrain
      texture: core/rendering/nft, core/rendering/vo_rotation
      unit:    blocks/d01_crypt/walls/crypt_wall_door_01_4c
      material: characters/boss_mummy/boss_mummy_hole
  - The dump also doubles as a ~109 k-word candidate/vocabulary list, which is
    what finally pushed shader_library_group over the line (the group list in
    render_config plus the basename rule).

17.4 TOOLCHAIN - Scripting/hashcracker (Rust)
  Multithreaded MurmurHash64A brute-forcer (rayon, offline cargo build,
  -C target-cpu=native), ~200-260 M candidates/s on 12 cores.
    prepare.py    -> data/{targets,dirs,names,prefixes,suffixes}.txt
    crack         -> template expansion; `%X` slots bound with --list X=FILE.
                     IMPORTANT: a slot letter is LOCKED, so `%a_%a` repeats one
                     word; use `%a_%b` for a cross product.
    --candidates  -> test a plain candidate list (used for the string dump)
    --matches F   -> append-only, deduped TSV `<hash-hex><TAB><name>` (streamed)
    --exclude F   -> skip solved hashes
    mutate        -> byte-level mutation to fixpoint (~10x faster than v1)
    mine          -> aho-corasick dictionary pruning
  Results feed Scripting/GauntletGameDigging/resource_name_overlay.json via
  merge_matches.py; list_game_bundles.py then regenerates bundle_manifest/*.

17.5 COVERAGE (resource-name overlay)
  1,509 names recovered. Unresolved: ~12,258 occurrences / ~4,470 unique hashes.
    texture 1492/4771   material 1872/4050   particles 632/2476
    animation 7/2276    unit 1458/1828       shader_library 104/1538
    level 379/719       shader 42/237        flow 21/159   ugg 0/144
  (lua 952/952 and bones 367/367 are 100 %; apb 98/98, state_machine 107/111.)

17.6 WHY THE REMAINDER RESISTS
  Every family we have checked derives its name from a SOURCE FILE that is not
  shipped and is not referenced in any readable text:
    - shader_library (leaf): the `.shader_source` filenames.
    - ugg: the audio `.wav` filenames.
    - animation (1,243 unique): the clip names come from the binary `.unit` /
      animation blobs; the 217 `animation = "x"` Lua clip names give 0 hits in
      every shape tried (`<dir>/<clip>`, `animations/<clip>`, bare `<clip>`).
    - texture / material: names live in binary material/level payloads; the
      material->texture graph, global dir x token x suffix sweeps and mutation
      to fixpoint are exhausted.
  Brute force only helps where a name is a *recombination* of words that DO
  appear in text (which is what cracked the shader groups, quilts, levels,
  equipment, decals, map suffixes, etc.).

17.7 NEXT
  - Per-unit clip tables (parse the .unit payload) for the animation family.
  - `.shader_source` name inference from technique/pass names embedded in the
    DXBC blobs (today only uniform names survive as text).
  - Fold the harvested word lists back through `mutate` after every new source.

================================================================================
17.8 TIER-1 RETEST PASS (extension semantics, binaries, graph, mutation)
================================================================================
  Re-ran the whole pipeline against the now-1,900-name overlay and fixed three
  blind spots in our own tooling.

  (a) clean_filepath() re-test  -> +2.
      The bundle-body string dump (scan_bundle_strings.py) had only ever tested
      strings VERBATIM. The engine strips every trailing extension before
      hashing, so any `.../foo.norm.dds`-style string could never match. Re-ran
      the dump through clean_filepath semantics (strip `.ext` repeatedly, `\`->`/`,
      drop a leading dir component): 174,722 variants -> recovered
      `characters/boss_mummy/boss_mummy_hole` and
      `blocks/d01_crypt/walls/crypt_wall_door_01_4c`, which the original scan had
      PRINTED as hits but never written to matches.tsv. New tool:
      scan_binaries.py (binaries), plus the variant list is now fed through
      `crack --candidates` so hits are always recorded.

  (b) game binaries -> 0.
      prepare.py only kept the LAST `/`-segment of a path-like string from
      gauntlet.exe/*.dll. scan_binaries.py keeps whole strings and expands them
      (ext-strip, every known root prefix): 855,744 candidates from 13 binaries
      -> 0 hits. The exe/DLLs carry IDENTIFIERS (`render_config`,
      `shader_library`, `elias_theme_*`), not resource paths. elias.dll (the
      dialogue system) contains no .wav/.ugg names either.
      .stream files are simply `<bundle-hash>.stream` -> no name material.

  (c) material -> texture graph -> +1.
      derive_textures.py re-run with the current overlay: only 1 new name. The
      reason is structural, not a bug: materials_database.json holds just 1,657
      decoded material records (a subset), of which 1,099 now have known names;
      most unknown materials reference textures that are themselves unknown, so
      the graph has no anchor to walk from.

  (d) mutate to fixpoint -> +106.
      84 + 18 + 4, then 0. Mutation is the single best value-per-second tool we
      have: it found e.g. `props/d01_common/fog_volume_swamp_01` and, notably, a
      NEW quilt field pattern `ox_ex_ix_ex` that the hand-written quilt grammar
      enumeration had missed. Lesson: prefer mutate over hand-built grammars.

  Net: 1,877 -> 1,986 names (+109).

================================================================================
17.9 ANIMATION — SOLVED THE STRUCTURE, NOT THE NAMES
================================================================================
  The single biggest remaining pool (1,243 unresolved) now has a real handle.

  DISCOVERY: every unit ships a `state_machine` sub-resource
  (type murmur64("state_machine") = 0xA486D4045106165C) that shares the unit's
  name. Its body contains the 8-byte little-endian Murmur hashes of every
  animation it plays (records look like [u32 flag][u64 hash]..., unaligned).
  Proof: the body of `characters/necromancer/necromancer` contains both
  `characters/necromancer/spawn_special_dormant` and
  `characters/necromancer/spawn_special_execute`.

  EXTRACTION (extract_anim_refs.py): 111 state machines, 51 unit dirs,
  1,246 animation hashes referenced — i.e. ALL 1,243 unresolved animations are
  accounted for, each attributed to the unit that owns it (e.g. skeleton_warrior
  44, mummy_risen 34, boss_mummy 31). Writes anim_dirs.txt / anim_targets.txt /
  anim_map.json, turning a hopeless global search into 51 tiny per-unit ones.

  WHY IT STILL DID NOT CRACK: the clip is the last path segment of
  `<unit_dir>/<clip>`, but that leaf is not present in ANY shipped text:
      <unit_dir>/<108k-token vocab> ........................ 0
      <unit_dir>/<2 tokens from that vocab> (11.9e9) ....... 0
      <unit_dir>/<3 tokens> ................................ 0
      <unit_dir>/<3 token vocab> + <token>_<number> ........ 0
      <any known dir>/<217 lua `animation=` clips> ......... 0
      <any dir under any lua path>/<217 clips> ............. 0
      <unit_dir>/<482 animation_events> .................... 0
  Only 3 animation names are known at all (all `<unit_dir>/<clip>`), so the
  namespace is barely sampled. e.g. necromancer.lua declares the logical clip
  `death_default`, but no `<dir>/death_default` resource exists — the lua name
  is a LOGICAL alias resolved through the (binary) animation set, and the real
  resource leaf comes from a source animation file name that is not shipped.

  NEXT for animations: we now know unit->animation-hash sets, so any future clip
  vocabulary (a bigger token corpus, a DCC export, a leaked asset list) can be
  tested with one `%d/%clip` sweep and would land ~1,200 names at once.

================================================================================
17.10 foundation/scripts/ — THE AUTHORITATIVE SOURCE-TYPE MAP
================================================================================
  Every resource family is produced by a build script that scans a SOURCE file
  extension and writes the resource name as `clean_filepath(file, root)` (path
  minus every trailing extension, `\`->`/`). Absolutes are built as
  `root .. path .. "." .. type` (dependency_resource.lua), which is why the
  hash never sees an extension.

  Complete list of source extensions referenced by foundation/scripts:
      .unit        units            (generate_unit_paths / unit_lookup / unit_data_lookup)
      .level       levels           (generate_level_lookup / level_info / level_package)
      .lua         lua + units      (dependency_parser)
      .flow        flows            (dependency_parser)
      .texture     textures         (texture_sanity_checks)
      .material    materials        (validate_materials_texture_resources)
      .state_machine  state machines + animation EVENTS
                                      (generate_animation_events_lookup)
      .physics     physics          (unit_sanity_checks, nav_grid)
      .level_editor / .bsi / .package / .override_settings
      .shader_source  shader libraries (generate_shader_libraries)
      .wav -> .ugg  elias voice lines  (generate_elias)
      .shading_environment

  **THERE IS NO ANIMATION SOURCE EXTENSION.** The clip names live inside the
  per-unit `.state_machine` (and `.unit`) SJSON sources:
  generate_animation_events_lookup.lua reads `sjson.events` out of
  `*.state_machine` files. Those sources are NOT shipped (only `.lua` is in
  Gauntlet-Source-DO_NOT_EDIT), which is the definitive reason the animation
  leaf names cannot be recovered from any shipped text — it confirms §17.9
  empirically rather than by elimination.

  Bonus vocabulary extracted here:
      - validate_materials_texture_resources.lua holds the engine's 20 material
        sampler/slot names (diffuse_map, normal_map, material_map, specular_map,
        emissive_map, ambient_occlusion_map, subsurface_scattering_map,
        detail_*, blend_*, outline_map, projection_map, ..., self_illum_map).
        Turned into 146 extra texture-suffix candidates -> +4 names.
      - nav-grid types (walkable, blocker, floor_border, sandpile, ...) and
        generate_surface_effects/tracer_effects packages (which just collect
        `effect.particle` strings already present in lua/settings).

================================================================================
17.11 THE LOCALIZATION BUNDLE — 6 LANGUAGES OF GAME TEXT AS A DICTIONARY
================================================================================
  `resource_packages/localization` (bundle dd5d5b00fde9b0fb) is a single banked
  resource, 2.35 MB, holding the whole game's text:

      0x010000  English          ("The password must contain at least one digit",
                                  "Bucket of the Dragon Rider", ...)
      0x040000  Polish
      0x0b0000  Portuguese
      0x0e0000  German
      0x110000  French
      0x140000  Spanish

  WHY IT WAS MISSED: scan_bundle_strings.py DID pass over it (53,784 strings),
  but its plausible-name filter rejects anything with a space, so prose never
  became a candidate and words were never split out.

  Tool: mine_localization.py
      * 25,150 English words        -> data/vocab/loc_words.txt
      * 25,810 phrase forms         -> data/vocab/loc_phrases.txt
        (lowercased, spaces -> "_"  e.g. "Rude Interruption" ->
         rude_interruption)
      * 1,019,079 prefixed variants -> data/vocab/loc_candidates.txt
        (each phrase x the icon/menu/hud/effects/... roots, plus _icon/_locked/
         _01/_02)

  YIELD: +12 names, all first-try via `crack --candidates`, e.g.
      achievement_icons/{secrets_of_the_flame, mysteries_in_the_dark,
        rude_interruption, we_need_to_go_deeper, blessed_by_the_gods,
        riddles_of_the_grave, faithful_customer, generous, unruly, volatile,
        heedless}
      effects/gold_collected
  i.e. the localised ACHIEVEMENT names are exactly the icon resource leaves
  (11 of the 12), including the four hidden achievements (volatile, unruly,
  heedless, generous) that no other source knows.

  The word list was folded into the composition dictionary (big2_dict.txt =
  big_dict + loc_words + loc_phrases = 173,483 entries) and re-run over the
  global sweeps (`%a_%b`, dir x word, effects/menu/hud/... x word): no further
  hits, so the value here is phrase->leaf for UI art, not new stem words.
