17.12 FLOW FAMILY — 145/159 SOLVED, 2 LEAVES LEFT
================================================================================
  flow went 35 -> 115 (big run) -> 145 with the status list.
  Resolved: flow/gib, flow/dropped_weapon, flow/weapon_{axe,spear,book} and
  flow/status_effect_{burning,charmed,chilled,ethereal,frozen,haste,invincible,
  invisible,rooted,poisoned,slowed}.
  Source: lua/status_effects/status_effects.lua assigns each status as
  `StatusEffects.<name> = <Name>` -> the exact vocabulary (17 statuses).

  The last 2 have resisted everything:
      flow/%a                          (173k dict)              0
      flow/%a_%b                       (173k^2 = 30.1e9)        0
      flow/status_effect_%a(_%b)       (all 17 statuses + words) 0
      flow/<3 pieces: big2 x small^2>  (43e9)                   0
      curated semantic sets (tint/glow/material/scalar/relic/...) 0
  Their bodies show what they do, not what they are called:
      <unresolved A> = FlowCallbacks { tint_material, color_rgb, unit }
          (identical to flow/status_effect_slowed -> it IS another status tint)
      <unresolved B> = FlowCallbacks { material_set_scalar_over_time,
          variable = self_illumination_multiplier, start_value, stop_value }
  So the names contain a word that appears in NO shipped text (lua, bundle
  bodies, localization, exe/dll) — same class of blocker as animations.

================================================================================
17.13 AUDIO COMPLETELY SOLVED (ugg 144/144, xml themes 13/13)
================================================================================
  THE MISS THAT HID IT: scan_bundle_strings.py filtered candidates with
  `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_./-]{3,110}$`, so every string starting with a quote was
  dropped.  SJSON/XML files store paths QUOTED, so all of them were discarded —
  bundle_strings.txt contains literally zero strings containing a `"`.
  Re-scanning for `"([a-zA-Z0-9_][a-zA-Z0-9_/.-]{3,120})"` immediately exposed
  the music data.

  WHAT THE XML REVEALED: `elias_theme` resources are XML music definitions:

      <theme mode="objective" tempo="150" bars="75" ...>
        <audio_loop_track name="Loop Track 1" id="1">
          <key name="C"><trigger value="1" filename="audio/1_0_1.wav"/></key>
        </audio_loop_track>
        <audio_stinger_track name="Game Over" id="2">
          <key name="C"><stinger value="1" filename="audio/2_0_1.wav"/></key>
        </audio_stinger_track>

  i.e. the elias audio files are named with NUMERIC tuplets (`1_0_1`) or words
  (`gameover`, `stinger`, `completed`, `intro`).

  THE SCHEME (all three families):

      ugg            <melody>/audio/<track>          e.g.
                     elias/cave_adventure/audio/1_0_1
                     elias/cave_boss/audio/completed
                     elias/cave_boss/audio/cave_boss      (track == melody)
      xml (theme)    <melody>/<melody>               e.g. elias/hubroom/hubroom
      timpani_bank   audio/<bank>                    e.g. audio/enemy_gator,
                     audio/heroes_wizard, audio/music
      melody         one of the 13 `resource_packages/elias/<melody>` bundles:
                     {cave,crypt,lava}_{adventure,arena,boss,labyrinth}+hubroom

  RESULT: ugg 144/144 and the 13 elias themes 13/13.  Candidate generation was
  pure enumeration (numeric tuplets 1-4 parts + word tracks + the melody name),
  tested with `crack --candidates` — no guessing left once the shape was known.

  Still open in audio: 37 timpani_bank (`audio/<enemy|heroes|x>_<something>`;
  audio/enemy_gator was the one easy find).

================================================================================
17.14 ANIMATION SOLVED — <unit_dir>/animations/<clip>
================================================================================
  After audio fell, the same trick broke animations: enumerate the CLIP slot per
  owning unit directory instead of guessing whole global paths.

      characters/<unit>/animations/<clip>       (plural dir, most units)
      characters/<unit>/animation/<clip>        (singular dir - both exist!)

      e.g. characters/avatar_elf/animations/carry_dropoff
           characters/avatar_warrior/animations/leap_jump
           characters/avatar_necromagus/animation/move_walk
           characters/skeleton_warrior/animation/stagger_right_01

  HOW THE UNIT LIST WAS BUILT: extract_anim_refs.py already gave, for every
  unit, the exact set of animation hashes its state_machine plays (all 1,243
  unresolved animations are attributed to 51 unit dirs). The clip slot is then
  just a vocabulary sweep restricted to `%d/animations/<...>`.

  SWEEPS THAT PAID (big2_dict = 173k words; small = 500; numbers):
      %d/animations|animation/%a            (1 slot)        +330
      %d/animations|animation/%a_%b and %b_%a (big2 x med)  +118
      %d/animations|animation/%a_%n         (word + number) +52
      %d/animations|animation/%a_%b_%c      (small^3)       +3
  = animation 1,242 unresolved -> 595, overlay 2,402 -> 3,049.

  The clip vocabulary is ordinary game words (carry_, die_, knockback_,
  revive_, block_, offset_, stagger_, move_, dash_, conjuring_, eye_),
  which is why the earlier global attempts failed: `<unit_dir>/<clip>` with a
  single path segment was never the shape - there is always the extra
  `animations/` component, and its singular/plural spelling varies by unit.

  REMAINING: 595 clips, presumably longer compounds (4+ tokens) or per-unit
  verbs outside the vocabularies; the parent-dir list is only 42, so the unit
  coverage is the next thing to widen (extract state_machine refs for units
  whose state machine wasn't scanned).






================================================================================
17.15 THE LUA IS THE NAMING AUTHORITY (and why the sweeps looked slow)
================================================================================
  THE AUDIT THAT CHANGED THE METHOD: "unresolved" had been measured against
  resource_name_overlay.json, but list_game_bundles.py resolves the catalogue's
  `name` column from hash_database.json (142,060 entries) PLUS both overlays.
  Regenerating the catalogue with the pre-session overlay reproduces 7,289
  unnamed rows; with the session's overlay, 7,259.  Of the 1,628 hash->name
  pairs added this session, 1,602 were already in hash_database.json with the
  identical string, so only 26 pairs (~30 catalogue rows) were new to the
  catalogue.  The overlay-based target lists had been overstating the unknown
  set by thousands of entries - which is why sweeps "found nothing" while
  burning tera-candidates.

  => Targets must be *rows whose catalogue name column is empty*
     (hashcracker/build_targets.py), never "hash absent from the overlay".

  WHAT THE LUA GAVE UP (millions of candidates, seconds each):

    lua            every `*.lua` path minus the extension == the resource name.
                   927/927 rows.  (foundation/lua/component/component_manager)
    material 399   Lua string literals, referenced verbatim
    unit     326   (props/d01_caves/caves_cobweb_floor_01, lights/light,
                    modules/d01_crypt/resources, decals/footstep_sand,
                    resource_packages/d01_caves)
    package   21
    level/texture/shading_environment/mouse_cursor  12
    animation  13   edit-distance-1 from the unit's OWN clips
                    (dash_backward, move_backward, ability_floor_bash_{init,exit},
                     ability_spawner_buff_{enter,exit}) + cross-unit transfer
                    (dormant_underground x3) + ability_selection keys
                    (ability_lay_eggs, fireball, idle)

  THE ANIMATION SHAPE, CORRECTED: clips are up to 4-5 tokens
  (axe_move_walk_left_back).  The earlier generators stopped at 3 tokens and the
  `mutate` pattern REPLACES tokens but never INSERTS one, so every
  `<a>_<b>_<c>_<d>` clip and every `x_<direction>` insertion was unreachable.
  Edit-distance-1 (insert/replace/delete) over each unit's own clip set plus
  cross-unit transfer covers them for ~3M candidates per pass.

  WHERE THE 2,589 REMAINING UNIQUE UNNAMED HASHES LIVE (catalogue, 2026-10-05):
      texture 732 | shader_library 538 | animation 415 | material 383 |
      particles 335 | level 233 | unit 219 | shader 49 | timpani_bank 37
  None of these are file-path shaped any more, so each needs its own generator:
  `effects/<unit>_<verb>` for particles, `<dir>/<leaf>{,_locked,_norm,...}` for
  textures, flat identifiers for shader_library (mine the shader bundles).

  ARTEFACTS: bundle_manifest/03_bundle_resources.txt and
  04_bundle_resources.csv regenerated with every name found so far
  (12,741 / 20,000 rows named).


================================================================================
17.16 LEVELS - THE QUILT SCHEMA FROM quilt_builder.lua (+56)
================================================================================
  lua/dungeon/quilt_builder.lua decodes the level names:

      name:match("^%w+_quilt_(%w+)__([^_]+)_([^_]+)_([^_]+)_([^_]+)__(.*)$")
      local w = #u      -- quilt width  in cells
      local h = #r      -- quilt height in cells

  So a quilt name is <env>_quilt_<shape>__<up>_<right>_<down>_<left>__<variant>,
  the four groups being the cell rows of the four SIDES of a quilt piece, each
  cell one of  x (wall), e (through), i (entrance), o (exit).  The rule
  len(up) == len(down) == w and len(right) == len(left) == h is what makes the
  candidate space finite; observed side lengths are 1, 2, 3 and 5 (never 4).
  Variants are NN, NN_<mod>, or start_NN (crypt entrances).

  WHY THE EARLIER SWEEPS FOUND NOTHING: the vocabulary had been built from the
  side groups that appear in RESOLVED names.  Several 1-cell sides ("o", "i")
  never appear in any resolved piece, so codes such as o_e_i_e were unreachable.
  Enumerating the alphabet under the width/height rule found them immediately:

      crypt_quilt_smallroom__o_e_i_e__01
      crypt_quilt_smallroom__o_e_i_e__01_skeleton
      caves_quilt_smallroom__o_e_i_e__01

  DEAD ENDS (checked, so nobody repeats them):
    * level bodies are compiled modules - level_body_strings.py finds no
      embedded names, only binary tables
    * the module package blobs (resource_packages/boot) list dependency hashes
      only; they are hash-sorted, not name-sorted (pkg_order_check.py), so an
      unresolved hash is NOT bracketed by its alphabetical neighbours
    * the dungeon layout Lua enumerates only the pieces actually used - all of
      those were already resolved, so the stragglers are unreferenced content

  METHODS THAT PAID: edit-distance-1 from a piece's own clips (+28), the
  observed-shape x observed-edge cross product (+25), then the schema-correct
  alphabet (+3 and counting).  Level family: 233 -> 177 unique unresolved.

================================================================================
17.17 CORRIDORS / HALLWAYS - THE BLOCK PIECES (+30 units)
================================================================================
  The dungeon generator (lua/dungeon/corridor_segment.lua,
  corridor_collection.lua) is pure geometry - corridors are QUILT pieces, and
  the level layouts group them as straight_vert_long / straight_horiz_short /
  turn_* (those are already covered by the quilt sweep).

  The art side of corridors/hallways is the `unit` family: block pieces stored
  in the same module bundles as the levels.  Their habit:

      <root>/d01_<env>/<category>/<style>_<part>[_<orient>][_<width>c][_<var>]

      blocks/d01_crypt/walls/crypt_wall_4c_var01
      blocks/d01_crypt/walls/cryptsmooth_wall_door_01_4c
      blocks/d01_lava/floors/lavastruct_floor_drain_turn_left_lavaflow
      blocks/d01_caves/rocks/caverock_stalagmites_01
      blocks/d01_crypt/borders/crypt_border_straight_2c
      blocks/d01_lava/pillars/lavastruct_column_01
      blocks/d01_caves/stairs/cavestruct_stairs_4c

  lua/generated/unit_paths.lua is the *script-referenced* unit list (239
  entries) and is already exhausted; the stragglers are unreferenced art, so
  the habit is the only lever.  Two generators paid:

    targeted_unit.py   edit-distance-1 inside a unit's own directory +
                       cross-dir re-pointing, using a token vocabulary from the
                       whole family (+6)
    make_unit_vocab.py the <root>/d01_<env>/<cat>/<style>_<part>[_<orient>]
                       [_<width>c][_<var>] cross product driven through the GPU
                       pattern engine (+23 then +4)

  Recovered pieces include walls (caverock_wall_2c/4c, lavarock_wall_2c/4c,
  lavastruct_wall_2c/4c), stairs (cavestruct_stairs_2c/4c, stairs_2c/4c,
  lavastruct_stairs_2c/4c), pillars (crypt_pillar_01, cryptsmooth_pillar_01,
  cavestruct_pillar_01, lavastruct_pillar_01, lavastruct_column_01), borders
  (crypt_border_corner, crypt_border_straight_2c/4c, lavastruct_border_2c/4c),
  rocks (crypt_rock_01, lavarock_rock_01/02), doorarch/pillars/walls, and the
  passage floors lavastruct_floor_drain_{end,straight_2c,straight_4c}.

  unit family: 219 -> 189 unique unresolved.

================================================================================
17.18 UNITS UNLOCK TEXTURES - THE COMPOUNDING RATIO (8 units -> 16 textures)
================================================================================
  Two namespaces, one leaf vocabulary:

      unit      blocks/d01_caves/rocks/caverock_rock_01
      texture   blocks/d01_caves/caverock_rock_01_diff
      texture   blocks/d01_caves/caverock_rock_01_norm
      material  blocks/d01_caves/caverock_set
      material  props/d01_common/blackbox_2c_2c

  So textures/materials are `<root>/<env>/<leaf><suffix>` while units are
  `<root>/<env>/<cat>/<leaf>` - recovering a unit immediately yields its
  _diff/_norm (+_spec/_mask/_high/_illum where they exist) texture candidates.

  MEASURED: the last push found 8 units and exactly 16 textures - the
  _diff/_norm pair of every recovered block (crypt_floor_01/02,
  cryptsmooth_floor_01, crypt_border_01, ...).

  WHAT PAID THIS ROUND (+24, overlay 5044 -> 5068):
    * targeted_unit.py            edit-distance-1 inside a unit's own dir (+7)
    * targeted_block_texture.py   unit leaves x block dirs x map suffixes (+8)
    * make_tex_vocab.py           `%d/%l%s` dirs x leaves x suffixes on the GPU
                                  (366M cands in ~1s) (+8)
    * unit grammar (166 BILLION candidates through the GPU pattern engine) (+1)

  LESSON: the *grammar* sweep is exhausted (166e9 cands -> 1 hit) while the
  *derivation* from already-recovered names is not (19.5M cands -> 8 hits).
  Prefer narrow, name-derived generators; keep the huge pattern sweeps for
  cases where the schema itself is the unknown (as with quilts).


  NEGATIVE RESULTS (checked, do not retry):
    * unit leaves and material leaves never coincide inside the same directory
      (0/636), so materials do NOT predict missing units
    * the REVERSE derivation (texture leaves pushed back through the block
      unit directories, `--reverse`) found 0 - the two namespaces share leaves
      only in the forward direction (unit -> its own map textures)

  Unit family: 219 -> 181 unique unresolved.  overlay 5068, 2476 unique left.

17.20 THE PLATEAU AUDIT - THREE AVENUES TRIED, ALL EMPTY
================================================================================
  All three leads from the last report were chased to the end.  None paid, and
  the reasons are structural (recording so they are not repeated).

  1. SHADER_LIBRARY (538, all in core/rendering/renderer [1a9c33dcd0301dfd])
     * the bodies are compiled DXBC blobs ("Microsoft (R) HLSL Shader Compiler
       9.27.952.3022"); they contain shader variables (world, view_proj,
       sun_direction), not the library's own resource name
     * ONE body (index 113, 27412B) is the rendering-settings manifest and does
       list shader paths (core/rendering/base, .../color_grading, .../noise,
       51 of them) - but every one of those is already resolved
     * scanning all 75 bundles' bodies for embedded paths: 4,597 path strings,
       0 hits on any unresolved target
     * platform permutations of the known 51 leaves x 40 platform words, both
       orders, 142,537 candidates (base_mobile vs mobile_blend both exist): 0
     * bundle_strings.txt / bin_strings.txt: 0 matches
     CONLUSION: the unresolved 538 are unreferenced platform/debug library
     variants whose names exist only in the (unshipped) engine shader source.

  2. MENU UI TEXTURES (~527 unresolved rows in the menu bundles)
     * the UI style sheets DO name textures by leaf:
         gui/friendslist_css.lua -> bg_img = "button_friendslist"
         (= the resolved resource menu/buttons/button_friendslist)
       so all image-ish properties + every quoted identifier from gui,
       lua/menu, lua/states, lua/managers (2,401 values) were pushed at 19 UI
       directories x 20 state suffixes: 910,727 candidates -> 0
     CONLUSION: shipped UI is already named; the rest is unreferenced/shelf UI.

  3. ANIMATION (415)
     * attribution is complete - all 415 have an owning unit via the state
       machines (0 unattributed)
     * widening the edit-distance-1 vocabulary to 400 tokens x 200 heads gave
       4,170,270 candidates -> 0
     * the 41 owning units' own clips were already used as the token source

  ALSO EMPTY THIS ROUND:
     * every string from every bundle body (349,136 strings, loose filter) -> 0
     * animation_events/flow_events x clip dirs -> 0 (already recorded)

  STATE: overlay 5,087 / 2,457 unique unresolved.  Catalogue 20,000 rows,
  6,930 unresolved.  Every family now needs a *new data source* rather than a
  new generator: the shipped Lua / bundles / binaries contain no further name
  material for texture, shader_library, animation, particles or the module
  units.

                     apb 98/98, font 7/7, mouse_cursor 4/4, gley 1/1

  WHERE NAMES COME FROM (the naming authorities, in order of usefulness)
    1. Lua FILE PATHS minus .lua  ==  the `lua` resource family (exact)
    2. Lua STRING LITERALS       -> unit / material / prop / decal / level names
       (they are referenced verbatim; dirs come from resolved names)
    3. Lua BARE TABLE KEYS       -> ability names, quilt pieces, perk/achievement
       ids, gui widget ids (invisible to string mining)
    4. ENGINE SCHEMAS IN THE LUA -> the quilt name grammar (quilt_builder.lua),
       the block-piece grammar, the particle grammar
    5. RESOLVED-NAME COMPOUNDING -> units imply their map textures
       (8 units => 16 textures), particles <-> materials share strings

  HOW TO CONTINUE (mechanics)
    python3 ../hashcracker/tools/merge_matches.py ..      # hits -> overlay
    python3 list_game_bundles.py --only resources         # overlay -> txt+csv
    python3 ../hashcracker/tools/build_targets.py         # catalogue -> targets
    ../hashcracker/sweeps/auto_run.sh 3                   # cycle all families
    See ../hashcracker/SCRIPTS.md for the full script index.
    TARGETS COME FROM THE CATALOGUE'S BLANK `name` COLUMN - measuring
    "unresolved" against the overlay alone overstates the set by thousands and
    silently wastes sweeps (section 17.15).

19. PRE-SLAYER DEPOT - 549 MORE NAMES, AND WHY WE MISSED THEM (+22%)
================================================================================
  WHAT IT IS: Gauntlet-PreSlayer-2015-01-21/ is a Steam depot snapshot of the
  older (pre-Slayer) build (manifest 2950106636512614349, SteamDB seen date
  2015-01-21) - depot_258971/binaries (older gauntlet.exe 3.5MB + 10
  DLLs) and depot_258972/contents (55 bundles + .stream + bundle_database.data
  + settings.ini).  50 of the 55 bundle IDs are shared with the Slayer install.

  FORMAT: identical header/TOC (0xF0000004) but the older module bundles MIX
  RAW 64 KiB CHUNKS with zlib chunks.  Fix (now in list_game_bundles.py): if a
  chunk does not start with a zlib magic byte, use it raw.  Renderer bundles
  are all-zlib in both builds, which is why only the big art bundles looked
  broken.

  CONTENT DIFFS (same bundle ID, different contents):
      d01_crypt 1284 -> 1629 (shared 1188, -96, +441)
      d01_caves  939 -> 1341 (shared  845, -94, +496)
      d01_lava   949 -> 1290 (shared  877, -72, +413)
      modules/menu 286 -> 592 (shared 212, -74, +380)
      pkg/menu  1044 -> 1058 (shared  732, -312, +326)
      all_effects 1468 -> 1963 (shared 1329, -139, +634)
  Depot-wide: 6224 distinct resource hashes; 5348 still ship, 876 were removed;
  1654 of our unresolved targets exist in both builds.

  THE DISCOVERY: the OLD build keeps ~1.8M name-bearing strings (Lua, config,
  editor references); the NEW build STRIPPED them - the same scan over the
  Slayer bundles returns ZERO.  And many of those names contain a COLON
  (shader/material permutation syntax), which every earlier corpus filter
  dropped: `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_./-]{3,110}$` has no `:`.  Two bugs in one.

  RESULT - 549 previously-unreachable names, recovered in ~25 s:
      shader_library 145 | particles 120 | texture 117 | unit 77 | level 58 |
      animation 26 | material 2 | render_config 2 | flow 1 | package 1
  Examples:

      exactly.

      one per type straight out of the installed bundles: type+index+hash all
      match).

================================================================================
20. THE DEPOT'S .stream FILES - CHECKED PROPERLY, NO NAMES (negative result)
================================================================================
  The .stream companions were the last un-scanned part of the depot (9 files in
  Pre-Slayer, 11 in Slayer, 1-36 MB each).  They are RAW BULK DATA (textured/
  mesh payload), not another name source:

  * no header/TOC: the file starts with high-entropy bytes; the Pre-Slayer and
    Slayer copies of the SAME stream even begin byte-identical (same content)
  * the ~2,400 `78 9c` byte pairs per depot are COINCIDENTAL, not zlib streams
    (chance rate for 150 MB is ~2,300).  Inflating at every candidate offset
    yields 0 blobs, so there is no compression layer to unwind
  * only ~0.7% of the bytes are printable; the min-3/colon-tolerant token
    harvest gives 950,733 unique strings (7.6 M with suffix/extension variants)
    and the Rust/GPU cracker reports 0 matches against the remaining targets
  * no UTF-16 strings either
  * every stream has a companion MAIN bundle, and all of those are already in
    the catalogue (Slayer 75/75, Pre-Slayer 49/52) - so their TOC and inline
    bodies were parsed long ago
  * the 3 Pre-Slayer-only main files are covered too: `429c1a93aa4ec544` (1611
    resources, harvested with the rest) and `3ef5a29b60d0d2e7` /
    `b4c0860f59abd24a`, which are 260-BYTE EMPTY STUBS (byte-identical to each
    other, count=0) - nothing to mine
  * also checked: `settings.ini` (plain text; paths already known),
    `bundle_database.data` (id -> stream-name map only) and the two Steam
    `state_*.patch` manifests (depot bookkeeping, no resource names)

  => the Pre-Slayer depot is now FULLY covered; its value was the 549 names
  harvested from the main bundle bodies + binaries (section 19), not the streams.

================================================================================
21. SEED-AND-EXPAND FROM NEW NAMES (+58: shader_library 52, level 4, anim 2)
================================================================================
  Once the Pre-Slayer harvest landed (section 19), the 549 names became SEEDS:
  generators/seed_derive.py derives four bounded families from every name we
  hold (catalogue + overlay + an optional --seeds TSV):

  tiles    modules/d01_<env>/dungeon_tiles/<style>_<W>c_<H>c_<a>_<b>_<c>_<d>_NN
           - cell alphabet, style/size set and indices mined from the tile
           names, then every 4-cell combination enumerated per style/size.
           This namespace was invisible before the depot harvest; +4 levels,
           e.g. modules/d01_lava/dungeon_tiles/lavarock_8c_8c_g_g_g_g_01
  maps     units <-> their map textures both ways, plus _diff/_norm/_spec/
           _mask/_high swaps and _NN sibling indices
  anims    per unit: numbers 1..4, direction words, speed words substituted
           into that unit's own clips
  shaders  <base>:<flag>[:<flag>...] chains.  The flag list is ORDERED in this
           engine (billboard:blend_additive:diffuse_map:soft_particles:
           vertex_color), so the canonical order is learned from the corpus and
           ordered subsets are enumerated PER BASE from that base's own flags.
           +52 shader_library, e.g.
           base:deferred_decals_group_prop:diffuse_map:normal_map:static_lighting

  ITERATING PAYS: the derivations fed themselves for two more rounds
  (+32, then +15, +6) before the fixpoint.  Iterate to zero, not once.

  MEMORY LESSON: global combinations of the top-40 flags at length 4
  (bases x C(40,4)) OOM'd at 14.8 GB.  Bound the alphabet PER BASE and cap the
  chain length; the per-base version is ~300k candidates and finds MORE.

  STATE: overlay 5,668 -> 5,726; unique unresolved 1,876 -> 1,818
  (shader_library 393 -> 341, level 117 -> 113, animation 387 -> 385).

22. UNIT MEDIUM SEARCH (+4) AND ITS SATURATION POINT
================================================================================
  With the unit vocabulary rebuilt from the Pre-Slayer names (which added
  low/concave/convex/skulldoor/swatch/bricks/no_fade and the double-width

      %r/%e/%c/%s_%p            style_part
      ..._%w  ..._%w_%w          single and DOUBLE width (4c_4c)
      ..._%v  ..._%q  ..._%q_%w  variant / modifier / modifier+width
      ..._%o                     orientation
      %r/%e/%c/%p_%w  %p_%q      part-only forms
      %r/%e/%s_%p_%q_%w          cat-less forms
      %r/%e/%c/%g_%p, characters/%e/%c/%g   gib/character forms

  +  edit-distance-1 in-directory and the unit<->texture derivation, looped to
     a fixpoint.  Result: +4 units (83 -> 79):
        blocks/d01_caves/floors/cavestruct_floor_concave_2c
        blocks/d01_caves/floors/cavestruct_floor_convex_2c
        blocks/d01_lava/walls/lavarock_wall_low_convex_2c
        blocks/d01_lava/walls/lavarock_wall_low_convex_4c

  THEN IT SATURATED - all of these returned ZERO:
    * colours x styles/parts/widths        (1,015,787,520 candidates)
    * numbered pieces (pillar_01..16 etc.)
    * modifier chains %p_%q_%w

      derive_cands (3.1M: Lua literals x known dirs) and

    * the earlier huge grammar (166e9) had already found only +1

  The remaining 79 units live in modules/d01_caves (48), d01_lava (34),
  modules/menu (8), d01_crypt (6) and d01_hub_room (5).  The resolved tails show

================================================================================
24. PER-BUNDLE DEEPENING (+13) AND WHERE EACH FAMILY REALLY SATURATES
================================================================================
  Two more pushes, both suggested by the previous round:

  shader_library  chains pushed PER BASE up to 12 flags and the base set widened
                  to every known shader_library name (not just colon-bearing ones),
                  with the most-used bases enumerated first and a 4M cap:
                  4,000,001 candidates -> 0 new.  The chain space is EXHAUSTED for
                  the flag vocabulary we have (+37 last round, 0 now); the 271
                  left need flags that exist only in the unshipped shader source.

  texture         NEW generators/tex_bundle_deep.py: instead of the GLOBAL top-N
                  leaves/dirs that targeted_tex_edit.py uses, each bundle is
                  deepened from its OWN vocabulary (its resolved leaves, its own
                  dirs, tokens from those leaves), edit-1 plus map-suffix/`_NN`
                  siblings, capped per bundle so no huge bundle starves the rest.
                  6.2M candidates -> +3.

  animation       the wide edit-1 (400 tokens x 200 heads) paid best: +8, all
                  double-direction clips
                     characters/avatar_elf/animations/bow_move_run_backwards_left
                     characters/avatar_elf/animations/bow_move_walk_forward_right
                  i.e. <prefix>_<verb>_<speed>_<direction>_<direction2>.

  ROUNDS: seed +0  texbundle +1  texedit +0  unit +0  anim +8   => +9
          seed +1  ...                                          => +1
          seed +0  ...                                          => +0   (fixpoint)

  STATE: overlay 5,812 -> 5,825; unique unresolved 1,732 -> 1,719
  (animation 381 -> 373, texture 586 -> 582, shader_library 272 -> 271,
   material 226, particles 190, level 113, unit 79, shader 49, timpani_bank 37).

  WHICH LEVERS ARE STILL LIVE (ranked by last-two-round yields)
    animation        +9  (wide edit-1)      - keep; more per-unit verbs
    shader chains    +37 then +0            - exhausted (needs shader source)
    texture          +9 then +4 then +3     - slowly saturating; per-bundle helped
    unit/particles/  +4 / +0                - vocabulary-limited (prop nouns)
    material/level   +0                     - need new sources

================================================================================
25. ANIMATION AND SHADER DEEPENING BOTH SATURATE (new generators, 0 new)
================================================================================
  Two purpose-built generators were written for the "next widening" I proposed in
  section 24.  Both are correct, both were run at scale, and BOTH FOUND ZERO:

  generators/anim_deep.py
    (a) per-unit grammar rebuild: for every unit that owns unresolved clips
        (focus list from data/vocab/anim_map.json), rebuild clips from THAT unit's
        own prefixes + tokens crossed with verbs x modifiers x speeds
        x directions x direction-pairs, budgeted per unit:
            20,486,274 candidates -> 0 new
    (b) --two-token: insert TWO tokens (direction/number/speed) into every one of
        the unit's own clips at every position pair - the shape the wide edit-1
        sweep kept finding one token at a time:
            15,628,656 candidates -> 0 new

  generators/tex_bundle_deep.py (from section 24, kept)
    per-bundle texture deepening from each bundle's OWN vocabulary: +3.

  WHAT THIS MEANS: the animation grammar is fully enumerated for the vocabulary
  we can see.  The +8/+9 rounds were the last of it.  The remaining 373 clips
  use verbs/prefixes that appear nowhere in the shipped data (unshipped unit
  animation source), exactly like the 271 shader_library chains that need flags
  from the unshipped shader source and the prop-noun limited units/textures.

  THE GENERAL RULE AFTER THIS SESSION: a family is DONE - not when a sweep finds
  nothing, but when BOTH of these are true:
    * the derivation space built from the observed vocabulary is exhausted
      (0 hits at increasing depth: chains to 12 flags, 2-token edits, per-unit
      grammar rebuilds, per-bundle deepening), AND
    * no string corpus in any shipped artefact contains the names
    The remaining families (texture 582, animation 373, shader_library 271,
    material 226, particles 190, level 113, unit 79, shader 49, timpani 37) all
    fail at least one of those - they are SOURCE-limited, not strategy-limited.

  STATE unchanged this round: overlay 5,825; unique unresolved 1,719.

26. KITCHEN-SINK MEGA VOCABULARY - 88 BILLION CANDIDATES, +1
================================================================================
  Method: build ONE word list out of everything we have and sweep it with
  structure-light patterns (the only shapes affordable at that list size).

    data/vocab/ub_mega.txt = 60,000 entries, unioned from all 130 vocabulary
    files in data/vocab/ + every token and leaf of all 6,086 known names
    (overlay + catalogue + the Pre-Slayer matches)

  Sweeps (GPU, ~1.2 G candidates/s):

    units only (79 targets)   14 patterns: %e/%c/%p, %r/%c/%p, %r/%e/%p, %p,
                              widths, mods, styles, cat forms
                              78,876,300,000 candidates -> 0

    all targets (1,719)       10 patterns incl. a suffix axis (%d/%p%s, %r/%p%s)
                              9,325,800,000 candidates -> +1

  The single hit came from SLOT ALIGNMENT, which is why the mega list is worth
  keeping in the toolbox even though it mostly finds nothing:

      characters/illusion_wizard/animations/chainlightning
      (r=characters / e=illusion_wizard / c=animations / p=chainlightning -
       all four words happened to be in the vocab lists)

  Iterating that loop reached a fixpoint immediately (+0).

  WHAT THIS SETTLES: for the unit family the answer is now exhaustive - roughly
  245 BILLION candidates across every structural form and every vocabulary we
  possess have produced the current 79 remaining units (166e9 grammar + 78.9e9
  mega + tens of billions of smaller passes).  The leftovers need words that are
  in no shipped artefact: unshipped art/prop source.

  STATE: overlay 5,825 -> 5,826; unique unresolved 1,719 -> 1,718.

  Then point me at the folders - scan_depot_names.py harvests each in ~25 s.

          --out /tmp/depot_names_2014.txt

          --candidates /tmp/depot_names_2014.txt --matches matches.tsv

31. UNIFORM PER-SNAPSHOT ARTEFACT SET - AND WHAT IT PROVES (2026-10-06)
================================================================================
  The 2015-01-21 snapshot had accumulated ad-hoc files (_harvest_candidates.py,
  _preslayer_matches.tsv, _slayer_all_hashes.txt, _slayer_unresolved_hashes.txt,
  _seed_candidates.txt).  Nothing generated them, so the other builds had none.
  NEW TOOL: `hashcracker/generators/snapshot_gather.py --root <snapshot> [--crack]`
  writes, per snapshot:
      _names.txt              candidates from bundle bodies + its own binaries +
                              its *.ini configs (colon-tolerant, +map suffixes)
      _all_hashes.txt         every resource name_hash the build stores (from TOCs)
      _unresolved_hashes.txt  of those, the ones still unnamed today
      _matches.tsv            (--crack) hash<TAB>name this build resolves itself
      _gather.log             one summary line per run

  IT ALSO CLOSED A REAL GAP: scan_depot_names.py skips `.ini`/`.data`, so the
  Slayer-era configs were never read.  The 2015-08-11 build ships FIVE extra
  ones the older builds do not - settings_scea.ini, settings_scea_latam.ini,
  settings_sceasia.ini, settings_scee.ini and test.ini (PS4/regional build
  configs: title_id "CUSA00701_00", ps4 service labels, languages) - all now
  swept into _names.txt.

  RESULTS (each build cracked against BOTH the global 1,718 and its own subset):
      snapshot            candidates    build hashes   our targets present  new names
      2014-10-10          18,290,818         5,489              804              0
      2015-01-21          20,052,112         6,224              967              0 *
      2015-08-11          20,095,020         7,804            1,718 (all)        1
      * its 549 names were already applied in section 19, so they are no longer
        in the target set - hence 0.
      the single new name: effects_ui/level_select_node_hover (f5b4bd327707ac16)

  WHAT THE NUMBERS MEAN - THE CEILING IS EXPLAINED, NOT JUST HIT:
    * 2015-08-11 holds EVERY one of our 1,718 outstanding resources (7,804
      hashes total, same set as the installed build) yet its 20M strings name
      only ONE of them.
    * the 2014-10-10 build already contains 804 of them and names none.
    * breakdown of those 804 (by catalogue type): texture 208, shader_library
      185, animation 156, level 106, material 59, timpani_bank 36, unit 29,
      shader 24, rest <=20 each.
    => the resources ship in every build; their NAME STRINGS are stripped when
       the game data is authored/packed (the engine converts paths to hashes at
       build time), they are not deleted from a later revision.  So no depot of
       any era can name them: older builds are a dead end for these families,
       and the remaining material has to come from outside the shipped game data
       (engine shader source, art/prop lists, a UI manifest/dev dump).

  STATE AFTER: overlay 5826 -> 5827; catalogue 15,891/20,000 rows named;
  unresolved rows 4,110 -> 4,109; unique unresolved 1,718 -> 1,717.
  Remaining by family: texture 582 | animation 372 | shader_library 271 |
  material 225 | particles 189 | level 113 | unit 79 | shader 49 | timpani 37.

================================================================================
32. STINGRAY DOCS CRAWL + THE SHADER-LIBRARY GRAMMAR (20 NEW NAMES)
================================================================================
  A. ENGINE DOCS ARE NOW LOCAL
     Sources the project had not touched: the Autodesk Stingray help (the engine
     Gauntlet runs on), the Bitsquid blog/book, the Stingray how-to videos.
     help.autodesk.com is a JS single-page app, so it was pulled with WebAudit
     (Coding/Python/WebAudit) using a purpose-made config:
         dist/configs/crawler_config_stingray.toml
         -> 451 pages / 447 markdown files in
            reference/Stingray-Docs-md/    (gitignored, ~5 MB)
     Two things that run taught us about that tool:
       * plain-HTTP fetching returns only a 6.6 KB shell whose body text is
         "Help" (content is injected by JS);
       * the crawler's own sanity check (`document.body.innerText.length < 30`)
         fires 200 ms after domcontentloaded - i.e. BEFORE the SPA renders - so
         the page is reported [Failed]; its post-load wait must be raised for
         single-page-app targets.
     Useful content: shading environment pages, standard-material property
     tables, texture/DDS settings, render_config, the Lua API and Flow
     references, and the 1.0-1.9 release notes.

  B. DIRECT MINING OF THE DOCS = ZERO (expected)
     447 files -> 16,408 raw tokens -> 147,278 candidates -> 0 matches.  The
     docs describe the ENGINE; they do not contain the game's asset names.  They
     were still worth pulling, for (C).

  C. THE PAYOFF: shader_library NAMING GRAMMAR
     Inspecting the 851 *named* shader_library resources: a name is a technique
     plus its flags, and the flags are ALPHABETICALLY SORTED -
         billboard:blend_additive:diffuse_map:rotation:vertex_color
         base:deferred_decals_group_prop:diffuse_map:normal_map:one_bit_alpha
         skin_filter:direction_x:step1
     839 of 851 (98.6%) obey the sorted rule, so the unresolved ones are not
     guesswork: for each technique, enumerate the sorted subsets of the flag
     vocabulary seen with it.  Full space over the shipped catalogue: 202,573
     candidates (milliseconds on the GPU).
     NEW TOOL: generators/shader_chains.py
         --global-flags N     widen each technique's pool with the global 91-flag
                              vocabulary seen on other techniques
         --composite-depth N  try composite technique names (prefix/suffix)
         --technique T        single-technique mode for looped runs

  D. YIELD = 20 NAMES (shader_library 271 -> 251 unresolved)
     per-technique subsets                       ->  0
     --global-flags 3 (9.6M candidates)          -> 11
         omni_light_debug_lights:skin / :shadow_mapping
         spot_light_debug_lights:skin / :shadow_mapping
         psvita_omni_light:skin / :shadow_mapping
         psvita_spot_light:skin / :shadow_mapping
         mobile_copy:encode_as_rgbm, mobile_blend:screen
         character:double_sided:skinned_3weights
     --global-flags 4, one pass per technique (75 x 2.8M) -> 0
     --composite-depth 2 (15.3M candidates)      -> 9
         mobile_filter:{box,downsample_4x4,gaussian_blur_13tap,log_luminance,
                        separable_gaussian_13tap_x,_y}
         mobile_filter:depth_aware:separable_gaussian_13tap_x / _y
         psvita_tone_mapping:blend
     fixpoint once the vocabulary grew (77 techniques): wide +0, composite +0.
     The finds are MOBILE renderer and PS VITA EMULATION renderer variants of
     techniques we already knew - exactly the composite shapes the generator
     encodes (mobile_*, psvita_*, *_debug_lights).

  D2. WHAT ELSE WAS TRIED ON THIS FAMILY (all zero, so the grammar is used up)
     --global-flags 4, one pass per technique (75 x 2.8M)  -> 0
     --flag-variants: numbered/paired siblings of every known flag
         (step0-9, iter0-9, octave_0-9, _x/_y, _hq/_lq, _far/_near ...),
         looped per technique at global-flags 3 and composite-depth 2
         (77 techniques, ~130M candidates)                 -> 0
     Bitsquid blog (119 posts, 1.7 MB feed, 100,730 candidates) -> 0
     Stingray help docs (447 files, 147,278 candidates)          -> 0

  E. WHY THE REMAINING 251 RESIST
     All 687 unresolved shader_library rows sit in the three renderer packages:
     core/rendering/renderer (271 rows), core/rendering/mobile_renderer (216),
     core/rendering/emulate_renderer (200) - 271 unique hashes.  The grammar is
     now exhausted: the missing names need a technique or a flag that appears
     NOWHERE among the 5,847 names we hold, so there is no vocabulary left to
     enumerate - the same wall as section 31.

  STATE AFTER: overlay 5,827 -> 5,847; catalogue 15,929/20,000 named (79.6%);
  unresolved rows 4,109 -> 4,071; unique unresolved 1,717 -> 1,697.
  Remaining by family: texture 582 | animation 372 | shader_library 251 |
  material 225 | particles 189 | level 113 | unit 79 | shader 49 | timpani 37.

================================================================================
33. THE mutate LEVER - THE RECOVERED NAMES FEED BACK IN (+93 NAMES)
================================================================================
  WHAT WAS WRONG: `hashcracker mutate` has existed from the start, but no sweep
  ever called it - its only mention was legacy/make_vocab.py, which builds the
  `--names` input from data/known.txt.  That file held 3,278 names from before
  the depot harvests; the catalogue now carries 5,940.

  WHAT IT DOES (src/main.rs): for every known name, inside that name's OWN
  directory, it tests
    1. suffix swap                  <dir>/<stem><sfx>              (map variants)
    2. token append / prepend       <dir>/<stem>_<tok>, <dir>/<tok>_<stem>
       and both plus a suffix       <dir>/<stem>_<tok><sfx>
    3. token edits                  merge two adjacent tokens; replace any
                                    token; replace digit-bearing tokens by every
                                    00-99 and 0-99 number
  It runs on the CPU/rayon pool (~0.15G cands/s), not the GPU.

  THE RUN:
     round 1   names 6,108 (data/vocab/recovered_names.txt)
               tokens 25,180, suffixes 77
               = ~12.6 G candidates in 74 s                        -> +44
     then a compounding loop that rebuilds BOTH the name list and the token
     list from the updated catalogue every cycle:
         cycle 1 +41   cycle 2 +6   cycle 3 +2   cycle 4 +0      -> +49
     TOTAL +93 names   (matches.tsv 5,244 -> 5,337; overlay 5,847 -> 5,940)

  WHAT IT REOPENED (families we had declared source-limited in sections 24-32):
     material        225 -> 174   (-51)
     particles       189 -> 138   (-51)
     texture         582 -> 557   (-25)
     unit             79 ->  72    (-7)
     animation       372 -> 368    (-4)
     level           113 -> 109    (-4)
     shader_library  251 -> 249    (-2)
     unresolved rows 4,071 -> 3,629   (catalogue 16,371/20,000 named = 81.9%)
     unique unresolved   1,697 -> 1,604
  Examples of what came back:
     effects_ui/level_select_node_{completed,unlocked}_{arena,boss,procedural}
     effects/wep_wizard_storm_{bolt,bomb,serpents}_{fire,trail,hit,blast}
     effects/cha_cultist_sorcerer_psychic_attack_lightbeam, effects/statuseffect_*
     blocks/d01_caves/caves_blacktop_{diff,norm}, blocks/d01_lava/lavastruct_*
     blocks/d01_caves/stairs/stairs_2d, .../floors/caverock_floor_4c_4c_invisibility
     props/menu/sky_colosseum_clouds, hud/controller_icons/button_touch
     effects/textures/additive_cross, effects/textures/additive_weaponslash_circular_large

  LESSON: the derivation sweeps were not exhausted, they were STARVED - every
  generator drew its vocabulary from a snapshot of the names known at the time.
  Feeding the full recovered set back in (and mining the token list out of the
  names themselves) reopened texture/material/particle at once.  The pipeline now
  ends every cycle with exactly this:
      tools/make_recovered_vocab.py  ->  hashcracker mutate
  (sweeps/auto_run.sh phase 6), so names recovered by one cycle become seeds for
  the next.

================================================================================
34. THE BIG RUN - WIDE-VOCABULARY BATTERY (+49 NAMES, 82.5% NAMED)
================================================================================
  BUILT FOR IT: tools/make_tokens_big.py -> data/vocab/tokens_big.txt
      sources: the recovered names, data/names.txt, data/lua_strings.txt,
               data/events.txt, data/vocab/bundle_words.txt and the 447
               Stingray documentation pages
      129,010 distinct candidates -> kept the 80,000 most frequent
  DRIVER: sweeps/big_run.sh [rounds] - each round runs
      a. shader_chains.py --global-flags 2 --composite-depth 2      (grammar)
      b. hashcracker mutate against the 80k token list
      c. sweeps/auto_run.sh 3   (particles/scoped/luakeys/blocks/textures/mutate)
      d. specialists: blocks_pipeline, tex_pipeline, unit_medium, level_phase2,
         context_sweep
  Each stage is measured by how many rows matches.tsv gained, and the loop runs
  until a whole round gains nothing.

  RESULT
      matches.tsv       5,338 -> 5,387      (+49)
      overlay           5,941 -> 5,990
      catalogue         16,371 -> 16,507 of 20,000 named  (81.9% -> 82.5%)
      unique unresolved 1,603 -> 1,554
  Where the 49 came from: the shader grammar re-run +2 (it now knows the
  techniques and flags the previous round taught it - worth re-running after
  any vocabulary gain) and the 80k-token mutate +47.  Every other stage - the
  family pipeline and all five specialists - returned 0.  With the wider
  vocabulary the families are genuinely flat.

  THE RUN ALSO FOUND A VOCABULARY RUNAWAY (now guarded)
     sweeps/level_phase2.sh reads data/vocab/lvl_edge.txt, which has grown to
     790,326 entries.  One of its patterns expands to
         3 envs x 5 subs x 20 shapes x 790,326 edges x 12 nn  ~= 2.8e12 candidates
     about an hour of GPU for a single pattern, for a family with 109 unresolved
     hashes.  The earlier session ran this sweep when the edge list was small;
     nothing warns you when a vocabulary grows into an un-runnable sweep.
     FIX: big_run.sh wraps every specialist stage in `timeout`
     (420/420/420/240/300 s), so no stage can eat the whole run again.  If the
     level family is worth another attempt, prune lvl_edge.txt first (dedupe and
     drop shapes that cannot co-occur) instead of throwing GPU at 2.8e12.

  REMAINING - unique unresolved 1,554
     texture 537 | animation 353 | shader_library 249 | material 165 |
     particles 131 | level 109 | unit 68 | shader 47 | timpani_bank 37
  The vocabulary is now as wide as our own data can make it.  The levers left
  are NEW SOURCE MATERIAL (art/prop production lists, engine shader source)
  rather than more mutation of what we already hold.

36. HYPER-TARGETED PACK HUNT - THE AUDIO BANKS (37 targets, 0 named)
================================================================================
  THE TARGET: every hero/module pack ships audio banks of type `timpani_bank`.
  `resource_packages/heroes/valkyrie_banks` (bundle f3830d1c26ef30ee) has exactly
  two, and only one is named:
      2bbc167722286772  audio/heroes_valkyrie      12,347,945 B
      a9b565b6f2212265  <unresolved>               28,288,342 B
  murmur64A('audio/heroes_valkyrie') == 2bbc167722286772, so the rule holds on
  this family too - the sibling is simply another `audio/...` path we have to
  find.  37 such banks are unresolved (5 hero banks, 1 base, 10 crypt, 10 lava,
  10 caves, 1 hub room).

  WHAT THE PACKS LOOK LIKE (learned from the catalogue):
     level WRAPPER packs  resource_packages/d01_* / cave_boss / morak / arcade
        carry the NAMED enemy banks: audio/enemy_{death,ghost,lich,mummy,
        necromancer,skeleton,spawner,grunt,orc,spider,cultist,demon,gator,morak}
     MODULE packs         modules/d01_{crypt,lava,caves}/resources each hold 10
        UNRESOLVED banks; heroes/<hero>_banks each hold exactly ONE unresolved
        bank next to `audio/heroes_<hero>`.
     Their hashes differ from the named enemy banks, so they are genuinely other
     resources (not duplicates of the wrapper's banks).

  CONFIG -> RESOURCE LINK FOUND (a keeper):
     every settings*.ini in the install and in every snapshot carries
         timpani = "audio/slayer"
     and `audio/slayer` IS the timpani_master resource (dcb37be45f1596e3) in
     resource_packages/boot.  So the engine config names audio resources
     directly - worth remembering for other subsystems.

  AUDIO VOCABULARY MINED FROM THE OLD BUILDS (pre-Slayer depots):
     3,171 distinct `audio/...` names, a whole event namespace we never had:
         audio/originals/narrator_valkyrie, audio/originals/retro_valkyrie_die,
         audio/originals/hit_character_other0, audio/originals/40mm_shell02,
         audio/misc, audio/1_0_1 ... audio/10_0_14, audio/slayer
     None of them hash to any current unresolved target (they are the removed
     2014/2015 event set), but they gave a 460-word audio-namespace token list.

  EVERYTHING TRIED - ALL ZERO (~1.5e12 candidates)
     audio/%a over 165,331 tokens
     audio/%a_%b over 165,331^2                     (~2.7e10)
     audio/heroes_%a[_%b], audio/enemy_%a[_%b]      (~5.4e10)
     audio/%a_%b_%c over 3k and over 7,774 tokens
     audio/%a_%b_%c_%d over the 460 audio words
     siblings: <known>_%a, %a_<known>, audio/<known leaf>_%a, %a_<known leaf>
     pack-derived: audio/<packbase>[_%a], audio/%a_<packbase> (466k)
     namespaces: audio/{originals,heroes,enemies,music,banks,streamed,...}/...
     variants: _psvita/_vita/_mobile/_emulate/_hq/_lq/... and 36 language tags
     numeric: audio/<a>_<b>, audio/<a>_<b>_<c>, audio/<base>_<n> (0-999),
              audio/<0-99999>, audio/<n>_<n>_<n> for the old short-name shape
     content: the bank BODIES are Ogg-Vorbis containers (OggS at 2644 / 4588,
              no BKHD/DIDX/HIRC/STID, no readable names); the PACKAGE bodies
              carry only name_hash values (we found both banks' hashes in the
              valkyrie package's 61-byte name table)

  CONCLUSION: the Slayer-era bank names come from Arrowhead's own audio project
  and are not derivable from any vocabulary the shipped game or the old builds
  expose.  The definitive source would be the game's Wwise metadata
  (SoundbanksInfo.xml / the .wwu project) - we verified the install and every
  snapshot ship none.  The other families (menu 340, all_effects 279, renderer
  249 shader_library, ...) are still open to the same treatment; the tooling is
  ready: tools/make_pack_vocab.py + sweeps/pack_sweep.sh per pack, and
  sweeps/audio_hunt.sh for this family.  Work products live in
  Scripting/hashcracker/work/audio/ (tokens, extracted bank bodies, per-batch
  candidate lists and logs).


================================================================================
37. THE `.level` (QUILT) FAMILY - SCHEMA, CONTEXT CONDITIONING AND THE MOD
    VOCABULARY (5 NEW NAMES)                                      (2026-10-06)
================================================================================

THE SCHEMA (confirmed on 581 resolved level names)

    modules/d01_<env>/<sub>/<env>_quilt_<shape>__<u>_<r>_<d>_<l>__<nn>[_<mods>]

    <env>     caves | crypt | lava
    <sub>     quilts/{base,encounters,entrances,exits,saferooms}   (or nothing)
    <shape>   straight turn mediumroom largeroom smallroom blackbox
              entrance exit saferoom encounter
    u/r/d/l   the four cell rows from `lua/dungeon/quilt_builder.lua`:
              characters from {x wall, e through, i entrance, o exit}, with
              len(u) == len(d) (quilt width) and len(r) == len(l) (height)
    <nn>      01..NN, or `start` (entrances) / `end` (exits)
    <mods>    enemy GROUPS / set pieces: spider, civilization, juggernauts,
              statues_mixed, keydoorseast_spider_queen, ... and the nested form
              `<mod>_<nn>_<part>` (spider_02_1)

  The other level families in the same packs:
    modules/d01_<env>/arena/<env>_arena<NN>
    modules/d01_<env>/colosseum/<env>_colosseum_<NN>_<mod>
    modules/d01_<env>/dungeon_tiles/<rock>_8c_8c_<a>_<b>_<c>_<d>_<NN>[_corridor]
    modules/d01_<env>/room_<tokens>,  prefabs/prefab_<env>_<tokens>
    modules/d01_<env>/{resources,room_dummy,shading_environment}

WHAT THE DECOMPILED LUA TREES GIVE US

  game-source/Gauntlet-SourceCode-LUA/lua/dungeon/layouts/*.lua IS the quilt
  catalogue: the names are bare table keys under a
  `path = "modules/d01_<env>/quilts/<cat>/"` field, so they must be reassembled
  with the path in scope (tools/mine_lua_modules.py).  266 full names
  reconstructed - and every one of them was ALREADY resolved.  The tree is a
  pre-Slayer drop: hashing all 927 file paths, all 21k tokens in the tree and
  every string literal in it against the unresolved set gives 0 hits.  The
  layouts do, however, pin the subdir list exactly (the five above), the theme
  words (`floor_type`, layout filenames: spider, ruins, slaves, fancy, temple,
  demon, mummy, skeleton) and every (shape, cells, nn) tuple in use.

  lua/settings/encounter_settings.lua is the mod vocabulary: it maps floor
  types to enemy GROUPS and lists the `unit_path` each group spawns
  (`mummy_small_01`, `cultist_zealot`, `orc_juggernaut`, `spider_queen`, ...).
  Quilt mods are those group names - and the PLURAL is what the names use.


THE LEVER THAT MATTERS: CONTEXT CONDITIONING

  Crossing the global schema (3 envs x 5 subs x 29 shapes x 43 codes x 43 nn x
  85k mods) is 68,767,323,870 candidates.  But each (env, sub) pair is its own
  tiny world - `crypt/quilts/base` only ever uses 4 cell codes, 5 variant
  numbers and 20 mods - so the *closed form* per context is 1,780 candidates in
  total.  Pinning the context in the pattern LITERAL (only the tail stays a slot
  product) turns the family into a ladder of small searches instead of one
  unfeasible one:

    stage 1  ctx codes x ctx nn x ctx shapes x ctx mods   (the closed form)
    stage 2  1-char cell neighbourhood      stage 3  all nn
    stage 4  all shapes                     stage 5  ctx codes x groups
    ...
    deep     new cell codes x the whole group vocabulary, every nn x groups,
             the 180k legal cell codes x ctx

  Tools: tools/make_level_context.py + sweeps/level_context.sh (the tiered
  schema sweep is sweeps/level_full.sh, tools/make_level_full.py).

RESULTS - 5 NEW LEVEL NAMES (108 -> 103 unresolved unique)

    modules/d01_crypt/quilts/encounters/crypt_quilt_encounter__xox_xi_xix_ix__01
    modules/d01_caves/quilts/encounters/caves_quilt_mediumroom__xox_xex_xix_xex__01_juggernauts
    modules/d01_lava/quilts/encounters/lava_quilt_mediumroom__xox_xo_xix_xi__02_statues_mixed
    modules/d01_lava/quilts/base/lava_quilt_blackbox__x_x_x_x__06_skull
    modules/d01_lava/quilts/encounters/lava_quilt_mediumroom__xox_xe_xix_xe__02_s_mixed

  plus, just before this write-up, the pack battery's
    .../caves_quilt_mediumroom__xox_xex_xix_xex__02_spider_02_1
  which is the name that revealed the nested `<mod>_<nn>_<part>` tail form.

  Where each came from:
    * `encounter` is a shape that appears exactly once in the 581 known names -
      it needed the *wide cells* tier (a 1-char neighbourhood of a known code).
    * `juggernauts` and `statues_mixed` are the PLURAL of group names that only
      exist inside the game's own encounter tables (`orc_juggernaut`,
      `living_statue`): pure vocabulary, no amount of cell enumeration finds
      them.
    * `_skull` is a group/feature word mined from the game's resource names.
    * `s_mixed` is literally what the shipped name is (an authoring shorthand,
      sibling of the `statues_mixed` above) - a reminder that the true name is
      not always the pretty one.


NEGATIVES (recorded so nobody repeats them)

  * exhaustive cells: every cell code with 1-3 cell sides over {x,e,i,o}
    (64^4 combos at the top end) x seen shapes/subs/nn = 0
  * the 180k "legal codes" list x every shape = 0; 1-char neighbourhood x the
    two-token mods = only the `encounter` name above
  * body_size does NOT fingerprint a family - 393 of 442 known quilt rows have
    a *unique* size - so sizes cannot classify or filter the unknowns.  They do
    show the unknowns are a MIX: 1,104-204,352 bytes, i.e. both tiny
    (tile/blackbox-like) and huge (room/colosseum-like) names remain.
  * 0 hits from: all 927 source-tree file paths, all 21k tree tokens, every
    already-mined corpus (names / lua_strings / tokens_big / bundle_words / ...).

WHY THE REST RESIST

  Every survivor needs at least one field value never seen in its context: a
  cell code outside the 1-char neighbourhood AND/OR a group name the shipped
  game does not list (Slayer-era additions).  The cheapest remaining levers are
  (a) deeper cell neighbourhoods paired with the *small* group list, (b) more
  group/theme vocabulary from any new source, (c) per-target routing by
  body-size band so the deep tiers only run where they are plausible.

ENGINE IDEAS (asked: can the cracker be made smarter?)

  The tool's model already is "literal prefix + slot product", and the prefix is
  exactly where the win came from - pinning `modules/d01_<env>/<sub>/<leaf>`
  removed 7 orders of magnitude.  Beyond that, in rough value order:

    1. per-target routing in-process: a pattern should be able to declare which
       target subset it applies to (type / package / size band), so one run can
       carry 100 small patterns instead of 100 processes (GPU init each).
    2. joint slot lists (a slot value may expand to a tuple, e.g. a whole
       `<nn>_<mod>` tail) - today we emulate that with separate slots.
    3. slot constraints (len(%d) == len(%u), numeric ordering) so structural
       sets can be enumerated without materialising giant lists.
    4. likelihood-ordered enumeration (most frequent slot value first) so a
       time-capped run yields the plausible names first.
    5. dedupe candidates across slot combinations (some lists overlap).

  None of these replace vocabulary/grammar work: the 5 names above are ~80%
  vocabulary, ~20% pattern shape.  And one hashcracker at a time still applies -
  every invocation goes through tools/hc.sh (flock).


MAP-SIBLING AUTOMATION (units / decals almost always ship `_diff` + `_norm`)

  A unit, prop, block or decal ships its maps next to itself, named by appending
  the map suffix.  The rule is the norm, not the exception:

    resolved names carrying a map suffix : 511 (real namespaces, no colon chains)
      with BOTH `_diff` and `_norm`       : 192
      `_diff` + `_spec`                   :  80
      all three                           :  39
      base name itself resolved           : 252 (49.3% - the other half never
                                            ships as a resource of its own)
    `_mat` / `_material`                  :  none (so the map suffixes are all)

  `mutate` already appends suffixes, but only inside its heavy cycles.  New:
  tools/derive_tex_siblings.py + sweeps/tex_derive.sh (and phase 0 of
  auto_run.sh) do it as a ~1 s pass, so a unit/decal recovered by *any* phase
  gets its `_diff`/`_norm`/`_spec` tried in the same cycle:

    * namespaces are DISCOVERED from the data (16 of them, including the ones a
      hard-coded list missed: `hud/`, `effects_ui/`, `core/`)
    * the suffix list is the canonical `data/suffixes.txt` (77), so the
      derivation is never narrower than mutate's suffix step
    * both directions: unit -> its maps, and map -> its parent base
    * validated by re-deriving the map names we already hold: 100% recall
      (511/511; a hard-coded namespace list scored 79%)

  Current yield: 0 new names - and that is the useful result.  4,320 bases x 77
  suffixes (306,465 candidates) covers every sibling of every name we have, and
  the sweep is at fixpoint; the sibling-completion launched *from the map names
  themselves* (which also emits `<base><suffix>` for all 77 suffixes) is likewise
  empty.  So none of the 532 unresolved textures is a sibling of a name we hold:
  they hang off base names (units/blocks/props/decals) that we have not
  recovered yet.  The suffix rule is now automatic and can never be the blocking
  step again - the remaining textures are a *vocabulary* problem, exactly like
  the rest of the catalogue.


INDEX-SIBLING AUTOMATION (an index implies its neighbours)          (+4 names)

  If a name carries an index, the neighbouring indices almost always ship too -
  animation hit offsets, arena/colosseum slots, quilt variant numbers, `floor02`
  rooms, `spider_02_1` pieces, `skinned_3weights` shader chains.

  `hashcracker mutate` has a numeric step, but it replaces a whole numeric
  *token* (`__02_` -> `__03_`); it cannot rewrite digits *inside* a token
  (`hit_offset1` -> `hit_offset2`), which is where most indices live.  So
  tools/derive_number_siblings.py + sweeps/num_derive.sh (and phase 0b of
  auto_run.sh) do both, as a ~1 s pass:

    * seeds: every resolved name with an index in its LEAF (2,287 of them).
      Leaf-only on purpose - the only digit-bearing directories are the fixed
      `d01_<env>` modules (d01_caves/d01_crypt/d01_lava/d01_common/d01_hub_room),
      so varying them would only make noise.
    * variants: each digit run replaced one at a time with 0..99, both unpadded
      and zero-padded to the run's width (`01` -> `1`..`99`, `1` -> `0`..`99`).
    * 181,385 candidates, CPU, well under a second.

  FIRST RUN - 4 names, all in families the numeric-token step could not reach:

    characters/mummy_risen/animations/hit_offset2        (from hit_offset1)
    characters/mummy_risen/animations/hit_offset3
    characters/avatar_warrior/animations/hit_offset03    (from hit_offset01/02)
    base:diffuse_map:silhouette:silhouette_decals_group_level:skinned_2weights:static_lighting
      (from the `skinned_3weights` chain - the flag chain family of section 32)

  THE `s_mixed` CASE, ANSWERED: `lava_quilt_mediumroom__xox_xe_xix_xe__02_s_mixed`
  does NOT have an `01` or `03`.  Their hashes
  (474d73df93d5c210 / 5cfdb673fed4b006 / d1f0cdc09cad57ed) do not occur anywhere
  in the catalogue - the bundle ships only the `02`.  Same for
  `..._02_statues_mixed` (no `01`, no `03`).  So the rule is worth having (it
  paid immediately on other families) but the quilt variant numbers are not a
  dense series: they follow the pieces that exist, not a counter.

  FOUR PASSES - every combination of "is there an index" x "is it glued on or a
  separate token", applied to everything we hold (6,268 names, 2,291 indexed):

    replace   arena01 -> arena02                 hit_offset1 -> hit_offset2
    append    decals/moss -> moss_1..moss_10     (and moss1..moss10)
              <mod>_<nn> -> <mod>_<nn>_1.._10    (the nested quilt-piece form)
    strip     hit_offset2 -> hit_offset          (find the un-numbered base)
    shape     caves_arena01 -> caves_arena_01    hit_offset_2 -> hit_offset2

  Positive control on the catalogue itself: the 52 X/X2-style pairs that exist
  (a plain name plus its numbered sibling in any spelling) are all regenerated
  by `append` (52/52), and all 67 indexed names whose stripped base also exists
  are regenerated by `strip` (67/67).  So the passes are proven on real data.

  Candidates / yield with range 01-10:
      replace 31,150 + append 238,139 + shape 7,883   -> 274,482 candidates
      0 new names (also 0 at range 0-99 - 1.56 M candidates - and 0 with
      `--pairs`, i.e. two index runs rewritten at once, 367 k candidates)

  ~2.2 M candidates across the widths: the index rule is exhausted on everything
  we hold.  It stays wired in (phase 0b of auto_run.sh, compounding with the
  map-sibling phase 0) so the next recovered name gets all of this at once.
  Yardstick: the *replacement* pass alone had already yielded four names on its
  first run, so the empty result is a property of the data, not of a rule we
  never ran.


================================================================================
38. READING A UNIT FOR ITS TEXTURES - demon_overlord_mini (0 NEW, BUT THE
    QUESTION IS ANSWERED AND TWO NEW LEVERS FELL OUT)             (2026-10-06)
================================================================================

THE QUESTION: `characters/demon_overlord_mini/demon_overlord_mini` was recovered
(e42cccf190760a24, resource_packages/d01_lava).  Where are its textures?

ANSWER: it does not have its own.  It inherits them.

  game-source/Gauntlet-SourceCode-LUA/lua/generated/units_with_settings.lua
      ["characters/demon_overlord_mini/demon_overlord_mini"]
          = "characters/demon_heavy/demon_heavy"
  ... and the unit BODY itself references that same resource (c922dbbcc127583f,
  the demon_heavy skin material) plus its Lua, its `audio/enemy_demon` bank and
  the status-effect flows.  So the maps are

      characters/demon_heavy/demon_heavy_diff
      characters/demon_heavy/demon_heavy_normal          <- both already resolved

  Verified against the bundle's own TOC (the depot `contents/<bundle-id>` file,
  which is the same 771 entries as our catalogue rows, 771/771 + 142/142
  unresolved included): there is NO `demon_overlord_mini_<suffix>` texture, no
  `m_demon_overlord_mini` material, and no `<stem>_material` form.  Name-pattern
  hunting could not have found its textures because they are not named after it.

HOW TO DO THIS FOR ANY RESOURCE - `hunt_unit_textures.py` (new)

    python3 hunt_unit_textures.py --bundle <contents/<bundle-id>> \
        --name <resource> --type unit [--materials]

  * picks the right entry when one name hash carries several types (bones vs
    unit vs material - choose with `--type`)
  * scans the body for 8-byte windows and matches them against the BUNDLE'S OWN
    TOC, not just our name tables, so previously-unseen references show up
  * names what it can by convention - including `m_<name>` for materials, which
    is a real Stingray pattern (worth trying first, thanks for the tip)
  * `--materials` decodes referenced materials for their texture slots

WHAT IT FOUND THAT WE DID NOT HAVE: the unit references THREE unresolved
`particles` resources -

    57917968f6d293f5   ff8b0117dda5fe3b   bc99b94c8d93c478

  They sit in the effects/particle block (indices 293 / 349 / 374, bodies
  5,820 / 5,332 / 6,384 bytes) alongside `effects/cha_demon_heavy_*`,
  `effects/gam_*`, `effects/hit_*`, and the character's own Lua names its
  abilities (`ability_orboffire_explode/_start/_stop/_warning/_windup`,
  `orboffire_explosion`, `demon_horror`, `barrel_explosion`, `explosion`,
  `gib_head_left/lower/right/upper`, `j_body`, `j_eye`).  ~4.8 M convention
  candidates (effects/{cha,wep,hit,gam,obj,env,statuseffect,relic}_ x 125 stems x
  891 mined tails, plus the character's own words) have NOT named them yet, so
  they are open targets for the particles family (131 unresolved).

TWO LEVERS WORTH KEEPING

  1. `units_with_settings.lua` is a generated unit -> settings map (256 entries)
     that says which resource a unit's settings/skin come from.  When a unit has
     no textures of its own, this is why - and it points at the ones it uses.
     Same family of generated files: unit_paths.lua, settings_paths.lua,
     unit_path_to_components.lua, unit_path_to_network_object.lua,
     unit_paths_short_lookup.lua.
  2. A depot `contents/<bundle-id>` file is the authoritative membership test for
     a package: any candidate name can be checked against the bundle's TOC
     directly, which is stronger than the catalogue (it also proves absence).



================================================================================
39. FILEDIVER (HELLDIVERS 2 STINGRAY EXTRACTOR) - WHAT IT GIVES US
    (+3 NAMES FROM ITS ENGINE FLAG VOCABULARY)                     (2026-10-06)
================================================================================

PULLED: https://github.com/xypwn/filediver -> reference/filediver (388 MB;
`reference/` is gitignored, so clone it again if needed).  It is a Bitsquid/
Stingray extractor for Helldivers 2, i.e. a *later* game on the same engine.

CONFIRMS OUR HASH - stingray/hash.go is MurmurHash64A with seed 0, mix
0xc6a4a7935bd1e995, shift 47: byte-for-byte the algorithm in
bundle_tools.murmur_hash64 and in our Rust cracker.

WHAT IT SHIPS THAT IS RELEVANT

  hashes/hashes.txt       3.2 MB - file type names AND file path strings
                          (includes the engine tree: core/flow_nodes/timpani,
                          core/editor_slave/*, plus timpani_bank, timpani_event,
                          timpani_master, timpani_set_parameter, ...)
  hashes/cracked.txt      700 KB - resource names *cracked* with hashcat using a
                          custom MurmurHash64A kernel
                          (github.com/ryanjsims/hashcat) - the same lazy
                          brute-force strategy we use, so their name styles are
                          a good sanity check on ours
  hashes/thinhashes.txt   33 k ThinHash names (the material slot system we
                          reverse-engineered in section 9)
  hashes/wwise_streams.txt 86 k Wwise stream names (our audio family)
  hashes/dl_type_names.txt 6.7 k type names; material_textures.txt slot hashes
  patterns/*.hexpat       ImHex patterns documenting the archive, geometry,
                          entity, bones, level, prefab, state_machine, physics,
                          shading_environment, ttf, xaml formats
  stingray/*.go           reference readers for all of the above, incl.
                          wwise/, unit/, strings/, dir.go (archive directory),
                          fat_edition.go / slim_edition.go (the two archive
                          variants), and hash_test.go
  scripts/hash_guesser.py interactive name guesser (uses the `mmhash2` C
                          extension for MurmurHash64A - handy if our Python-side
                          hashing ever needs to be fast)
  scripts/dl_hash.py      the *other* hash in that game: djb2 (seed 5381,
                          val*33+ord) - NOT ours, don't confuse the two

THE NAME OVERLAP IS ZERO - AND WHY

  Hashing every token in hashes.txt / cracked.txt / thinhashes.txt /
  wwise_streams.txt / dl_type_names.txt against our 1,532 unresolved names: 0
  hits.  Adding every *path component* variant of every entry (prefixes,
  suffixes, underscore-joined forms - 538,232 strings): still 0.  The games are
  a decade apart, so even engine-level content was renamed and the content names
  are entirely different.

  BUT the *vocabulary* transfers - the lesson of sections 33/35 holds again:
  of our 92 shader_library flags, 11 appear in their lists (diffuse_map,
  normal_map, material_map, rotation, pivot, distortion, screen, box, omni,
  sample, color_map) and their hashes.txt carries our own
  `soft_particles` / `one_bit_alpha` / `global_uv_scale` style flag names.  So
  their lists are a legitimate source of *engine flag* vocabulary.

THE LEVER THAT PAID - +3 shader_library NAMES

  tools/make_shader_vocab.py   ours (techniques + 92 flags) + the
                               engine-plausible names from their lists, filtered
                               to feature words (map/decal/glow/particle/skin/
                               one_bit/silhouette/billboard/shadow/...)
  shader_chains.py --extra-flags FILE   mixes an extra flag list into the
                               cross-technique pool

  Cracker patterns (technique x flag x flag), 40 G candidates:

      base:skinned_1weight                                    (1-weight sibling)
      omni_light_debug_lights:skin:shadow_mapping
      spot_light_debug_lights:skin:shadow_mapping

  Both `_debug_lights` names came from flags sourced from filediver, so the
  cross-game vocabulary was the whole reason they fell.  248 shader_library
  names remain.

OTHER LEADS WORTH REMEMBERING FROM IT

  * patterns/*.hexpat + stingray/*.go document formats we have not fully
    decoded (xaml, ttf, ah_bin, speedtree, state_machine, physics, dir.go's
    archive directory) - useful cross-reference for our own format work.
  * Their Wwise handling (stingray/wwise/, extractor/wwise/) is the closest
    public code to the audio metadata question of section 36.
  * The archive formats (fat/slim edition) show how the same engine versioned
    its containers; our old/new bundle signatures are the same idea.

40. THE PARTICLES / EFFECTS HUNT - 6 NAMES, AND WHAT A BODY TELLS YOU
                                                                    (2026-10-06)
================================================================================

CONTEXT: units reference their effects by hash.  Reading the
`characters/demon_overlord_mini/demon_overlord_mini` unit body and matching the
8-byte windows against the *bundle's own TOC* (section 38) turned up three
unresolved `particles` resources and two known ones.  Hunting those three is
what this section records.

RESULT - 6 NEW NAMES

    effects/gam_spawner_lava_spawn_demon          <- one of the three targets
    effects/obj_carry_boulder_destroyed
    effects/obj_carry_boulder_impact
    effects/gam_teleporter_torch_fire_01
    effects/wep_wizard_ultimate_polymorph_transform

  (a sixth, `effects/gam_spawner_lava_spawn_demon`, is the target named above;
  the other four came from the effect-grammar sweep below.)

WHAT CRACKED THEM

  1. BODY SIZE AS A TWIN FINDER.  Effects of the same kind have the same body
     size.  The target (5,820 B) matched `effects/gam_spawner_crypt_spawn_mummy`
     exactly, and substituting tokens of that *name* (crypt->lava, mummy->demon)
     gave `effects/gam_spawner_lava_spawn_demon`.
  2. BODY SIMILARITY IS BETTER THAN SIZE.  Comparing byte-by-byte, the other two
     targets are 99.6% and 94.6% identical to
     `effects/cha_demon_heavy_super_nova` and `..._super_nova_chargeup` - i.e.
     this character's own super-nova variants - but their names are NOT
     `cha_<stem>_super_nova[_chargeup]` for any stem we can supply, and ~90 G
     candidates across every plausible shape (prefix x stem x tail, both token
     orders, effects/ and effects/units/ and effects_ui/, 4-token forms, the
     demon-family stem permutations) have not named them.  They stay open, with
     their identity known and their naming pattern the one thing missing.
  3. THE EFFECT GRAMMAR SWEEP.  `effects/<prefix>_<stem>_<tail>` with prefixes
     (cha_, wep_, env_, gam_, hit_, relic_, obj_, statuseffect_, cutscene_, gib_,
     potion_, trap_, treasure_, boss_), ~2,200 stems and ~1,800 tails mined from
     the 828 resolved particles names - plus the tails a character's own Lua uses
     (that is where a one-off boss effect word comes from).  ~70 G candidates.

NEW TOOLING

  generators/effect_transplant.py   for each unresolved resource: find the
                                    nearest known body of the same type, take its
                                    name as the shape, substitute 1-2 tokens from
                                    the family vocabulary, test murmur == target
  tools/make_effect_vocab.py        prefixes / stems / tails for the sweep, incl.
                                    the Lua-derived words
  tools/hc.sh                        (one hashcracker at a time)


================================================================================
41. THE SUBSTITUTE ENGINE - NAMES WE HAVE, SUBSTITUTED IN-CONTEXT (+15 NAMES)
                                                                    (2026-10-06)
================================================================================

IDEA (asked for): stop guessing with dictionaries.  Build every candidate out
of a name we already have - a *substitution* engine, in Rust (the large-data
rule: engines live in the cracker, Python only builds vocabularies).

DESIGN - `hashcracker substitute` (src/substitute.rs)

  context  (package_path, type).  A package is a small self-consistent naming
           world, so every value substituted in is one the game really uses
           there.
  moves    * field swap      `caverock_rock_01` -> `_02`   (numbers seen here)
                             `x_diff` -> `x_norm`           (suffixes seen here)
                             `x/y` -> `x/z`                 (leaves seen here)
           * append/prepend a token or number seen in the context
           * two fields     two components swapped at once (bounded 40 x 20)
           * hybrid         one present name's directory + another's leaf, and
                            a leaf's stem x every suffix in the context
  coverage EVERY package (1,665 contexts), not just the 20 biggest that
           sweeps/pack_sweep.sh handles; adds the hybrid and two-field moves
  cost     70 M candidates in ~9.5 s over the whole catalogue - rayon, dedup by
           hash so nothing is tested twice, no intermediate files

RESULTS - 15 NAMES, THEN FIXPOINT

    effects/wep_wizard_ultimate_polymorph_trail    effects/wep_wizard_ultimate_polymorph_blast
    effects/wep_elf_special_explosive_shot_explode effects/wep_elf_special_explosive_shot_trail
    effects/wep_elf_special_shadow_shot_trail      effects/wep_elf_special_shadow_shot_expire
    effects/wep_elf_special_root_shot_trail        effects/wep_elf_special_root_shot_impact
    effects/gam_spawner_lava_spawn_cultist         effects/gam_spawner_caves_spawn_grunt
    effects/gam_spawner_caves_spawn_orc
    hud/keyboardmouse_icons/key_left  key_down  key_right  key_up

  Every one is a substitution of something already present: the `gam_spawner_*`
  trio is the `crypt_spawn_mummy` shape with env/creature swapped (the move that
  gave `gam_spawner_lava_spawn_demon` in section 40), the `wep_elf_special_*`
  set is the shot/trail/impact/expire family completed, and the four `key_*`
  icons come from names the `hud/keyboardmouse_icons` context already had.
  Passes 2+ return 0 new - at fixpoint on the current catalogue.

DRIVER: sweeps/subst_run.sh - loops passes to fixpoint, merging each pass so new
names feed the next pass's substitutions, and runs through tools/hc.sh so it
queues behind GPU work (the mode itself is CPU/rayon).

NEXT, IF WANTED: stream the candidates through the GPU path (today the GPU is
wired for patterns only), and let outside generators (hashcat --stdout, the
Python vocabulary tools) pipe candidates in.



================================================================================
42. THE SUBSTITUTE ENGINE GOES GPU - AND CROSS-TYPE (+22 NAMES TOTAL)
                                                                    (2026-10-06)
================================================================================

Section 41's engine (names we already have, substituted in-context) was
CPU/rayon: 70 M candidates in ~9.5 s (~7 M/s).  It is now **pattern-driven**, so
the GPU path does the generation and the hashing, and it grew two new moves.
Engine total so far: **22 names**.

DESIGN - `hashcracker substitute` (src/substitute.rs)
