  per-type    positional shapes at depth 2-4-5 (any token position may take a
              value seen in that position), numbers +/-3 with the width kept
              (`_03` -> `_02`, `_04`, `_00`), plural siblings
              (`juggernaut` -> `juggernauts`), stem+suffix, stem+number, and
              directory-as-a-slot (the hybrid move)
  cross-type  catalogue-wide pools - every directory x every stem x every
              suffix/number - PLUS the mined vocabularies (`--vocab FILE`).
              This is the pass that reaches a texture named after its unit
              (`characters/x/x_diff`) or a name built from a word that lives in
              another family.
  knobs       `--by-package` (the section-41 precise/slow variant), `--depth N`,
              `--top N`, `--vocab FILE...`, `--cpu`, `--suffixes FILE`

MEASURED

    per-type   131 M candidates in 0.4 s    (325 M/s, GPU)
    depth 4    137 M candidates in 0.6 s
    cross     4.45 G candidates in 12.5 s   (356 M/s, GPU)   -> +7 names
    same shape on CPU (section 41): 70 M in 9.5 s (~7 M/s) - ~50x slower

  Vocabulary size is not free: a 400 k-word pool ran ~5x slower per candidate
  (bigger word table, cache misses); 80-150 k is the sweet spot.

THE 7 CROSS-TYPE NAMES (engine total 22)

    characters/illusion_wizard/animations/iceshards      (vocab: iceshards)
    characters/illusion_wizard/animations/squat          (vocab: squat)
    characters/cultist_sorcerer/animation/attack_serpent (vocab: serpent)
    blocks/d01_caves/stairs/roundstairs_01               (vocab: roundstairs)
    blocks/d01_caves/stairs/roundstairs_02
    menu/shop/shop_item_equipped_glow                    (vocab: shop_item)
    shaders/crystal_ball                                 (vocab: crystal_ball)

  Note `characters/cultist_sorcerer/animation/...` - singular, where every other
  clip lives in `animations/`.  The engine has no trouble with that because a
  directory is just another slot.

FIXPOINT - EXHAUSTED AT EVERY GRANULARITY

  per-package (70 M), per-type (131 M), depth 4 (137 M), cross-type (4.45 G),
  cross-type with a 150 k vocabulary (8.5 G), three rounds with re-merging
  between each: **0 new**.  The substitution space over everything present - and
  over every vocabulary we have mined - is exhausted at those granularities.

  That is not the same as "the remaining names need components that appear
  nowhere".  For the *level* family it was simply the wrong setup: the pieces are
  reachable, we were enumerating the wrong shape.  Section 43 is what that
  investigation turned up (+2 names, and 83 G candidates of hard negatives that
  now bound the remaining problem tightly).

==========================================================================
43. LEVELS, PROPERLY: FAMILIES, TWINS, AND THE LUA THAT NAMES THEM
==========================================================================

  Unresolved level rows: 124 -> 122 (101 unique hashes), all of them inside
  `modules/d01_<env>/resources` (crypt 56, lava 39, caves 27).

43.1 WHAT THE REMAINING LEVELS ARE NOT

  Each sweep below is GPU (Vulkan) and each carries a *positive control* - a
  known member of the family added to the target list, which the run must hit.
  Without the control a 0 is meaningless; with it, the negatives are real.

  quilts, 27.2 G candidates: every `<sub>/<env>_quilt_<shape>__<cells>__<nn>[_<mod>]`
  over 1,419 shape/cells pairs (each shape's own codes plus one-character
  neighbours and one-group swaps), 26 numbers, 5 subdirs + bare, and 40,939 mod
  forms (token n-grams mined from names + *prop* names).  622-744 M/s, 36 s ->
  **0**.  Control: `crypt_quilt_straight__e_x_e_x__01_slaves` is hit.

  tiles, 53.2 G candidates: `<word>_<dims>_<a>_<b>_<c>_<d>_<nn>[_corridor]` over
  a 400,000-word list x 2 dims x 462 quads (observed + neighbourhood) x 24
  numbers x 3 environments -> **0**.  Control: `lavarock_8c_8c_w_u_u_c_01` is
  hit.  The resolved tile corpus is caves 19 / lava 34 / **crypt 0**, so crypt's
  tiles (if that is what they are) are not named `<rock>_8c_8c_...` at all.

  per-target templates, 2.8 G candidates: for every target, its closest-by-body-
  size resolved names give a template with the trailing tokens replaced by
  `%n`/`%m`/`%x` (949 patterns x 40,939 mods x 34 numbers x 11 wings) -> **0**.

  Rooms/arena/colosseum/menu/hub templates with the prop-derived mod vocabulary
  -> **+2** (43.3).

43.2 THE THREE THINGS THAT MAKE THE LEVELS TRACTABLE

  (a) A level body contains its own evidence.  `hunt_level_refs.py` (GameDigging)
  decompresses a depot bundle, finds the body by name hash, and matches every
  8-byte window against that bundle's TOC - the `hunt_unit_textures.py` technique
  applied to `.level`.  `--twin` ranks resolved levels by Jaccard similarity of
  their reference sets; `--diff` prints exactly which references differ.  For one
  crypt target the diff was:

      only-target: props/d01_common/chains_wall_bend_2c_01;
                   props/d01_common/chains_wall_bend_4c_01;
                   props/d01_crypt/chains_var01; props/d01_crypt/shield_02
      only-twin  : (nothing)

  i.e. the *props* name the mod.  This is how `chains` and `shield` were found -
  tokens that occur in *no* resolved name, which is precisely why recombining
  resolved names could never guess them.  Mod vocabulary must be mined from the
  prop catalogue (`props/`, `gameobjects/`, `blocks/` leaf n-grams), not from
  names we already have.

  (b) Body size is nearly a function of the name.  `crypt_colosseum_02_mortar` is
  12,032 B and an unnamed target sits at 11,952 B; `room_crypt_floor_05_adventure`
  is 6,688 B and another sits at 6,720 B.  The exact-size neighbours are the
  structural twin - for one target the 4,880 B neighbours are
  `crypt_quilt_turn__*__02`, a different family than the reference-based twin
  (a `straight`) suggested.  `hunt_level_refs.py --guess` seeds from *both* and
  hashes the implied names; validated by recovering a known quilt name from a
  sibling seed.

  (c) The shipped Lua names the rooms.  `mine_source_names.py` walks
  `game-source/Gauntlet-SourceCode-LUA` and hashes every string plus every
  `<path = "...">`+key pair.  Findings:

    * the 266 quilt identifiers in `lua/dungeon/layouts/*.lua` all resolve to
      names we already have - 0 new.  The layouts list only the pieces the game
      actually uses; unused authored pieces are referenced nowhere, which is
      exactly the set we are chasing.
    * `lua/dungeon/layouts/` holds 68 files whose names are the floor/room
      identifiers: `crypt_floor_01..10`, `crypt_floor3_*`, `crypt_endless_01_slaves`,
      `caves_endless_04_ruins_mixed`, `lava_floor_colosseum_03_beam`, ...
    * the quilt grammar is confirmed in code, `quilt_builder.lua`:
      `name:match("^%w+_quilt_(%w+)__([^_]+)_([^_]+)_([^_]+)_([^_]+)__(.*)$")`
      - the four cell groups are `[^_]+` each, and the tail is free.
    * mods are 1-3 token *sequences*: `gold`, `gold_sand`, `sarcophagus_chain`,
      `bridge_fancy_02`.

43.3 RESULT

    modules/d01_crypt/room_crypt_floor3_03_west_wing
    modules/d01_crypt/room_crypt_floor3_05_south_wing

  Found by the family sweep (room x `floor3` x number x `_<wing>`), each verified
  independently: murmur64A(name) == the catalogue's name hash, and both are
  `modules/d01_crypt/resources` level rows.  16,676 -> 16,678 of 20,000 (83.39%),
  1,505 unique unresolved.

43.4 WHAT IS LEFT, PRECISELY

  The 101 remaining levels are not quilt-shaped, not `<word>_8c_8c_...`-shaped,
  and not any template reachable from their size twins with a 40 k mod list.
  Three candidate explanations, in order of likelihood:

    1. a *directory* we have never seen (every template so far inherited its
       directory from a resolved twin).  Cheap test: replace each template's
       directory with a list of every directory token seen anywhere.
    2. a *novel cell code* crossed with a mod - the pair space is observed codes
       plus one-character neighbourhoods; a code differing in two characters is
       not in it.  Test: profile-constrained pair enumeration (each shape's
       side-length profile, letters drawn from that shape's own groups) x a
       bounded mod list.
    3. a stem word outside the 400,000-word list.

  The tooling for all three now exists (`make_target_patterns.py` templates,
  `make_family_vocab.py`, `make_quilt_vocab.py --full`, `make_tile_vocab.py`).

==========================================================================
44. THE LEVEL GRAMMAR, ENUMERATED PROPERLY
==========================================================================

  Following the hint that there are ten levels per environment with the tenth as
  the boss floor, and that the wings and numbers line up, each level template was
  taken apart and enumerated from the game's own data rather than from the list
  of names we happen to hold.

44.1 ROOMS ARE `room_<env>_floor...`

  The resolved corpus shows every shape the room name takes:

    room_caves_floor_10_boss          floor_%f_boss          (the boss floor)
    room_lava_floor_10_boss_felix     floor_%f_boss_%m
    room_crypt_floor_06_boss
    room_crypt_floor_01_adventure     floor_%f_%m
    room_caves_floor_adventure_1      floor_%m_%k
    room_caves_floor_adventure_1_dark floor_%m_%k_%m
    room_lava_floor_adventure_2
    room_lava_floor3                  floor%f
    room_crypt_floor3_02_north_wing   floor%f_%k_%w_wing
    room_crypt_floor3_03_west_wing    (both found in §43)
    room_dummy / prefab_* / arena / colosseum

  `sweeps/room_sweep.sh` (`make_room_vocab.py`) crosses floor x sub-number x wing
  x mod with padded *and* unpadded numbers (floor_01 / floor01 / floor3), both
  orders (floor_01_adventure vs floor_adventure_1) and the wing grid.  It is
  cheap (a few 1e6) and returned 0: with the two wings already found in §43 the
  wing grid is complete, so the remaining levels are not rooms of this shape.

44.2 THE QUILT CELL CODE IS FULLY ENUMERABLE

  Verified over every resolved quilt (29/29 distinct groups, no exceptions), a
  cell group is always

    x^a  s^b  x^c        s in {e,i,o}, b >= 1        (or all x)

  - `x` solid, `e` open, `i` entrance, `o` exit - hence `xox`, `iix`, `eee`,
  `oox`, `xxi`, `xxxxx`, and never `exi`.  Each shape's side-length profiles are
  fixed too (blackbox (1,1,1,1); straight (1,1,1,1)/(1,2,1,2)/(2,1,2,1); turn
  (1,1,1,1)/(1,2,1,2); smallroom (1,1,1,1)/(2,2,2,2); mediumroom (3,2,3,2)/
  (3,3,3,3); largeroom (3,3,3,3); encounter (3,2,3,2)/(3,3,3,3); saferoom
  (3,2,3,2); entrance (2,2,2,2)/(3,2,3,2)/(3,5,3,5); exit (2,2,2,2)/(2,3,2,3)/
  (3,2,3,2)/(3,5,3,5)).

  Crossing the two gives the complete plausible code set - 2,171,139
  `<shape>__<cells>` pairs (`make_quilt_codes.py`), covering 43/44 of the codes we
  hold (the 44th is a genuine authoring typo in the data:
  `caves_quilt_largeroom__xox_xex__xix_xex__02_spider`, double underscore).

  Sweeps closing that space, all 0:
    * `qcode_sweep.sh` - the whole 2.17 M pairs x 19 numbers x 5 subdirs x each
      environment's mod vocabulary (the caves tier, 8.3e10 candidates, completed).
    * `qcode_shape_sweep.sh` - one shape at a time, so the 40 k prop-derived mod
      list fits (a shape has 256 - 846 k codes).
    * `mod_pattern_sweep.sh` (`make_mod_patterns.py`) - the harvest-derived mod
      vocabulary including the *glued* attach form (`__%n%m`).

  So the cell code was not the missing component either.

==========================================================================
45. THE DEPOT IS NOT STRING-FREE: HARVESTING RAW BUNDLE BODIES
==========================================================================

  The breakthrough.  Every snapshot carries `_names.txt` - a previous session's
  harvest of every ASCII string found in the *decompressed* bundle bodies plus
  the binaries, reproducible with:

    python3 tools/harvest_depot.py      # -> work/harvest/{raw,*_ids}.txt

  Plain `grep` reports the depot as containing no strings at all, because each
  resource body is a chain of zlib blocks - which is exactly why the depots had
  been written off as name-less.  Decompress the blocks and real resource names
  tumble out, as slash-joined runs of them:

    .../caves_quilt_straight__xx_e_xx_e__05_spider/caves_quilt_...__04_spider/...

45.1 WHAT THE HARVEST PROVED ABOUT THE NAMING

  * mods are frequently **glued straight onto the number**, with no separator:
    `crypt_quilt_straight__e_x_e_x__01chain`.  No `%n_%m` pattern can express
    that; the sweep has to try `%n%m`.
  * compound mods are **single tokens**: `goldchain`, `bridgechain`,
    `sarcophaguschain`, `spiketrapschain`, `spinnertraps`, `speartraps`,
    `crankbridge`, `fountainsandcenter`, `centerpiecewithwalkway`,
    `keydoorseast`, `statueeast` - recombining tokens we had seen cannot produce
    them.
  * tiles are far richer than assumed:
      `lavarock_16c_16c_uu_ug_uu_gu_01_encounter`   non-square, multi-char groups
      `lavarock_8c_16c_g_gu_g_uu_01_stairspass`     mixed cell widths
      `lavastruct_8c_8c_w_d_d_w_01_corridorturn`    a `lavastruct` rock
      `caverock_8c_8c_g_g_g_g_start01`              `_start01` / `_05_end` tails
      `lavarock_dungeon_tiles_master`               per-environment master lists
      `blackbox_2c_2c_01`
  * wings and floors are NOT in the harvest (`north_wing`, `floor3` -> 0 hits),
    so the remaining rooms cannot be read off it - they are unreferenced.

45.2 RESULT

  62 level names from the harvest, none of which 80 G candidates of pattern
  sweeping could reach, because every one of them violates a grammar assumption
  the sweeps encoded:

    crypt_quilt_straight__x_e_x_e__03_goldchain
    crypt_quilt_turn__e_x_x_e__01chain
    crypt_quilt_straight__xx_e_xx_e__04_goldchain_skeleton
    crypt_quilt_straight__e_x_e_x__02_spinnertraps
    crypt_quilt_straight__e_x_e_x__03_speartraps
    crypt_quilt_straight__x_e_x_e__02_sarcophaguschain
    crypt_quilt_straight__e_xx_e_xx__03_bridgechain_skeleton
    crypt_quilt_encounter__xxoxx_xxxi_xxixx_ixxx__01
    lava_quilt_mediumroom__xox_ox_xix_ix__02_fountains_demons
    lava_quilt_mediumroom__xox_xi_xix_xo__03_wide_demons
    lava_quilt_mediumroom__xox_ix_xix_ox__02_sides_mixed
    lava_quilt_mediumroom__xox_xo_xix_xi__04_centerpiecewithwalkway_mixed
    lava_quilt_mediumroom__xox_ex_xix_ex__03_statueeast_cultists
    lava_quilt_straight__o_xx_i_xx__01_crankbridge
    lavarock_8c_8c_g_g_g_g_03_hole / _02_rocks
    lavarock_16c_8c_gg_g_gu_g_01_smallstairs
    lavarock_16c_16c_uu_ug_uu_gu_01_encounter
    lavarock_8c_16c_g_gu_g_uu_01_stairspass
    lavastruct_8c_8c_d_d_w_d_01_corridor / _w_d_w_d_01_corridor
    lavastruct_8c_8c_w_d_d_w_01_corridorturn
    lavastruct_8c_8c_w_w_d_d_01
    lavastruct_16c_16c_ww_ww_ww_dw_01_end
    caverock_16c_16c_uu_gu_gu_ug_01_encounter
    caverock_8c_8c_g_g_g_g_end01 / _start01
    lavarock_dungeon_tiles_master / caves_dungeon_tiles_master
    lavastruct_dungeon_tiles_master
    ... (62 total)

  Pipeline: `harvest_depot.py` -> `mine_harvest_names.py` (whole lines, path
  expansion around each leaf) -> `mine_harvest_ids.py` (id -> every plausible
  package path).  Three tools, no GPU, seconds each.

45.3 SESSION RESULT

  16,676 -> 16,758 of 20,000 (83.79%); unresolved levels 103 -> 32; unique
  unresolved 1,505 -> 1,436.  The remaining 32 levels are not quilt-shaped (2.17 M
  legal codes x mods -> 0), not tile-shaped over every rock/dims/group/tail the
  harvest shows, not rooms, and not module-level (`d01_common`, `debug`, `menu`,
  `hub_room` -> 0).  The harvest does not contain them, so they are unreferenced
  authored content - the same wall as the two unnamed super-nova siblings in §40.

45.4 NEXT

  The harvest is the highest-value vein and is *not* level-specific: the same
  decompressed strings must contain texture/material/animation names, which is
  where ~1,100 of the remaining 1,436 hashes live.  `mine_harvest_any.py` is the
  starting point (it pairs each harvested leaf with the directory found in the
  same line); the missing piece is a bounded way to pair a leaf with a directory
  when the line carries none - the token-join heuristic (try the directories that
  share a token with the leaf) is the obvious next step.

==========================================================================
46. THE HARVEST, APPLIED TO EVERY TYPE
==========================================================================

  The harvest is not level-specific.  It holds complete paths for the other
  families too:

    characters/avatar_warrior/animations/axe_attack_light3_sweep
    base:colored_specular:diffuse_map:diffuse_uv_anim:one_bit_alpha
    base:DIFFUSE_MAP:MATERIAL_TINT_RGB:SILHOUETTE:SKINNED_3WEIGHTS:STATIC_LIGHTING
    effects_ui/hud_colosseum_wave_complete_impact

46.1 THREE MECHANICS

  * the length byte in front of every string is printable, and the strings are
    runs of names glued together, so both the leading and the trailing 1-2
    characters of every path-shaped substring have to be tried:

      ..._01chainqmodules/d01_crypt/quilts/base/crypt_quilt_..._01chainqmodules/...

    (`mine_harvest_paths.py`, `ROOT`/`CHAIN` patterns).  Matching is exact, so the
    extra variants can only add true hits, never false ones.
  * **hashing is case-insensitive** - `bundle_tools.murmur_hash64` lowercases
    (`key.lower()`), and so does the engine.  That is why a harvested uppercase
    shader chain and its lowercase twin both match the same hash; candidates are
    collapsed to the lowercase spelling, the form the rest of the corpus uses.
  * a resource name is `<directory><leaf>`, so the whole plausible space is one
    cracker pattern per directory crossed with a leaf list:
    `patterns_<type>.txt` = `<dir>%m`.  Directories come from the resolved names
    of that type *and* from the harvest's own path prefixes (15,322 of them -
    the game's real directory layout).

46.2 WHAT THAT PRODUCED

  `mine_harvest_paths.py` (full paths, ~1.4 M split variants):

    modules/d01_crypt/prefabs/door_walls/prefab_crypt_door_wall_4c
    modules/d01_crypt/prefabs/floor_details/prefab_crypt_floor_detail_raised_altar_02
    modules/d01_crypt/prefabs/gate_exit/prefab_crypt_gate_exit_01
    modules/d01_crypt/prefabs/spawners/prefab_crypt_spawner_sarcophagus_lit_01
    modules/d01_crypt/prefabs/special_rooms/prefab_crypt_special_rooms_mummy_king_01
    modules/d01_lava/prefabs/floor_details/prefab_lava_floor_detail_pit_square_01
    base:colored_specular:diffuse_map:self_illumination_map:self_illumination_multiplier_map:static_lighting
    shadow:deferred_decals_group_floor:deferred_decals_group_level:deferred_decals_group_prop:diffuse_map
    multilayer_mesh:diffuse_map:red_diffuse_map:green_diffuse_map:blue_diffuse_map:alpha_diffuse_map
    billboard:diffuse_map:external_rotation:pivot:soft_particles:tangent_locked:uv_animation:vertex_color
    characters/avatar_warrior/animations/axe_attack_light3_sweep
  -> the remaining levels were **prefabs** all along, in six subdirectories
    (door_walls, floor_details, gate_exit, spawners, special_rooms, wall_details),
    plus 16 shader_library chains, 1 animation, 3 materials.

  `sweeps/leaf_sweep.sh` (`make_leaf_patterns.py`) - directories x leaves:

    +64 textures   equippables_icons/relic_arcadian_hatchet, menu/colosseum/help_arrow_left,
                   cutscenes/outro/outro_image02, effects/cha_valkyrie_foot_slide_dust, ...
    +3  materials  effects_ui/hud_colosseum_wave_complete_impact, ...
    +5  from the harvest's own directory list
    +2  more once the leaf list was extended with number/suffix siblings

  The texture hits exposed the family pattern - one stem, a numbered set suffix
  (`equippables_icons/<item>_set01` ... `_set07`) - so the leaf list is extended
  with the other numbers of a set and the other map suffixes of a stem
  (`leaf_ext.txt`, 4.16 M).

46.3 TOTALS

  16,676 -> 16,898 of 20,000 (84.49%); unique unresolved 1,505 -> 1,335;
  levels 103 -> 15.  The remaining big blocks are texture 455, animation 345,
  shader_library 237, material 139, unit 61, shader 45, timpani_bank 37.

46.4 NEXT

  * animation/unit: the names are `<unit dir>/animation(s)/<clip>`.  The unit
    directories are known (157 resolved unit dirs), so those 345 clips are
    reaching for a clip vocabulary that is not in the harvest - the harvested
    Lua (ability/state names) is the obvious source, the same way
    `make_effect_vocab.py` mines ability names for effects.
  * texture: 455 left, mostly in directories the harvest never prints.  Pairing
    the harvested *stems* with the *resolved* map-suffix families in both
    directions (the way `derive_tex_siblings.py` does for names we hold) is the
    next step.

==========================================================================
47. SCALING THE HARVEST: DIRECTORIES, BUNDLES, AND CHAINS
==========================================================================

  With `name = <directory><leaf>` established, three further scalings pay off.

47.1 RE-RUN THE SIBLING DERIVATIONS

  Every new name is a new seed for the sibling rules, so the derivations have to
  be re-run after every batch: `tex_derive.sh` -> 0 (map suffixes of the new
  names are all present), `num_derive.sh` -> +1.

47.2 DIRECTORY x VOCABULARY (universal_sweep.sh, make_universal_vocab.py)

  The directories are almost all known (365 resolved, 47 of them animation dirs,
  157 unit dirs, 109 texture dirs), so what is missing is the *leaf vocabulary*.
  One tool now collects every vocabulary we have - the harvested leaves, their
  number/suffix siblings (`leaf_ext.txt`, 4.16 M), every string and identifier
  in the shipped Lua, and the leaves/tokens of the names we hold (125,503) - and
  crosses it with every resolved directory and with the harvest's own 15,322
  path prefixes, as one cracker pattern per directory.

47.3 BUNDLE-SCOPED DIRECTORIES (bundle_sweep.sh, make_bundle_targets.py)

  A resource's bundle constrains its directory much better than the global list:
  a texture in the `characters/mummy_risen` bundle is almost certainly
  `characters/mummy_risen/<leaf>`.  So the sweep runs *per bundle*: that bundle's
  own targets against the directories of the resolved names in the same bundle
  (51 bundles, 969 patterns, 3,101 targets - about 19 directories each).

47.4 SHADER CHAINS: ONE FLAG IN, OUT OR SWAPPED (make_chain_candidates.py)

  A shader_library name is `<technique>:<flag>:<flag>:...` with the flags
  alphabetically sorted (§41), so the legal neighbourhood of a chain is small:

    insert one vocabulary flag in its sorted position
    drop one flag
    swap one flag for another vocabulary flag (re-sorting)

  From the 1,541 chains we hold plus the 1,335 the harvest prints, with 118
  techniques and 255 flags, that is 2.48 M candidates.  It is also *self-feeding*
  - each round's new chains are new seeds - so `chain_sweep.sh` loops it: round 1
  **+47**, round 2 **+22**, then fixpoint.  **69 `shader_library` names in two
  rounds** (190 left -> 168), the best yield of the batch:

    base:deferred_decals_group_prop:diffuse_map:instanced:static_lighting
    base:diffuse_map:material_map:material_tint_rgb:one_bit_alpha:silhouette:silhouette_decals_group_level:skinned_3weights:static_lighting
    base:blend_normal_world_positive_z:deferred_decals_group_prop:diffuse_map:double_sided:one_bit_alpha:skinned_3weights

  The same candidates against the `shader` type -> 0.

47.5 PREFABS (prefab_sweep.sh)

  The last levels are prefabs in six subdirectories, with the shapes
  `prefab_<env>_door_wall_<Nc>`, `prefab_<env>_<kind>_detail_<mod>_<NN>[_16c]`,
  `prefab_<env>_spawner_<mod>_<NN>`, `prefab_<env>_special_rooms_<mod>_<NN>`,
  `prefab_<env>_gate_exit_<NN>` - swept over the mod vocabulary, the dimension
  suffixes (1c..32c) and 01..32 -> 0, so the 14 left are outside those shapes.

47.6 A TYPE'S LEAVES ARE THE GAME'S OWN VOCABULARY (sibling_sweep.sh)

  For the animation family the leaf is not arbitrary: clips are *shared between
  units on the same rig* (`idle`, `walk`, `axe_attack_heavy1_sweep` appear under
  many `characters/<unit>/animations/`), so the clip we are missing is usually a
  sibling or a variant of one we hold.  `make_type_sibling_vocab.py` crosses
  every leaf we hold for a type (plus number/suffix siblings, plus the harvested
  leaves) with every directory we hold for it (plus harvested directories whose
  path mentions the type): animation 15 M, unit 417 M, material 26 M, texture
  17.7 M, particles 1.4 M, shader 10 k candidates.

  Result: **+2 animations** (`animation` tier), everything else 0.

47.7 NEGATIVES, FOR THE RECORD

  * the 20 M-line snapshot harvests (`_names.txt`, the earlier session's
    suffix/case-mutated lists) run through `mine_harvest_paths.py`
    (`--raw`): **70.8 M candidates, 0**.  The *raw* strings are the useful part;
    the generated mutations add nothing.
  * bundle-scoped directories (`bundle_sweep.sh`, 51 bundles, ~19 directories
    each): 0.
  * the universal directory x vocabulary sweep (`universal_sweep.sh`): 0 in the
    resolved-directory tiers; its harvest-directory x extended-leaf tier is the
    slowest and was stopped.

47.8 SESSION RESULT (this block)

  16,898 -> 17,102 of 20,000 (85.51%); unique unresolved 1,333 -> 1,261.
  shader_library 237 -> 168, animation 345 -> 343, level 14.

  The remaining blocks - texture 453, animation 343, shader_library 168,
  material 139, unit 61, shader 45, timpani_bank 37, level 14, flow 1 - are now
  almost entirely resources whose *directory* and *leaf* both appear nowhere in
  the bundles, the Lua, or the names we hold: the same unreferenced-authored-
  content wall as §40 and §45.3, with `timpani_bank` still needing Arrowhead's
  Wwise metadata.

47.9 ALSO TRIED, ALSO 0

  Animation clips are named after the ability/state that plays them
  (`axe_attack_heavy1_sweep` beside the ability `axe_attack_heavy1`), and
  `lua/generated/animation_events.lua` carries the game's own 482 event names.
  Each name, with and without its `ability_` prefix, crossed with the clip
  suffixes the corpus shows (`_sweep`, `_right`, `_left`, `_back`, `_start`,
  `_end`, `_windup`, `_loop`, `_offset`, `_hit`, `_01`..`_05`) against the 113
  animation directories: 1.14 M candidates, **0**
  (`make_anim_vocab.py`).

==========================================================================
48. TEXTURES BY REFERENCE, AND THE ATLAS SUBDIRECTORY
==========================================================================

  A texture's name is not in the bundle, but its *referrers* are: material and
  unit bodies carry the hashes of the textures they draw.  `hunt_texture_refs.py`
  decompresses each bundle that holds unnamed textures, matches every 8-byte
  window of every resolved body against the unresolved set (`hunt_unit_textures`
  technique, vectorised with numpy at all 8 alignments), and writes
  `texture_refs.json`.

  **418 of the 453 unnamed textures turn out to be referenced**, by resources
  whose names we know:

    effects/                 398      effects_ui/           101
    gameobjects/spawners/    110      menu/                  74
    hud/                      65      equippables_icons/     53
    blocks/d01_caves/         37      equipment/{warrior,wizard,elf}/  36 each

48.1 WHAT THE EDGES ARE GOOD FOR

  Two things, one obvious and one not:

  * the referrer's directory is the texture's directory, and its name supplies
    the stem - which is what `gen_texture_candidates.py` does
    (`<dir><stem><suffix>` with the suffix vocabulary mined from the resolved
    textures).  It found 2 (`blocks/d01_caves/stairs/roundstairs_{diff,norm}`).
  * **the atlas subdirectory**.  Resolved icons live in a subdirectory named
    after their atlas:

      hud/controller_icons              (the atlas material/unit)
      hud/controller_icons/button_y     (its icon textures)
      hud/keyboardmouse_icons/lmb

  so a texture drawn by an atlas `D/L` is `D/L/<icon>` - and that directory
  appears in **no resolved name**, so every directory sweep in §46-47 was blind
  to it.  `make_atlas_patterns.py` turns each referring atlas into one pattern
  (`<atlas>/%m`, `<atlas>_%m`, `<atlas>/%m_%n` - 1,581 of them) and crossing them
  with the vocabularies found **32 names in one run**:

    hud/action_icons/...   (20)   hud/spells/fire_bolt, hud/spells/void_chain,
    hud/spells/...         (12)   hud/spells/ice_beam, hud/spells/storm_bomb, ...

  which is the single best texture yield so far, and it generalises: any atlas we
  can identify yields its icons.

48.2 THE EFFECTS CONVENTION (THE OTHER 398)

  Probing resolved materials with `hunt_texture_refs.py --pairs <bundle> --prefix
  effects/` shows what the `effects/*` edges mean - the textures are **generic**,
  shared across effects, and not derived from the referrer's name:

    effects/cha_pickup_skullcoin  [material]
        effects/textures/additive_blob
        effects/textures/additive_glow_spikey_02
        effects/textures/alpha_skullcoin
    effects/env_wall_burst        [material]
        effects/textures/alpha_droplet
        effects/textures/alpha_impact_splash_01
        effects/textures/alpha_smoke_01
        effects/textures/alpha_stone_pieces

  i.e. `<kind>_<descriptor...>[_<n>][_norm|_diff]`, kinds {alpha, additive, atlas,
  normal, stunned, hellfire, beam, glow}, descriptors mined from the 1,103
  resolved effect textures (smoke, fire, liquid, cloud, blob, glow, thin,
  sparkle, weaponslash, circular, shockwave, lightning, ...).  Since 398 of the
  unnamed textures are drawn by `effects/*` particles they are further members of
  that family, so `make_fx_texture_vocab.py` enumerates kind x descriptor[ x
  descriptor] x number x suffix (156,288 candidates) behind a single
  `effects/textures/%m` pattern -> **0**.  The descriptors are more idiosyncratic
  than a token recombination reaches (`glow_spikey`, `impact_splash`,
  `stone_pieces`, `candle_flame`), and 30.6 G candidates of the atlas patterns
  crossed with the 4.16 M extended leaves also added nothing beyond the 32 above.

48.3 WHERE THAT LEAVES TEXTURES

  By-reference coverage is now **418 of 453** textures (and 343 of 343 animations,
  104 of 139 materials, 61 of 61 units), which is what says the atlas-subdirectory
  rule matters: an icon's name is its atlas's directory plus the icon leaf
  (`hud/action_icons/<icon>`, `hud/spells/<spell>`), and that directory appears in
  no resolved name anywhere, so every directory sweep is blind to it.  The rule is
  proven - `make_atlas_patterns.py` supplies the patterns and it found +32 - and
  the recipes it unlocks are the ones this section goes on to mine.  `effects_ui/`
  turned out to have its own texture tree too (`map_caustics`, `map_gold_glitter`,
  `additive_glow_strip`, `additive_glow_ring`).

  What is left in the atlases is the part whose naming is neither
  referrer-derived nor a token recombination - most likely a hand-authored icon
  list, so the UI/flow Lua is where to look next (`mine_source_names.py` already
  walks it).

48.4 PER-ATLAS FAMILY RECOMBINATION (make_atlas_family.py)

  Each referring atlas is a directory, and the icons in it are *members of a
  family*, so the resolved leaves of that directory carry the whole grammar:

    menu/level_select/level_button_adventure[_completed|_silver|_locked|_over]
    menu/shop/shop_item_background_{normal,owned,equipped,over}
    menu/shop/shop_relic_tier_{1,2,3}
    menu/hero_coins/{warrior,elf,...}_{colored,over,standard}
    menu/icons/padlock_locked

  i.e. `<stem>[_<state>]`.  So for each of the 527 referring atlas directories:
  take the stems and state suffixes seen in it (plus the global state set and the
  environment/class/mode words), and enumerate the cross product
  (54,372 words x 1,054 patterns, 372 M candidates) -> **+1**, and a telling one:

    menu/icons/padlock_unlocked        (sibling of the padlock_locked found above)

  A hint worth recording because it did *not* pan out: `hud/relics` and
  `hud/talisman` are plausible UI atlas names, but this build has **zero** names
  under either - only `hud/relic_icons`, which references a single unnamed
  texture.  The relic art actually lives in `equippables_icons/relic_<item>`
  (all resolved) and `menu/shop/shop_relic_tier_{1,2,3}`.  Their candidate
  vocabularies (`gameobjects/relics/<item>`, `equipment/<class>/talisman01..03`)
  were swept anyway -> 0.

48.5 THE MACHINERY IS TYPE-GENERIC

  Nothing in the by-reference method is texture-specific, so
  `hunt_texture_refs.py --type T` writes `hashcracker/work/refs_<T>.json` and
  `gen_texture_candidates.py --type T` reads it: the same edge-driven candidate
  generation now covers material, animation and unit as well.

  What it found for textures, one more layer in:

    * referrer *parent* directories (and their `textures/` subdirectory) crossed
      with the universal vocabulary -> 3, in a convention we had not seen:
        effects_ui/textures/map_caustics
        effects_ui/textures/map_gold_glitter
        effects_ui/textures/additive_glow_strip
      i.e. `effects_ui/` has its own `textures/` tree with `map_*` / `additive_*`
      kinds - and it is self-feeding: regenerating the family from those names
      added `effects_ui/textures/additive_glow_ring`.
    * referrer directories x the 4.16 M extended leaves (5 G candidates) -> 0.
    * the whole `effects/textures/` number range `<kind>_<descriptor>_{01..99}`
      (418 k candidates) -> 0, so the missing effect textures are not simply
      further numbers of the ones we hold.
    * material candidates `<effect-name>_<phase>` over every unit/particle/material
      name we hold x 60 phase words (`marker`, `trail`, `windup`, `explode`, ...; the tails
      resolved materials actually use) -> 0.

48.6 WIDENING THE TWO PROVEN LEVERS

  Two levers were already proven and simply needed their ranges widened now that
  many more names are seeds:

  * **shader chains, two flags at a time.**  `make_chain_candidates.py` gained a
    two-flag insert (pairs that co-occur in a chain we hold, 4,000 of them), a
    two-flag drop and a technique swap (118 techniques): 5.08 M candidates ->
    +3, then fixpoint on a re-run.
  * **index siblings, 0-99 instead of 1-10** (`derive_number_siblings.py
    --range 0-99 [--pairs]`): 1.65 M candidates -> +2, then the two-index-run
    (`--pairs`) pass -> +1 more, and the fixpoint re-run reproduced a family the
    narrow range could never reach:

      characters/avatar_valkyrie/animations/character_select_pos03_var1
      characters/avatar_valkyrie/animations/character_select_pos03_var2
      characters/avatar_valkyrie/animations/character_select_pos03_var3

    i.e. `_varN` families run past N=1, which is exactly the kind of tail the
    1-10 default truncates.

  Both are cheap enough to re-run after every batch, which is now the standing
  procedure: merge, re-derive, re-crack, repeat.

48.7 RE-RUN THE HEAVY ENGINES TOO - THEY SELF-FEED

  The substitute engine (§42) is driven by the names we hold, so every batch of
  new names is new fuel.  Re-running it (`sweeps/subst_run.sh`) after this
  chapter's additions gave **+16** where the same engine had reported fixpoint
  before - and the finds show why: it completes *naming grids*.

    hud/spells/frost_bolt      hud/spells/fire_serpents
    hud/spells/frost_bomb      hud/spells/frost_serpents
    hud/spells/storm_serpents

  i.e. once `hud/spells/<element>_<form>` is partly known, the engine fills in
  the rest of the element x form grid.  Spread across types, which is typical of
  the engine: texture 416 -> 403 and level 14 -> 11 in the same pass.

  Standing procedure, now: merge, re-derive (index/tex siblings), re-run the
  substitute engine, re-crack the atlas families - *every* batch, because each
  one only pays off against the newest names.

==========================================================================
49. TWINS, AND THE VARIANT TOKENS THEY REVEALED
==========================================================================

  Almost everything left looks like a *derived* variant of a name we hold, and
  two fingerprints locate the name it derives from (`tools/twin_by_refs.py`):

  * **size + bundle** - a body size is nearly a function of the name (§43), so
    the resolved resources adjacent to an unnamed one *in the same bundle* are
    its family.  Needs only the catalogue; found a twin for 886 of 1,229 targets.
  * **reference graph** (`hunt_ref_graph.py`) - decompress each bundle once and
    match every 8-byte window of every *named* body against the named resources
    of the same bundle, giving 2,708 referrer sets.  A resource's referrer set is
    a near-unique fingerprint, so the twin is the resolved resource whose
    referrers overlap most.  Found a twin for 665-719 targets at similarity
    0.5-0.2 -> **0**, an honest negative.

  The size twins did land, and taught the shape of what is left:

    gameobjects/traps/trap_spikeplate_caves_diff   (twin trap_spikeplate_diff)
    gameobjects/traps/spikeplate_caves_1c_1c       (twin spikeplate_1c_1c)
    props/d01_common/torch_floor_blue_01           (twin torch_floor_01)

  i.e. **environment, colour and state variants** of known assets.  That generalises
  past the twins, so the same edit space is now applied to *every* name we hold -
  `<name>_<variant>`, `<variant>_<name>`, one token swapped, one token inserted,
  a token pair swapped, over 51 variant tokens (environments, colours, creature
  groups, states, sizes) - 2.46 M candidates, which found:

    blocks/d01_lava/railings/lavastruct_railing_broken_2c        (_broken)
    props/d01_common/torch_floor_blue_01_diff                    (a map of a
                                                                  name just found)

  The second is the engine feeding itself again (§48.7), so this is re-run after
  every batch like the others.

==========================================================================
50. CROSS-BUILD DIFFS: WHAT CHANGED BETWEEN SNAPSHOTS
==========================================================================

  The older snapshots share bundle ids with the current one (same package name),
  so a bundle that exists in both is the same package at two points in time and
  its TOC entries line up by name hash.  `hunt_build_diff.py` reports three
  things per shared bundle (49 of them between 2015-01 and 2015-08):

    old-only 1,706   names that were removed
    new-only 3,992   names that were added
    content moves  513   bodies that are byte-identical across builds but carry
                         a *different* name hash - i.e. renames

  of which 341 are clean 1:1 moves (many-to-many buckets are just resources that
  share a body, e.g. the placeholder effects that all three of `env_lightbeams_
  small_01`, `env_lightbeams_large_01` and `env_receptor_beams` point at).

50.1 NAMING THE OLD BUILD

  A hash depends only on the name, so a hash present in an old build but not in
  the current one is a name that was removed or renamed - and our corpus can
  never name it (every name we hold exists in the current build).  The old
  build's *own* harvest can: `hunt_old_names.py` matches that snapshot's
  `_names.txt` (17.7 M lines, 124.5 M split variants) against the old build's
  TOC and resolved **3,329 old names**, of which **476 were new to us**.

50.2 THE RENAME GRAMMAR, FROM 101 EXAMPLES

  Joining the three sets gives 101 rename examples (both sides named) and - because
  almost every renamed resource is one we had already resolved - only 3 targets.
  The examples are worth having anyway; they are the game's own vocabulary edits:

    characters/mummy_boss/mummy_boss_spec -> characters/boss_mummy/boss_mummy_spec
    equipment/elf/wep_bow01_diff          -> equipment/elf/weapon01_diff
    characters/morak_sword/morak_sword_norm -> characters/boss_morak_sword/boss_morak_sword_norm
    effects/wep_elf_arrow_explosive_trail -> effects/wep_elf_special_explosive_shot_trail

    token translations: bandit -> soldier (x7), wep -> cha (x5), arrow -> special (x4)

  Applied corpus-wide (`apply_renames.py`, 19,217 candidates) -> 0, and the 3
  targets -> 0: those renames are all resolved already.  The old names are not
  wasted though - used as bases for the variant/map edits -> **+1**, and a telling
  one: `effects/wep_elf_arrow_light_trail`, an *old-style* `wep_` name that still
  matches a resource in the current build.


50.3 WHAT THIS SAYS ABOUT THE REMAINDER
  The corpus is *newer* than either older build (the renames all run old -> new,
  and we hold the new side), so cross-build evidence cannot reach the 1,191 left:
  their resources were **added after 2015-08**, or renamed *within* the current
  line of builds, and neither older snapshot contains them at all.  Combined with
  the earlier negatives, the remaining blocks now look like content authored for
  the shipped build and never referenced by anything we can read:
  animation 340, texture 398, shader_library 165, material 135, unit 60,
  shader 45, timpani_bank 37 (needs Wwise metadata), level 11, flow 1.

==========================================================================
51. PROBING RESOURCES DIRECTLY (AND TWO MORE NEGATIVES)
==========================================================================

  `probe_resource.py` is the reusable form of a check that had been ad hoc all
  session: given a name it decompresses the owning bundle, finds the body, prints
  its printable strings and - matching every 8-byte window against *every* name we
  hold, not just the bundle's TOC - the resources it references.

51.1 STATE MACHINES DO NOT NAME THEIR CLIPS

  State machines are the natural place to look for the animation block (340):
  a `.state_machine` drives a character's clips, and 111 of them are resolved.
  They are per-unit and share the unit's name (`characters/mummy_priest/
  mummy_priest` is a unit, a bones, a material, a lua *and* a state_machine).

  Probing one: 520,903 bytes, 8,128 printable runs and not one is a word (the
  body is binary), and its 16 references are `flow/status_effect_*`, materials,
  `audio/enemy_mummy` and another state machine - **no animations at all**.
  So the animation names are not recorded there.  Negative.

51.2 THE SHIPPED LUA'S UNIT TABLES

  `unit_path_to_components.lua`, `units_with_settings.lua`,
  `spawners_unit_path.lua`, `unit_paths.lua`, `unit_paths_short_lookup.lua`,
  `settings_paths.lua`, `flow_events.lua` and `characters_unit_path.lua` are
  literal resource-path tables (2,089 names).  Their names are already held -
  which is why testing them as-is gave 0 - so the last useful thing is to use
  their *leaves* as bases: 925 leaves x 173 directories from the tables, plus
  token-drop and number/map-suffix edits, 2.0 M candidates -> **0**.  Negative.

51.3 WHY: A NAME MAPS TO SEVERAL RESOURCES

  One name hash covers every resource type recorded under it (a unit, its bones,
  its material, its state machine and its lua all share one hash, because the
  hash *is* the name).  So a name resolved anywhere resolves all of that name's
  rows - and it means the tables in the shipped Lua, the status-effect names and
  the unit paths we already hold are *exactly* the set of names the game used for
  those objects.  Anything still unresolved is therefore a name our sources have
  never printed, in any build.


  453 -> 421 unnamed.  418 of them are still referenced, so the edges remain the
  best lever: the atlas rule is proven (any atlas whose icons follow the
  `D/L/<icon>` convention yields them), and what is left is the icon/descriptor

==========================================================================
52. TOKEN COMBINATION: BUILDING LEAVES OUT OF THE GAME'S OWN WORDS
==========================================================================

  Every vocabulary sweep up to here used ONE slot per pattern (`<dir>%m`), so it
  could only ever propose a leaf we already held.  Combinations were only tried
  where they were cheap: `pack_sweep.sh` combines a pack's own tokens but only
  inside that one package, `make_guess_patterns.py` only for levels with a twin.

  Split every resolved name on `/` and `_` and the whole catalogue collapses to
  **1,958 tokens** (1,930 non-numeric; 784 distinct leaf-first tokens, 1,292
  distinct leaf-last).  That is small enough to cross three deep against all
  1,185 unresolved hashes at once (`tools/make_token_combo_vocab.py` +
  `sweeps/token_combo_sweep.sh`):

    free 2 tokens    %a_%b  %a/%b                                7.7 M
    free 3 tokens    %a_%b_%c  %a/%b_%c  %a_%b/%c  %a/%b/%c     30.0 G
    dir + 2 tokens   <known dir>%a_%b                             1.4 G
    dir + basename   <dir><its own name>_%a_%b                    0.9 G

  The free tiers carry no literal directory at all, which is the point: they can
  reach a name whose *directory* is itself a token combination.

52.1 MEASURED SPEED, AND WHY IT MATTERS

  Pure token combinations are the fastest thing this engine does - no branching,
  no per-candidate variation: **30,026,039,648 candidates in 16.1 s = 1.87 G/s**
  on the RX 6600 XT, ~2.5x the 755 M/s the mixed sweeps achieve.  A
  directory-anchored tier costs less per candidate (650 M/s) because 368
  patterns mean 368 pipeline runs.

  That changes what is affordable.  The deep tiers - `<dir>%a_%b_%c` (2.76 T) and
  `<dir><basename>_%a_%b_%c` with both extra slots from the tail vocabulary
  (1.2 T) - are 25-70 min, not the two hours estimated at 350 M/s, so they are
  worth running.

52.2 CONTROLS, AND WHAT THEY CAUGHT

  Six positive controls, one per tier, each proved *structurally* (some pattern
  can generate the name) and then *empirically* (mini vocabulary, real crack);
  every hash came back byte-exact.

    free2  flow/gib
    free3  characters/necromancer/necromancer
    dir2   characters/mummy_priest/mummy_priest
    base2  characters/orc_melee/orc_melee_weapons_diff
    dir3   props/d01_common/banner_torn_01
    base3  gameobjects/carry/carry_crypt_relic_mask

  Writing the controls caught a real bug: `--pattern '%a_%b_%c'` is a
  *standalone* template, not an append - so the deep phase would have generated
  `a_b_c` instead of `<dir><basename>_a_b_c`.  Hence
  `make_token_combo_vocab.py` now emits `patterns_dirbase3.txt` as its own file.

52.3 THE HAUL

  **+6**, in two shapes the single-slot sweeps could not reach - `dir x 2 tokens`
  (four of them) and `dir + the directory's own basename + 2 tokens` (the
  blackbox block, whose leaf restates its directory):

    blocks/d01_crypt/d01_crypt_blackbox_secret
    blocks/d01_lava/stairs/morak_platform
    characters/avatar_warrior/animations/default_falling
    characters/spider_queen/animation/spawn_rock
    characters/spider_warrior/animation/spawn_hanging
    menu/sparkle_overlay

  Note the recurring `spawn_*` / `default_*` clips and the `blackbox_secret`
  block: new *members of families we hold*, the same signature as section 49's
  twins, reached this time from the token side instead of by reference.

  The rest of the standing battery (`sweeps/battery.sh`), re-run on the same
  batch, was flat: map siblings +0, index siblings (wide range, paired) +0, twins
  by fingerprint 874 targets / 1.68 M candidates +0, the atlas families 239.6 M
  +0, the substitute engine 146 M per-type + 4.59 G cross-type +0.  The new
  *space* is what paid, which is the expected shape - edits and derivations of
  the names we hold were already exhausted, combinations of them were not.

52.4 A TRAP WORTH REPEATING

  The first run was killed mid-flight by a 30 s command timeout, and the second
  run then reported `+0` for a 32 G-candidate space in 20 s.  That looked like
  the "stale lock" failure the notes warn about, but measuring the tiers
  properly (30,026,039,648 in 16.1 s) showed the 20 s was *correct* and the +0
  genuine - the six had already been written by the killed run and folded in by
  the next round's merge.  Lesson: when a run is suspiciously fast, measure the
  tier directly rather than either trusting or discarding it.

==========================================================================
53. POSITIONAL TOKENS: FOUR, FIVE, SIX TOKENS DEEP
==========================================================================

53.1 WHAT THE DEEP TIERS OF SECTION 52 ACTUALLY PAID

  The overnight run finished the two tiers section 52 called affordable, and they
  were the best single result of the project so far:

    base3  <dir><basename>_%a_%b_%c   +4
    dir3   <dir>%a_%b_%c              +47
    ----- **+51** in one round (round 2 was a clean fixpoint)

  Overlay 6,359 -> 6,411.  Every one of the 51 has a *known* directory, ends in a
  token from the tail vocabulary, and 47 have **three**-token leaves - i.e. exactly
  the shape `dir x token^3` reaches and nothing else here did.

53.2 THE NEXT SHAPE, AND WHY IT IS NOT `token^4`

  Four- and five-token leaves are the bulk of what is left, but `dir x token^4` is
  368 x 1958^4 = 5.4e15 - out of reach.  The way in is that a leaf's token is not
  drawn uniformly: the *position* has its own vocabulary.  Measured over the
  resolved leaves:

    len 4   |pos| = 131  245  334  283            -> 1.1e12 x 368 dirs ~ 10 min
    len 5   |pos| =  59   80  126  170  144       -> 5.4e12          ~ 48 min
    len 6   |pos| =  16   20   29   34   38   36  -> 0.16e12         ~  2 min
    len 7   |pos| =   4    6    7    6    8   10   9 -> 0.0003e12    ~  0 s

  so the tier that paid for three tokens extends to four, five, six and seven for
  a few GPU-hours.  `make_token_position_vocab.py` builds, per length: the
  positional lists, the plain tier (`<dir>` + one token per position), the
  basename tier (`<dir><basename>_` + positions 1..L-1), a `postail` variant whose
  last slot is the whole 1,300-token tail vocabulary instead of the position's
  own, and a `wide` variant drawing every slot from a frequency-ranked list - the
  guard against a token that is plausible in a position but has never been seen
  there.  It also emits `tiers.tsv`, a manifest the sweep walks cheapest-first,
  and the sweep caps a tier by size (default 7e12, which drops `postail5` at
  48e12).

  This is genuinely new space: the substitute engine does positional shapes
  inside one package and a three-slot cross-type pass, but nothing has crossed
  four *corpus-wide positional* slots.

53.3 CONTROLS, AND A CHECKER THAT HAD TO BE FIXED TWICE

  `tools/check_token_tiers.py` turns each tier's first matching pattern into a
  regex with one group per slot, finds a **resolved** name that matches *and*
  whose token at each position really is in that position's list, then runs the
  real crack against that name's hash with one-word lists.  All 13 tiers within
  the cap reproduced their own control byte-exact.

  The checker itself failed twice before passing, both times because its *search*
  was too narrow, not because of a tier: it first tried only the pattern file's
  first line (one specific directory), then its directory index could not reach a
  pattern that opens with a slot (the free-form tier).  Worth recording: a
  control that reports FAIL is a claim about the checker as much as the tier.

53.4 THE HAUL: +29, AND THEY ARE ALL FAMILIES

  Round 1 of the positional sweep returned **+29** (round 2 a fixpoint), overlay
  6,411 -> 6,440, catalogue 17,334 -> 17,418 of 20,000 (87.09%).  Per tier:

    pos4 +18   postail4 +3   pos5 +5   postail6 +2   wide4 +1
    (every basename tier and every free tier: +0)

  Shape confirms the design.  Of the 29, **22 have four-token leaves**, 5 have
  five and 2 have six; **all 29 end in a token from the tail vocabulary**, and
  **none** begins with its directory's basename - which is exactly why the
  basename tiers stayed at zero while the plain and tail-anchored ones paid.
  By type: texture 14, material 10, unit 3, animation 1, level 1.

  The more useful observation is that **18 of the 29 are members of named
  families**, i.e. the engine did what section 48.7 described - it completed
  naming grids rather than finding isolated names:

    menu/level_select/flow_background_special_01 .. 06        (a run of six)
    gameobjects/spawners/spawner_{mummy,orc,grunt,skeleton,cultist}_crystal_diff
    effects_ui/achievement_unlocked_background_{bronze,silver,gold}
    blocks/d01_lava/floors/lavastruct_floor_border_01_{convex,concave}
    effects/wep_elf_special_root_bomb_{pulse,field}

  plus `effects/textures/alpha_floor_dust_trail`,
  `effects/cha_necromagus_coffin_burst`, `decals/decal_crypt_sand_01`,
  `blocks/d01_caves/caves_water_01_norm`,
  `blocks/d01_lava/borders/lavastruct_border_stairs_end`,
  `gameobjects/spawners/spawner_damage_effect_energy`,
  `modules/d01_lava/prefabs/prefab_lava_pit_2c_2c`,
  `characters/avatar_valkyrie/animations/spear_attack_heavy_impact`,
  `effects/wep_wizard_frost_serpents_charge`,
  `effects/cha_necromagus_skeleton_light_destroy`,
  `effects/obj_morak_trap_spikes`,
  `effects/wep_elf_special_root_bomb_pulse` / `_field`.

53.5 RATE OF THE ANCHORED TIERS

  Round 1 was ~6.4 h of wall clock for 29.7e12 candidates, i.e. **~1.3 G/s**
  effective - slower than the 1.87 G/s of the free-form tiers, because a tier
  with 368 `--pattern`s pays the pipeline restart 368 times.  Worth pricing in:
  the `wide5` tier (9.19e12) is ~2 h, not the 82 min a pure-combination rate
  suggests.  `wide5` was the one tier the 7e12 default cap skipped, and it is the
  only shape in this family still untested; `postail5` (48e12, ~10 h) remains
  over the cap deliberately.

==========================================================================
54. FAMILY COMPLETION, AND A CORPUS-WIDE STATE-SIBLING NEGATIVE
==========================================================================

54.1 THE ACHIEVEMENT TRIPLET IS COMPLETE

  The positional run closed a family that had been found half-named - the
  `_bronze` and `_gold` members were recovered, and the obvious missing member was
  recovered in the same pass:

    effects_ui/achievement_unlocked_background_bronze
    effects_ui/achievement_unlocked_background_silver
    effects_ui/achievement_unlocked_background_gold
    effects_ui/achievement_unlocked_text_glow

  Pressing on it - 4,878 hand-built variants (13 metals x 9 states x 14 subdirs,
  with and without `_01`/`_02`, and the reverse orders) - returned **0**, so the
  family really is the triplet.  The game's own convention nearby corroborates the
  metals vocabulary: `achievement_icons/frame_{basic,bronze,silver,gold}` uses
  the same three plus a generic.  Recorded as closed.

  The atlas around it is complete too, which is worth spelling out because the
  counts look suspicious: `achievement_icons/` holds **125** textures - 60
  `<icon>` / `<icon>_locked` pairs, the atlas root
  `achievement_icons/achievement_icons`, and four `frame_*` icons.  Every
  `_locked` icon has its plain partner, and the only plain entries without one are
  the atlas root and the four frames, which have no locked state by design.  Their
  `_locked` / `_unlocked` / `_norm` / `_diff` / metal forms were all tested anyway
  (35 hashes): none is an unresolved target.

54.2 THE SAME INSTINCT, CORPUS-WIDE: 689,073 FORMS, 0

  The general form of that guess is a *state sibling*: take every name we hold and
  (a) drop its last token, (b) swap the last token for 43 state/index tokens
  (`locked`, `unlocked`, `norm`, `diff`, `spec`, `over`, `off`, `on`, `empty`,
  `full`, `owned`, `equipped`, `base`, `complete`, `bronze`, `silver`, `gold`,
  `01`..`03`, ...), (c) append one, (d) prepend one - then test each against the
  unresolved hashes.  689,073 forms, **0 hits**.

  Expected, and worth stating: `hashcracker mutate` already does exactly this
  inside each name's own directory (suffix swap, token insert/append/prepend/
  replace, numeric edits) and has been run to fixpoint, so that space is owned and
  closed.  The shapes that keep paying are the *positional* tiers of section 53;
  the shapes that do not are the ones `mutate`, the map/number derivations and the
  atlas families already cover.

==========================================================================
55. THE DIRECTORIES THAT ARE NOT IN OUR DIR SET, AND WHAT CLOSED
==========================================================================

55.1 A DIRECTORY IS INVISIBLE UNTIL ONE OF ITS RESOURCES IS NAMED

  Every token sweep so far used the 368 directories that appear in *resolved*
  names.  But a directory only enters that set once one of its resources is
  named, so a directory whose resources are **all** still unresolved is invisible
  - and stays invisible, because the sweeps that could name those resources are
  exactly the ones that would have needed the directory.

  The depot harvest prints those directories (`data/vocab/leaves/
  dirs_harvested.txt`, 15,322 of them).  Most are glue artefact, because the
  harvest's runs have no delimiters - `acharacters/avatar_elf/animations/` is `a`
  + a real path - but anchoring each harvested string on a known dir and taking
  the suffix cleans them, and artefacts that survive cost only GPU time: a hit
  means the directory was real.  That yields **213 directories we have never
  tested** (`tools/make_harvest_dir_vocab.py`), e.g.

    characters/dragon/abilities/        characters/grunt_wretch/animations/
    characters/grunt_brute/animations/  characters/ghost_mother/animations/
    characters/avatar_elf/elf_itemsets/set01/  blocks/d01_crypt/corridor/

  Four tiers (`sweeps/harvest_dir_sweep.sh`): `<dir>%m`, `<dir>%a_%b`,
  `<dir>%a_%b_%c` with the last from the tail vocabulary, and the positional four.
  Two controls pass (a known dir with a known 2-token leaf and a known 3-token
  leaf ending in `scepter`, both found byte-exact).

55.2 MOST OF IT IS CONTENT THAT WAS CUT BEFORE RELEASE

  Worth recording, because it explains a zero: `characters/dragon/dragon` hashes
  to **no catalogue row at all** - it is not a resource in the shipped build - and
  `characters/dragon/` has zero resolved names.  Crucially there is no way for it
  to exist as a hash *without* being a row, so this is proof rather than
  inference.  The harvest's extra directories are largely cut content
  (dragon, hydra, grunt_wretch, grunt_brute, ghost_mother, lake_dweller, larva,
  spider_spitter, illusion_warrior).

  So the two sets are different things and should not be confused: our 1,101
  unresolved are **shipped** rows whose names we lack, while the harvest's extra
  directory space is largely content that never shipped - it cannot name our
  targets however good the vocabulary.  This is the first hard evidence for the
  "authored content we cannot read" conclusion of section 50.3.

55.3 WHAT CLOSED THIS ROUND

  * **audio / timpani_bank** - 11,160 hand-built candidates over the audio
    vocabulary (creatures, classes, environments, music words, both separator
    orders, +_01 variants): 0.  The 37 unresolved banks sit in three bundles of
    ten plus five hero bundles holding exactly one resolved
    `audio/heroes_<class>` each, and nothing in that shape hits.
  * **the Stingray `m_<name>` material convention** - both directions over 865
    particles + 1,540 materials: 0.  (Those 1,540 material rows are mostly the
    *same names* as particles, confirming section 48's effect = particle +
    material pair.)
  * **shader chains** - the grammar is settled: `<technique>[:<flag>]*` with
    flags **`:`-separated and alphabetically sorted**, underscores living inside a
    flag name, over 39 techniques and 100 flags.  Sorted means *combinations*,
    not permutations, so the whole space is C(100,k): k<=3 (6.5 M) 0, and k<=4
    (159.4 M in 801 s) 0.  `tools/chain_combo_hunt.py` runs it on the CPU, which
    is why it could run while a GPU sweep held the lock.
  * **new tokens from the harvest** - 154 tokens the resolved names lack, of which
    34 are candidate units, crossed with every known convention: 71,089 -> 0.

55.4 A SILENT +0 THAT WAS NOT A SEARCH

  `wide5` had **never actually run**.  The tier references `top40.txt`,
  `top60.txt` and `top80.txt`, and `make_token_position_vocab.py` only ever wrote
  top50/100/150/200/300/500 - so the crack exited with "no such file", and the
  driver piped stderr into the `new matches` grep and logged a clean `+0` in 12 s
  for a 9.19e12 space.  Twelve seconds is the tell: 9.19e12 cannot finish in 12 s
  at 1.3 G/s, so a suspiciously fast tier had to be measured directly (section
  52.4 says the same thing the other way round, where 20 s turned out to be
  genuine).

  Fixed on both sides: the lists are generated, and `token_combo_pos.sh` and
  `harvest_dir_sweep.sh` now capture each tier's output, log a loud `!!` line with
  the error, and **abort** rather than reporting a fixpoint when a tier failed.
  A sweep that can only say "+0" is not evidence of anything.

==========================================================================
56. MOVING THE WORK TO ANOTHER MACHINE
==========================================================================

  Porting this comes down to two questions, and each has a tool.

56.1 WHAT STATE DOES NOT TRAVEL IN GIT

  The repo is the vehicle: every script, the docs, the catalogue
  (`04_bundle_resources.csv`), the recovery overlay and `matches.tsv` are tracked,
  so a clone carries the whole pipeline.  What does not travel is the gitignored
  state, and almost all of it regenerates in seconds from the tracked catalogue -
  the big generated vocabularies (`work/chains` 931 MB, `work/audio` 538 MB,
  `work/level` 523 MB, `work/variants*.txt` 411 MB, `data/vocab/{qcodes,lvl,leaves}`
  213 MB) are rebuilt by the sweeps themselves every round.

  Four things cannot be regenerated cheaply, because they come from *big* inputs:
  the depot harvest (`work/harvest2/raw.txt`, 14 MB - input to every leaf, dir and
  universal vocabulary, and it needs the depot), the per-type reference sets and
  the referrer graph (from the bundle bodies), the old-build name/rename files
  (from the older snapshots), and the shipped Lua tree (`game-source/`, 7.5 MB).
  `tools/make_transfer_bundle.sh` packs those plus the portable release binary and
  the tier manifests: **11 MB**, against 2.5 GB of `work/` and 6.5 GB of snapshots.
  `--with-snapshots` adds the current snapshot for harvest/reference work.

56.2 WILL THE GPU PATH WORK ON A DIFFERENT CARD

  `src/gpu.rs` requests `wgpu::Backends::VULKAN` **explicitly** and requires
  `wgpu::Features::SHADER_INT64` (MurmurHash64A needs 64-bit multiplies).  Both are
  available from NVIDIA's Vulkan driver, so a port should be uneventful - but the
  failure mode would otherwise be discovered after a five-hour sweep, so
  `tools/gpu_smoke_test.sh` answers it in five seconds: it generates a *known* name
  from two one-word lists and requires the engine to find it with `--gpu`, which
  means the adapter exists, advertises SHADER_INT64, the pattern plumbing works and
  the GPU's reported index re-hashes to the same name on the CPU.

    control characters/mummy_priest/mummy_priest -> 01322d9d265d043f  PASS

  If it fails it is the driver, not the code: a missing or mis-selected Vulkan
  ICD/loader (`vulkan-tools`, `mesa-vulkan-drivers`, or the vendor driver).
  Dropping `--gpu` always still works, 20-50x slower, and `tools/hc.sh` serialises
  it either way.

56.3 SIZING A RUN ON A NEW CARD

  Measured here (RX 6600 XT): 1.87 G/s on free-form token combinations, ~1.3 G/s on
  directory-anchored tiers (368 `--pattern`s means 368 pipeline restarts), 350-750
  M/s on the mixed sweeps.  Each generator prints a candidate count and a time
  estimate per tier, so a new machine only needs its own speed number to plan.

  And a faster card is worth more than a faster repeat: the one tier in the
  positional family we have never been able to afford is `postail5` - the 5-token
  tier with the last slot drawn from the whole 1,300-token tail vocabulary instead
  of the position's own 144 - at 48e12, about ten hours here.  Its narrowed sibling
  `postail5top` (11e12) exists precisely because of that budget, and its 6-token
  counterpart `postail6` paid +2 on the last run.  On a card two or three times
  faster, `postail5` becomes an overnight run rather than a day.

==========================================================================
57. THE LAST STRUCTURAL HOPE, SIX TARGETED HUNTS, AND A CORRECTION
==========================================================================

57.1 THE SHIPPED DATABASE HOLDS NO NAMES

  `contents/bundle_database.data` (756 KB, the file the game itself loads) was the
  last place a readable name table could have been hiding.  It is not: its strings
  are bundle ids and their `.stream` markers (`0164ad48615ba9ed`,
  `0164ad48615ba9ed.stream`, repeated per entry) and nothing else.  Probed with
  five names we know exist - `mummy_priest`, `bombardier`, `mummy_priest_scepter`,
  `achievement_icons`, `spawner_daemon` - it returns **0 hits for every one**.

  So the database maps packages to bundle ids and holds resource *hashes*; the
  names are not in it.  That closes the structural question: there is no readable
  name table anywhere in the shipped data.

57.2 SIX TARGETED HUNTS, ALL ZERO

  Driven by the twin/referrer evidence of the previous round, each cheap and each
  aimed at a name family whose shape we could see:

    collision geometry     <resolved block/prop> + _collision/_collisionbox/...   93,926  -> 0
    achievement icons      every id/string/identifier in the achievement UI Lua  8,324  -> 0
    cross-character clips  every clip of the other 44 animation dirs + direction,
                           angle and spelling swaps                             26,016  -> 0
    clip grammar           weapon x verb x direction x turn-angle x offset, over
                           all 45 character animation dirs                   3,519,165  -> 0
    icon atlases           every equipment stem x equippables_icons/shop/hud
                           prefix and _set01..07                           128,837  -> 0

  (plus, in the same round: audio vocabulary 11,160 -> 0, `m_<name>` materials -> 0,
  shader flag combinations k<=4 159.4 M -> 0.)

  The clip-grammar failure is the informative one: the unresolved clips sit 0-6
  bytes from resolved clips of the same kind, so the grammar is right, but the
  *words* are not in it - `die_decapitated` on the elf versus `death_decapitated`
  on the necromagus and valkyrie, `legs_turn_left30`, `idle_squat_right`,
  `blend`-style ability names like `chainlightning` and `shroudshot`.  The game is
  internally inconsistent, and its ability vocabulary is not in the Lua we hold.

57.3 CORRECTION: SIZE IS NOT DISCRIMINATING FOR TEXTURES

  Section 55's twin reasoning leaned on body size, and for most types that holds -
  but not for textures.  **124 `achievement_icons/`, 90 `equippables_icons/`, 89
  `effects/textures/`, 27 `hud/action_icons/` and 21 `hud/spells/` all sit at
  exactly 43,836 bytes**, because an icon's size follows its resolution.  So "the
  80 textures that are size-adjacent to achievement icons" was weaker evidence
  than it looked, and the 62%-size-identical figure for textures should be read as
  "same resolution class", not "same family".

  What *is* discriminating: the **referrer**.  A texture's referrer is the atlas
  material, and it names the atlas outright - 16 of those unresolved are referenced
  by `equippables_icons/equipment`, 9 by `equippables_icons/shop_action_icons`, 9
  by `menu/colosseum/arenas`, 8 by `hud/action_icons`, 6 by
  `achievement_icons/achievement_icons`.  Atlas membership is therefore knowable;
  it is the icon *word* that is not.

57.4 WHAT THE ATLASES DO TELL US

  Reading `menu/how_to_play/` (38 resolved icons) exposes the game's own
  per-class ability vocabulary, which is exactly what the missing ability icons
  need:

    <class>_<category>, where the categories DIFFER per class:
    elf/necromagus/valkyrie: defensive heavy light potion relic special
    wizard:                  cast conjure fire ice lightning potion relic
    warrior:                 potion relic rush slash smash special
    misc:                    explosives food gold keys orbs potions

  So the game names abilities with class-specific words (`rush`, `slash`, `smash`
  for the warrior; `conjure`, `ice`, `lightning` for the wizard), and the icons
  that are missing are the ones whose ability word we have never seen.

57.5 THE LAST TIER IS RUNNING

  `postail5` - the five-token positional tier with its last slot drawn from the
  whole 1,300-token tail vocabulary rather than the position's own 144 - is the
  only tier of the family never to have run.  Widening the last slot has a track
  record (postail4 +3, postail6 +2, pos5 +5) while the narrowed middle ground
  postail5top was 0, so this is the honest last bet: **51.5e12 candidates, about
  eleven hours** on the RX 6600 XT, or a fraction of that on a faster card.

==========================================================================
58. THE ARTIFACT SWEEP IS COMPLETE; WHAT IS LEFT IS THROUGHPUT
==========================================================================

58.1 EVERY READABLE ARTIFACT CLASS IS NOW MINED

  Going through the install by class, rather than by hypothesis:

  * **the shipped source tree** (`Gauntlet-Source-DO_NOT_EDIT/`, 927 `.lua`, 7.5 MB)
    is **byte-identical** to the Lua tree already being mined (`cmp` on samples,
    same file count) - no new vocabulary there.
  * **the binaries** (`gauntlet.exe` 3.9 MB, `elias.dll` 262 KB - Bitsquid's UI
    system) contain **no resource names**: five known-existing names (`mummy_priest`,
    `bombardier`, `achievement_icons`, `spawner_daemon`, `unit_path`) return 0 hits
    each; the only hit at all is the *type* name `shader_library`, twice.  The
    executables are a thin launcher; the game is data.
  * **the `.stream` companions** to every bundle are compressed/opaque - not one
    printable string, and no audio names (the 37 `timpani_bank` stay source-limited).
  * **the community mod area** (`Other/`) holds mod sources, a script template and
    debug logs: 215 resource-shaped paths, all of them names we already hold.
  * **the database** holds no names at all (57.1).

58.2 A SAME-ENGINE DICTIONARY FROM ANOTHER GAME DOES NOT TRANSFER

  `reference/filediver/` (BSD, a Helldivers 2 / Bitsquid tool) ships a community
  hash database: 15,671 cracked names, 109,510 path strings, 86,486 wwise stream
  names, 33,802 thin hashes, and real wordlists (10,052 + 224 words).  Helldivers 2
  runs the **same engine** and the same MurmurHash64A over the same lowercased
  names, so engine vocabulary *should* transfer.

  It does not: **0 exact hits from 154,309 names**, and **0 matches from
  122,120,970** directory-anchored candidates (335,564 words x 365 directories).
  That is a precise result: what we are missing is **Gauntlet-specific words**, not
  engine vocabulary - and those exist only as hashes.

58.3 SO THE LEVER LEFT IS THROUGHPUT, NOT VOCABULARY

  The same repository points at a **hashcat kernel for this exact hash**
  (`github.com/ryanjsims/hashcat`, branch `murmur64a`) and ships its own
  `hash-tool` - "a simple tool for calculating and cracking murmur64a hashes",
  crack mode `-c -d _ -w <wordlist> <hash_in_little_endian>`.  The community built
  the tooling; we were hand-rolling it.

  `tools/dump_hashcat_targets.py` emits our 1,096 unresolved hashes in hashcat's
  little-endian form (and the big-endian form our engine reads), so the big
  mask/combinator spaces become affordable on a card that is 10-30x the RX 6600 XT:
  `postail5` in minutes rather than eleven hours, and the six-token spaces after it.

58.4 LICENSING AND SAFETY

  Audited rather than assumed: the engine is our own Rust with MIT/Apache-2.0 deps
  (wgpu, rayon, pollster, bytemuck, anyhow, aho-corasick) pinned and built from the
  local cargo cache, offline; the tools and sweeps are ours; python/numpy are
  PSF/BSD; `reference/filediver` is BSD and is used only for its **data**
  (wordlists), never executed.  The one closed component is the **NVIDIA driver**,
  which is unavoidable on that hardware and sees nothing but game resource names.
  hashcat itself is MIT, but the murmur64a support is a **fork branch**, so: build
  it from source and read the diff, or skip it - our engine already works.

58.5 RUNNING IT ON THE OTHER MACHINE: `REMOTE-GPU.md`

  The GPU box needs the driver, rust and the archive.  The engine's compute path
  creates no window or surface, so **headless Vulkan over SSH works with no X
  server**; `tools/gpu_smoke_test.sh` is the gate that proves it in five seconds
  (with `xvfb-run` and the CPU path as fallbacks).  The workflow - key-based SSH,
  resumable `rsync`, **`tmux` so a dropped connection cannot kill a ten-hour
  sweep**, bring `matches.tsv` back and merge - is written up in
  `hashcracker/REMOTE-GPU.md`.

==========================================================================
59. THE CATALOGUE IS HASH-KEYED; THE INDEXES MAKE IT READABLE
==========================================================================

59.1 WHY A SECOND VIEW EXISTS

  `04_bundle_resources.csv` is keyed by hash, because that is the only key the
  game uses.  That is the right shape for the sweeps and the wrong shape for
  reading, so `make_name_index.py` renders the same table the other way round,
  from the catalogue plus `resource_name_overlay.json` alone - no game copy and no
  run state needed:

      bundle_manifest/06_names_by_name.txt   every recovered name, A-Z by name
      bundle_manifest/07_names_by_type.txt   the same, grouped by resource type

  Both are column-aligned for reading; `--tsv` emits tab-separated rows instead,
  for grep/cut/sort.  The name column is sized to the **90th percentile** of name
  lengths, not to the longest name: a single 150-character shader chain would
  otherwise pad most of the file into blankness.  Names longer than the column
  simply push their hash to the right instead of aligning.  The numbers 05 and 06
  were already taken by `list_game_bundles.py` (`05_summary.txt`), hence 06 and 07.

  A name can carry several types - a unit, its bones, its material and its lua
  share a hash, because the hash *is* the name - so the type column is a set,
  written `a+b+c`.

59.2 WHERE THE REMAINING 1,096 SIT

  Counting *rows* (a hash can appear in several bundles, so 2,565 rows are
  unresolved but only 1,096 are distinct hashes):

      texture          951      unit              70
      animation        566      shader            69
      shader_library   407      timpani_bank      37
      material         261      level             12
      particles        188      flow               4

  By package, the largest single concentration is `resource_packages/all_effects`
  (203 rows - the effects bundle), then `resource_packages/menu` (171), and the
  three `core/rendering/*` bundles, which between them hold all 407 shader_library
  rows.  The 70 unresolved `unit` rows are the one cluster that is character-shaped
  and still open (section 60.5), and no deprecated character name has a row in the
  shipped build at all (section 60.2).

==========================================================================
60. HUNTING A DEPRECATED UNIT: `gator_brute` IS `boss_orox`
==========================================================================

60.1 THE QUESTION, AND WHY THE CATALOGUE CANNOT ANSWER IT

  `characters/gator_brute/...` appears in the depot strings, but no gator unit has
  a row in the shipped catalogue - and neither do the six `lizard_*` units those
  strings mention.  A name that is not in the build has no row, so the catalogue is
  silent by construction.  Two other sources can answer: the older snapshots (which
  do name resources) and content identity (bytes do not care what a resource is
  called).

60.2 THE ANSWER: IT WAS RENAMED, NOT CUT

  `hunt_old_names.py --snap 2015-01` mines the 2015-01 build's own strings against
  that build's own hashes (3,102 names recovered, seven of them `gator_brute`).
  `hunt_build_diff.py --old 2015-01 --new 2015-08 --dump-renames` pairs resources
  whose **bytes are identical while their hashes differ** - which can only mean the
  name changed.  All seven `gator_brute` resources appear, one to one:

      characters/gator_brute/gator_brute                   -> characters/boss_orox/boss_orox
      characters/gator_brute/gator_brute_diff              -> characters/boss_orox/boss_orox_diff
      characters/gator_brute/gator_brute_norm              -> characters/boss_orox/boss_orox_norm
      characters/gator_brute/gator_brute_spec              -> characters/boss_orox/boss_orox_spec
      characters/gator_brute/beastoforox_fallingrock       -> characters/boss_orox/abilities/fallingrock
      characters/gator_brute/beastoforox_fallingrock_tell  -> characters/boss_orox/abilities/fallingrock_tell
      characters/gator_brute/beastoforox_leap_marker       -> characters/boss_orox/abilities/leap_marker

  The old names say it themselves: the effect leaves were already called
  `beastoforox_*` - "*beast of Orox*" - before the rename, so the vfx team's
  internal name for the creature is the one that survived into the shipped build.
  "Gator Brute" was a working title; the boss shipped as **Orox**.  The window is
  narrow and certain: the same seven names are recovered from 2014-10-10 as well, so
  the unit was live in both snapshots and renamed between 2015-01-21 and 2015-08-11.
  Re-running the same diff reproduces the same 341 moves, and a direct hash check
  confirms that neither the 7 old hashes nor 169 spelled-out variants of them has a
  row in the shipped build.

60.3 WHY NO SWEEP COULD HAVE FOUND IT

  Every sweep substitutes one *token* and keeps the shape.  This rename changed the
  shape: a unit-prefixed leaf (`beastoforox_fallingrock`) became a directory
  (`abilities/fallingrock`), and the unit directory itself changed name.  A
  per-component dictionary over all 3,102 old names therefore returns nothing, and
  more vocabulary cannot close that gap.  Only content identity can.

60.4 THE LIZARDS: CUT BEFORE 2014, AND THE HARVEST IS NOT A LIVENESS TEST

  The lizard units in the depot strings - `lizard_guardian`, `lizard_warlord`,
  `lizard_melee`, `lizard_heavy`, `lizard_drone`, `lizard_archer` - are live in
  **none** of the three snapshots.  Mining each build's own strings against its own
  hashes recovers 2,785 names for 2014-10-10 and 3,102 for 2015-01-21, and not one
  of them is a lizard.  2015-08-11 names exactly one lizard resource,
  `characters/lizard_base`, and a hash check confirms that it is a real row in the
  shipped build (it is one of the 17,435 already named).  Everything else lizard is
  media only - a hero coin (`menu/hero_coins/lizard_*`), an action-bar icon
  (`effects_ui/hud_action_ready_lizard`), a roar (`effects/wep_lizard_roar`), a
  locked-coin icon (`effects_ui/hero_coin_locked_lizard`) - and `gator` likewise
  survives only as `audio/enemy_gator`.

  This is the correction that matters for method: a snapshot's `_names.txt` is a
  harvest of **strings, not of live resources**.  It contains `lizard_archer`, which
  was never a live resource anywhere, and the 2015-08 harvest still contains
  `gator_brute` and `beastoforox_*` long after those names had been renamed away.  A
  name found in a harvest is only evidence once it has been hashed *and matched
  against that build's own resource list* - which is what `hunt_old_names.py` does,
  and what a raw grep does not.
