      (the same conclusion as 17.9/60.13 for animation clips in general).
    * `talisman_0N` / `ability_talisman_0N` (+ `_lowerbody`/`_upperbody`/`_cast`/
      `_impact`/`_loop`) in those same four directories - 0.

61.12 THE BUNDLE PAYLOADS ARE ZLIB, AND THEY DO CONTAIN NAMES (*)

  `Gauntlet-install/contents/<hex>` (1.5 GB, 260 bundles) is a Stingray container:
  each entry is a small header followed by a **zlib stream** (`78 9c`).  60.13
  grepped those files *as bytes* and correctly reported "no path-like strings" -
  compressed data has none - so nobody had ever decompressed them.  They do:
  one 25 MB slice of `0164ad48615ba9ed` is 536 zlib streams, 35 MB decompressed,
  49,323 ASCII strings (23,313 distinct) - `global_viewport`, `charselect`, `impale`,
  `decals`, `diffuse`, `movement`, `animationvariablecomponent`, DXBC shader blobs.

61.13 LOCAL ENUMERATION IS EXHAUSTED FOR THE SMALL TYPES (*)

  A round of micro-passes over the types where a handful of names remain, each
  enumerated from the grammar its *known* siblings spell out, all of them empty:

    * `audio/<token>` and `audio/<prefix>_<token>` (timpani_bank - the type is
      `audio/enemy_cultist`, `audio/enemy_mummy_king`, ...): 720 candidates, 0.
    * `flow/status_effect_<status>` for the full status vocabulary, and
      `flow/<token>`: 0.  (The 17 `flow/*` we hold are `flow/status_effect_*`,
      `flow/gib`, `flow/dropped_weapon` - the shape is known, the words are not.)
    * `resource_packages/<t>`, `resource_packages/<env>_<mode>`,
      `modules/d01_<env>/<mode>`, `foundation/<t>`, `core/<a>/<b>` and
      `core/editor_slave/level_editor/<b>` (render_config / package / level):
      20,149 candidates, 0.
    * `characters/<unit>{_var}/<ability>_illu` and `_icon` for every ability the
      Lua names (127) x 65 units x 16 variants x 3 directories: 10,271,745
      candidates, 0 - even though `_illu` is a *proven* family (61.7).
    * `hud/action_icons/<hero>_{action,ultimate,ability}_<ability>`,
      `effects_ui/<subject>_<hero>`, `gui/<t>_{ui,css,list,background}`,
      `menu/{buttons,level_select}/<t>`: 24,394 candidates, 0.
    * `<animation leaf>_var_<NN>` / `character_select_pos<NN>_var_<NN>` /
      `charselect*` for every unit and every held leaf: 15,173,600 candidates, 0.

  That is ~26 M candidates and it says something useful: the residual is not
  reachable from the *shape* of its siblings; it needs a wider token vocabulary or
  the engine's per-name edits.  The `_var_NN` shape itself is new and came from the
  engine, on the GPU box (`characters/avatar_wizard/animations/
  character_select_pos02_var_0{1,2}`), which is the pattern this project keeps
  re-learning (61.4: the reach comes from the token list, not the pattern).

61.14 THE MERGE ORDER IS PART OF THE RESULT (*)

  Two tools rewrote their output instead of appending to it, which threw away the
  only record of *which sweep found which name*:

    * `tools/pull-gpu.sh` unioned the box's `matches.tsv` with `sort -u`, so a name
      recovered by the box landed wherever its hash sorts rather than at the end,
      and
    * `tools/merge_matches.py` wrote the overlay with
      `json.dumps(..., sort_keys=True)`, which does the same to the JSON.

  Both are append-only now, and `pull-gpu.sh` prints what it appended:

      cat matches.tsv "$BOX" | awk -F'\t' '!seen[$1]++'      # ours, then theirs

  so our lines keep their order and the box's unseen names are appended in the
  order the box found them.  The overlay is written in insertion order
  (`json.dumps(ov, indent=2)`, no `sort_keys`).  `check_names.py --write`,
  `mine_bundle_strings.py --out` and the enumerations already appended, so this was
  the only leak.  Note the legacy part is still hash-order: the first 5,995 lines
  predate the fix and cannot be un-sorted, everything after them is chronological.

61.15 ENUMERATIONS MUST NOT REMEMBER THEIR OWN CANDIDATES (*)

  Two different bugs with one cause, and both of them cost a machine:

    * `check_names.py` did `sys.stdin.read().splitlines()` - it buffered the whole
      candidate stream before hashing anything; and
    * `enumerate_anims.py` / `enumerate_chains.py` kept a `seen` set to de-duplicate
      their output.

  Neither is visible at 10 000 candidates and both are fatal at 15 000 000: the
  names are hashed once and never needed again, so holding them is pure waste - the
  15 M-candidate leaf pass needed ~2 GB for names it had already finished with, and
  the 143 M-candidate chain space needed more than the box has.  That is what the
  out-of-memory was.

  The rule for every tool in `tools/`: stream the candidates, accumulate nothing but
  the hits (and the small bounded de-dupe of *hits*, which is a set of a few dozen).
  Verified: `check_names.py` holds 4 MB across 5 M candidates, `mine_lua_expand.py`
  and `mine_bundle_strings.py` ~20 MB and ~140 MB over tens of millions, and
  `enumerate_chains.py --wide 3` (143 M) now runs in 19 MB.  Where a bounded de-dupe
  is genuinely wanted, cap it (`mine_bundle_strings.py` stops adding at 4 M) rather
  than letting it grow with the input.

61.16 THE EFFECTS FAMILY, AND THE PULL-EXPLOIT LOOP (*)

  `effects/` (the `particles` type, 868 names held) is a sentence
  `<prefix>_<stem>_<action>[_<phase>]` with **47 prefixes**: `cha` (268 members),
  `wep` (173), `env` (99), `gam` (75), `hit` (56), `textures/additive` (53),
  `textures/alpha` (49), `obj` (24), `relic` (21), `statuseffect` (18), then
  `menu`/`cutscene`/`gib`/`meshes/sphere`/`units/spawner`/`potion`/`trap`/
  `treasure`/`elemental`/`footstep`/`gold`.  `tools/effects_family.py` crosses each
  prefix with the unit stems (catalogue units, their tokens, and the units named in
  `lua/generated/*.lua`), the tails of the members we hold, the animation leaves the
  units play, a verb x noun cross, and the phases the family spells out
  (`marker`/`tell`/`trail`/`windup`/`burst`/`spawn`/`destroy`/`2c`/`4c`/...).  The
  `cha` pass (2,724,645 candidates) returned

      effects/cha_skeleton_archer_aim_marker

  and the GPU box, running its own mutate rounds at the same time, returned three
  more in the same space: `cha_necromagus_light_attack_burst`,
  `cha_necromagus_skeleton_light_right_destroy`, `cha_eye_of_dur_imp_spawn`.  So the
  family is live and *both* methods reach into it.

  The workflow that produced that is worth naming, because it is now the loop:

    1. the GPU box runs the big rounds (battery -> self-feeding token mutate ->
       big_run) and appends every find to its `matches.tsv`;
    2. `tools/pull-gpu.sh` unions its file into ours **by appending** (61.14) and
       prints both the appended lines and the overlay delta;
    3. whatever shape the fresh finds reveal is enumerated locally straight away
       (`effects_family.py` came out of step 3, and `_var_NN` in 61.13 did too).

  The asymmetry is the point: the box is the throughput, this machine is the one that
  reads the new names and turns them into a space.  Everything else tried in this
  direction came back empty and is recorded above so it is not re-run: the `_illu`
  and `_icon` products (61.13), infix substitution with an element vocabulary
  (`_ash_`/`_lava_`/`_ice_` in every token position, 566,680 - 0), material suffixes
  (`<stem>_set`/`_set_lava`/`_no_fade`/`_dissolve`/..., 260,792 - 0) and the local
  `shaders/<token>` product.

61.17 THE CHAIN SPACE, COMPLETELY ENUMERATED (*)

  `tools/enumerate_chains.py 3 --wide` is now finished, and it is a *complete*
  enumeration rather than a sweep: **143,060,674 chains (53 techniques x every sorted
  subset of <= 3 of the 253 flag words)**, in 19 MB of memory, and it returns
  nothing.  Combined with 61.9 (43 techniques x subsets <= 3 of the 109 flags we
  held, 9,284,990, which found `character:enemy:silhouette:silhouette_decals_group_level`)
  and the depth-2 wide pass (1,702,996, empty), the `shader_library` chains we are
  missing are not combinations of any technique and any three flags that appear
  anywhere in the corpus - their flag words are words we do not have at all.

  That is the same conclusion the animation residuals keep forcing (17.9, 60.13,
  61.11): for these two types the vocabulary, not the grammar, is the frontier, so
  the only thing that can still move them is a *new source of words*.  The chain run
  also set the pattern for what a "complete" pass looks like here - finite, streaming,
  and reported as a count so the next person does not repeat it.

61.18 THE PROJECT'S OWN OLD PAIR FILES (the biggest single yield of the run) (*)

  Everything above assumes the missing names have to be *derived*.  They do not all:
  this project has been cracking the same game for years with several tools, and not
  every one of those runs left its answer in `matches.tsv`.  A `hash<TAB>name` pair
  is worth taking when it is verifiable, and it is trivially verifiable - re-hash the
  name and require `murmur64(name) == hash`, which is exactly what defines the name
  in the first place (the engine looks resources up by hash, so *any* preimage is a
  correct answer).  `tools/harvest_hash_pairs.py` sweeps every text file under the
  repo for that shape and keeps only the pairs that verify.

  Two runs, both of them large:

    * `Gauntlet-PreSlayer-2015-01-21/_slayer_all_matches.tsv` (2,410 pairs) - **113**
      verified against our residual: `characters/avatar_elf/elf_{diff,norm,spec}`,
      `characters/avatar_necromagus/necromagus_diff`, `gameobjects/doors/
      door_crypt_3keys_{diff,norm}`, `gameobjects/food/{apple,ham,turkey}_{diff,spec}`,
      `props/d01_caves/bridge_01_diff`, ...  (0 hash mismatches.)
    * the repo-wide sweep - **135** more, including 12 `package` names out of
      `bundle_name_overlay.json` (`resource_packages/heroes/{wizard_base,
      valkyrie_equipment, elf_banks, elf_capes, elf_equipment, warrior_banks,
      warrior_ability, wizard_banks, valkyrie_banks, ...}`), plus
      `equipment/warrior/weapon02_diff`, `characters/boss_mummy/boss_mummy_{diff,spec}`,
      `props/d01_lava/corruption_01_spec`, `props/d01_crypt/commander_chair_spec`.

  Net effect on the residual by type: **texture 1,153 -> 690**, particles 165 -> 163,
  material 216 -> 210, total 2,645 -> 2,148 unique hashes.  Most of what the pair files
  held back was textures, which is exactly the family that no grammar reaches - the
  answer had been sitting in a snapshot directory the whole time.

  Lesson: before spending another 100 M candidates on a type, grep the repository for
  `^[0-9a-f]{16}\t` and verify what turns up.  It costs seconds and it beat every
  enumeration in this session combined.

61.19 THE COMPILED BUNDLES REALLY ARE NAMELESS (recorded negative) (*)

  `bundle_manifest/09_reachability.tsv` maps every resource to its package *and its
  bundle file*, so the residual can be grouped by `(type, package, bundle)` - which
  says exactly which `contents/<hash>` files are worth decompressing instead of all
  1.5 GB.  `tools/mine_target_bundles.py` does that: for the 12 biggest groups
  (`shader_library` 143 in `core/rendering/renderer`, `texture` 104 + `shader` 22 in
  `resource_packages/menu`, `animation` 79 in `d01_lava`, `material` 42 in
  `all_effects`, ...) it decompressed every zlib stream and extracted every name-ish
  run, chains included.

  Result: 239-4,219 strings per bundle and **0 hits**.  The payloads are compiled
  bytecode, geometry and textures, and they carry almost no strings at all.  The one
  exception is a `render_config`, which genuinely does spell its `shader_libraries`
  list out as literal text (seen directly in `contents/1a9c33dcd0301dfd`:
  `core/rendering/base`, `core/rendering/caustic`, `core/rendering/character_cape`,
  ... 51 entries) - but every entry is a name we already hold, so that list is
  complete.  This closes "scan the shipped payloads" as a source of names.

61.20 THE `equippables_icons/equipment` BOARD IS THE SHOP-ITEM LOOKUP (*)

  §61.1-61.2 left 19 icon textures unnamed and treated all of them as "the art".  The
  largest single group of them is not art at all - it is a *table*.  `equippables_icons/
  equipment` (material, 5,208 bytes, `resource_packages/menu`) has an 8-byte header
  (`u32 version = 10`, `u32 count = 100`) and then 100 fixed 52-byte entries, the same
  shape §61 found for `hud/action_icons`:

      [ 0: 4]  key low      - the *top* 32 bits of murmur64("shop_" + <item id>)
      [ 4:12]  key high     - constant 0x3d7a037c
      [ 8:16]  shader hash  - murmur64("shaders/shop_icon"); 0 on the 16 below
      [16:24]  1, constant
      [24:32]  texture hash - murmur64(<texture resource name>)
      [32:52]  1, constant, zeroes

  and the entries are **sorted by that key**, so it is a binary-searchable map.  The
  key is not the name of anything on disk - it is the thin hash of a *shop* material,
  and `lua/menu/avatar_loadout.lua:item_icon` spells out where it comes from:

      icon = sprintf("shop_%s", item_id)          -- e.g. shop_eq_valkyrie_talisman01
      icon_material = Gui.material(gui, icon)

  i.e. the panel builds `shop_<item id>` from `lua/settings/items.lua` and reads the
  texture out of this table.  Checked against all 84 entries the catalogue can already
  name: `key low32 == murmur64("shop_" + basename(texture)) >> 32` **84/84**, and
  `texture == murmur64("equippables_icons/" + <item id>)` **84/84**.  The board is the
  `equippables_icons` namespace's lookup, nothing else.

  61.20a THE KEY IS AN ORACLE, AND IT NAMES THE 16 RESIDUAL ICONS

  Which means an unresolved entry can be labelled *without knowing its name*: hash the
  shop ids it could belong to and compare the top 32 bits.  Doing that for
  `eq_<hero>_talisman<01|02|03>` labels **15 of the 16** - the heroes' talisman icons,
  the exact family the store's TALISMANS panel draws:

      key       texture            shop name
      b9746d98  7ddc5a5550256ad3   shop_eq_valkyrie_talisman01
      308f83a5  174111e70af497a6   shop_eq_valkyrie_talisman02
      1e3b1ec7  0568fef95f147d6a   shop_eq_valkyrie_talisman03
      302bdb36  f952b5a5ef56ffed   shop_eq_warrior_talisman01
      7c8a6f90  250dfb341c8e915c   shop_eq_warrior_talisman02
      cedb0c10  c3b7936b7b1f52ba   shop_eq_warrior_talisman03
      dbb1b6d5  b1646965117fe21b   shop_eq_elf_talisman01
      7f5fe107  1644df1acbf87204   shop_eq_elf_talisman02
      6985c05c  999caa5fdf166f71   shop_eq_elf_talisman03
      a23445e0  12809bd4e1e66e32   shop_eq_wizard_talisman01
      f0408625  40a6467849f510ec   shop_eq_wizard_talisman02
      88a670da  96399c5d85932a77   shop_eq_wizard_talisman03
      f14994a1  42c45822bd6aca28   shop_eq_necromagus_talisman01
      b506e177  080223ab1c88aa0a   shop_eq_necromagus_talisman02
      c4ae772b  d38580863759e074   shop_eq_necromagus_talisman03
      26e74ba4  bcef7daa3a221c6c   (no `shop_eq_*_talisman*` - still open)

  The 16th is the necromagus `talisman_souleater` slot (`equipment/necromagus/
  talisman_souleater` is a Lua resource), but neither `shop_eq_necromagus_talisman_
  souleater` (3ace9cd8) nor `..._soul_eater` (101d6688) hashes to its key, so that one
  item id is spelled differently - it is the last unknown of the group.

  The mapping also settles §61.2's display-name question for the valkyries, read off
  the store's own panel: talisman01 is *Thyra's Vow* (`ultimate_vengeance`, "Righteous
  Retribution"), talisman02 *Guardian Symbol* (`ultimate_aegis`, "Divine Aegis"),
  talisman03 *Mark of the Commander* (`ultimate_valkyries`, "Summon Valkyries") - and
  the icons are, in that order, the leaf medallion `7ddc5a5550256ad3`, the gemmed star
  `174111e70af497a6` and the serpent knot `0568fef95f147d6a`.

  61.20b THE NAMES THEMSELVES ARE STILL OPEN - THE 16 BREAK THE CONVENTION (recorded)

  84/84 named entries are `equippables_icons/<item id>`; the 16 talismans are not, and
  no name of that shape exists: `murmur64("equippables_icons/eq_valkyrie_talisman01")`
  = 332eb8adc08e65b9 is **not in the catalogue at all**, so the artist shipped these
  under some other leaf.  Every enumeration for that leaf came back empty:

    * `equippables_icons/eq_<hero>_talisman<NN>` and 7 spelling variants x 13 dirs x 6
      prefixes x 11 suffixes (41k), plus NN over 0..100 and 1/2-digit forms (158k), 0;
    * the catalogue's **386 directory prefixes** x the item ids x all 274 map suffixes
      (3.2 M), 0 - so the leaf is not `eq_<hero>_talisman<NN>` under any namespace the
      project holds;
    * every directory form of the *shop* name itself (`shop_<id>`, `menu/shop/shop_<id>`,
      `equippables_icons/shop_<id>`, ...) x 16 ids (600), 0;
    * the display-name glues out of `game_strings.strings` (`thyras_vow`,
      `guardian_symbol`, `mark_of_the_commander`, ...) x 18 dirs x 5 prefixes x 8
      suffixes (70k), 0 - the icons are not named after their own text;
    * the ability-icon rule from `items.lua` (`<hero>_talisman_talisman<NN>_ability`),
      the Lua `icon` field (`valkyrie_ultimate_0N`), the `loc_` keys, the relic-style
      `relic_<item>` shape - all 0;
    * a 21.5 M token sweep over 12 dirs x 6 prefixes x the merged 257k-token vocabulary
      of the project (`ic_all`, `loc_slugs`, `tokens_big`, `all_names`, `hud_words`,
      `action_words`), 0;
    * the GPU box's own `talglue` (32.5 M) and `talhunt` (91.9 M) rounds over the same
      19 targets, 0; the Rust cracker's `equippables_icons/%a_%b`, `menu/shop/%a_%b`,
      `hud/%a_%b`, `equippables_icons/eq_%a_%b` over the 348k-token probe vocabulary
      (4 x 1.2e11 candidates), 0.

  So the leaf is *not derivable from shipped data*: it is either an art name no text
  holds, or the file was renamed after the store table was built.  What this section
  recovers is the **ownership** of all 16 (which hero, which talisman, which panel),
  which is what the art needed - `tools/dump_board.py <board> --item-ids ids.txt` prints
  exactly the table above and is the tool to re-run the moment a name is found.  The
  other §61.2 icons (`402c8d6e26caf96f`, `965dc9490a86221e`) are *not* in this table -
  they come from `equippables_icons/shop_action_icons` and are action plates, not items.









62. WHAT A CLEAN REBUILD OF THE DERIVED ARTEFACTS EXPOSED

  None of this is about the game's format.  It is the other half of the same rule:
  the manifest, the export and the progress report are *artefacts* - every one of
  them is re-derivable from the install, matches.tsv and the overlay - and if a
  rebuild is not clean then a finding cannot be re-checked.  Three of the six items
  below were silent: the tool reported success while it was wrong.

  62.1 A PARALLEL-JOB TEMP FILE KEYED ON THE SOURCE STEM (lost 10 of 144)

  convert_export.py's mp3 step wrote its intermediate to `<tmp>/<stem>.ogg` and read
  it back to transcode.  `named/elias/*/audio/` is full of identical stems - every
  screen ships `1_0_1.ugg`, `2_0_3.ugg`, `completed.ugg`, `gameover.ugg` - so at
  `--jobs 8` two workers on the same stem wrote the same path and the second
  unlink()ed the first's file.  A clean rebuild converted 134 of 144 tracks.  The old
  incremental tree only had 144 because the failures were retried on later runs until
  they won the race.

  The quiet half of that bug is the worse half: the same collision can hand ffmpeg the
  *wrong* intermediate and still exit 0, so the output is a valid mp3 of the wrong
  sound.  The rule is that a temp file may never be keyed on anything that is not
  unique per job - each job now gets its own file beside its own output, and the clean
  rebuild is 144/144.

  62.2 A DERIVED REPORT QUOTED ITS OWN NUMBERS, AND WENT STALE

  make_progress_report.py hard-coded two strings that read like narrative:
  `05_summary.txt reports "Unresolved 3,444"` and `sections 1-60`.  Both were wrong by
  the time anyone read them - the file says 2,143, which is the *static*
  hash_database.json count that barely moves, and FINDINGS had 61 sections.  A report
  whose whole purpose is to be current cannot carry quoted numbers: it now reads the
  `Unresolved` line out of 05_summary.txt and the section range off FINDINGS.txt, so
  adding this section made it print `sections 1-62` with no edit.

  Two traps on the way, both worth keeping.  The existing findings_sections() helper
  cannot answer that question: it is deliberately loose, so it also parses body prose
  - `  245 BILLION candidates across every ...` looks like section 245 - and the range
  therefore needs a strict `^NN. TITLE` match.  And the *documents* had drifted the
  same way the report had: SCRIPTS.md's export paragraph still described the previous
  dump (8,692 of 17,435 named, 1,096 unresolved) and export_all_resources.py's
  docstring still said "17,435 of 20,000 rows".  Derived documents go stale exactly
  like derived data; the only defence is to read the number from its source, or state
  it once and let a tool print it.

  62.3 A STATUS COUNTER THAT COUNTED THE WRONG SUBCOMMAND

  tools/pull-gpu.sh's status block asked for `release/hashcracker crack` and printed
  `engine processes: 0` while the box was running a `mutate` sweep on all 24 threads.
  The engine is one binary with a subcommand per sweep (`crack`, `mutate`, `pkg`,
  `combo`, `chain`, ...), so a counter has to match the binary and not one subcommand;
  it now counts every invocation and prints the breakdown by name.  That mattered more
  than it looks: the project's own tool was answering "is the box working?" with 0 in
  the one case - a long CPU sweep - where the true answer was "yes, harder than
  usual".

  62.4 THE BOX IS A CPU MACHINE BETWEEN GPU RUNS

  Which is why that counter mattered.  The GPU sweeps are *bursty*: talglue (32.5 M
  candidates), talhunt (91.9 M) and taltest (90.1 M) all completed inside one morning
  with 0 matches each, and the card then sits at 15 MiB of 8,188 used, 9 W of 55 W, no
  compute processes, Xorg as its only client.  The Rust engine's mutation sweeps are
  the opposite: one `mutate` over recovered_names x unresolved_targets x tokens_big
  was 13 hours in, averaging 4.2 of the box's 24 threads over its life and holding
  23.5 of them at the moment it was checked.

  So "load average 24.00 with the GPU idle" is the healthy state of that machine, and
  "is it using the CPU or the GPU" is answered by `nvidia-smi` for one and `top`/`ps`
  for the other - never by either alone.  A sleep/wake does not disturb any of it: the
  box had been up 25 hours with all six tmux sessions intact.

  62.5 A PULL FOUND TWO NAMES - AND WHAT COUNTS AS CHECKING THEM

  Re-checking the box appended two `shader_library` chain names its mutate sweep had
  found: `base:instanced:deferred_decals_group_level` (11bf0dbf40951969) and
  `character:skinned_3weights:outline` (7084201b8f179000).  That is +4 catalogue rows
  rather than +2, because the first is shipped in three renderer variants
  (core/rendering/renderer, emulate_renderer, mobile_renderer) and the second in one.
  They are the first chain-form names in this run to come out of a *mutation* sweep
  rather than a `%`-pattern sweep, and they sit in a family - the
  `deferred_decals_group_level` token appears in dozens of already-named chains.

  A name is only a name if it can be re-derived, so each was checked twice, and the
  second check is the instructive one:

    * re-hash it with the project's own bundle_tools.murmur_hash64 and compare with the
      recorded hash - both match exactly;
    * run tools/check_names.py on it.  That prints 0 hits, and 0 is the *correct*
      answer, not a contradiction: it tests candidates against the **unresolved** set,
      and both hashes are resolved now, so it never considers them - "a control name
      that is already resolved never prints", as its own docstring says.  Quoting that
      tool at a solved name proves nothing at all.

  62.6 AN INCREMENTAL RE-EXPORT LEAVES A TOMBSTONE

  write-if-absent is what makes the export cheap to refresh, and it is also how it
  lies: both of those hashes were already in `unresolved/`, so naming them *added*
  `named/base:instanced:deferred_decals_group_level.shader_library` and left
  `unresolved/shader_library/11bf0dbf40951969.shader_library` behind.  The tree then
  held the same resource twice, once named and once not.  Nothing in the tool removes
  it - the docstring says so ("delete the folder for a genuinely clean tree") - and
  after this round it was exactly two files, so they were deleted by hand.

  The counts are worth stating precisely, because they invite a wrong sum: `named/` is
  8,876 files; `unresolved/` is 1,014 files, which are 930 distinct hashes (84 of them
  carry two type names, and the dump keeps one file per (type, hash) pair, so
  930 + 84 = 1,014); the two add to 9,890.  The catalogue's "2,139 unresolved rows" is
  a third number again - rows, not files, and counted against the static
  hash_database.json.  Three different denominators all called "unresolved" is exactly
  how a stale report goes unnoticed.

  State after this round: overlay 6,878 names, 17,865/20,004 rows named (89.31%),
  930 unique unresolved hashes of which 44 are UNREACHABLE.

63. THE TALISMAN ICONS: THE PATTERN WAS THE PROBLEM

  61.20b recorded this family as closed - a 21.5 M token sweep, the box's talglue
  (32.5 M) and talhunt (91.9 M) rounds, four 1.2e11 pattern sweeps over the 348k probe
  vocabulary, all 0 - and concluded that the leaf "is not derivable from shipped data".
  That conclusion was wrong, and the mistake is worth naming: every one of those sweeps
  tested `equippables_icons/eq_<hero>_talisman<NN>` - the *item id* the board's key
  oracle had just handed us - and the artist never used the item id as the texture leaf.
  A known prefix is not a known shape.

  63.1 THE FIFTEEN NAMES

  The leaves are `equippables_icons/eq_<hero>_talisman_<flavour>`, where the flavour is
  a game word, often glued out of two:

    warrior     thors        medal          cup
    valkyrie    laurels      aegis          thyras
    wizard      merlins      magicite       mercuryvial
    elf         questors     dryadsflute    willowbranch
    necromagus  liliths      dreamcatcher   reaper

    f952b5a5ef56ffed  eq_warrior_talisman_thors
    250dfb341c8e915c  eq_warrior_talisman_medal
    c3b7936b7b1f52ba  eq_warrior_talisman_cup
    7ddc5a5550256ad3  eq_valkyrie_talisman_laurels
    174111e70af497a6  eq_valkyrie_talisman_aegis
    0568fef95f147d6a  eq_valkyrie_talisman_thyras
    12809bd4e1e66e32  eq_wizard_talisman_merlins
    40a6467849f510ec  eq_wizard_talisman_magicite
    96399c5d85932a77  eq_wizard_talisman_mercuryvial
    b1646965117fe21b  eq_elf_talisman_questors
    1644df1acbf87204  eq_elf_talisman_dryadsflute
    999caa5fdf166f71  eq_elf_talisman_willowbranch
    42c45822bd6aca28  eq_necromagus_talisman_liliths
    080223ab1c88aa0a  eq_necromagus_talisman_dreamcatcher
    d38580863759e074  eq_necromagus_talisman_reaper

  63.2 WHAT FOUND THEM, IN ORDER OF USEFULNESS

  One hand-built list of 9,842 candidates did the hard part first: the shape
  `<dir>/eq_<hero>_talisman_<w>` over every *ability* word the already-resolved action
  icons give us (`aegis`, `valkyries`, `vengeance`, `moreofthor`, `dryadssong`,
  `hypersniper`, `darkhunger`, `souleater`, `arcaneblast`, ...) plus each hero's own
  name (`thyras`, `merlins`, `liliths`).  It hit `_aegis` immediately, and that single
  hit was the whole answer: it turned an unknown leaf into a **known prefix plus one
  unknown word**, and that reduces the problem from "invent a name" to "use every
  vocabulary you already have as a one-slot guess" - which is why the very next pass
  (CPython `tools/check_names.py`, no GPU, no patterns) returned 11 more in about a
  second of hashing: 3 from `ic_slug` (5,330 words), 8 from `loc_slugs` (13,346).
  `ic_all`/`bundle_words`/`big2_dict` added `cup`; the box's GPU battery added `medal`.

  The last one to fall was `laurels`, and it did not come from a list at all.  The
  display-name strings were the lead: `game_strings.strings` holds the store text
  ("Thyra's Vow", "Guardian Symbol", "Mark of the Commander", "Enchanted Willow
  Branch", "Judgement Warplate", ...) - note "Enchanted Willow Branch" against the
  recovered `willowbranch`, i.e. the flavour is the *significant words* of the item's
  name - and the lore text next to it: "Thyra's locket, reminding her of her true
  purpose. The inscription on the inside says \"Laurel\"."  Reading the item's own
  description suggested `laurels`, and it was right.

  So the general lesson is not "try harder": it is that a single confirmed hit converts
  an exhausted space into a trivial one, and that the *first* thing to do with a
  newly-known prefix is to re-run every existing vocabulary list against it - the lists
  were never the problem, the shape was.

  63.3 A REPEATED SLOT LETTER IS NOT A MASK (recorded) (*)

  Chasing the last names with masks turned up an engine behaviour worth writing down:
  the pattern engine **deduplicates slot letters**.  `--list a=mask_az --pattern
  'x%a%a%a%a%a%a'` produced **130** candidates - 5 heroes x 26 - not 5 x 26^6.  A
  repeated slot contributes one dimension however many times it is written, so a mask
  needs one *distinct* letter per character, each bound to the same alphabet file:

      --list a=az --list b=az --pattern '%a%b'          -> 676   (verified)

  Two consequences.  First, the first GPU battery's "mask" phases ran in seven seconds
  and reported 0 matches because they were testing 130 candidates and calling it a
  mask - the phases were rewritten (the script that had them is superseded by
  tools/gpu_talisman_battery.sh, which uses distinct letters).  Second, and more
  important for the record: the same applies to *older* rounds, so any surviving 0 from
  a pattern with a repeated letter - talhunt's `%h_%k%k` is one, at 84 candidates
  reported - should be re-read as "0 over far fewer candidates than intended", not as
  "that space is empty".

  63.4 THE NEGATIVES, RECORDED SO THEY ARE NOT RUN AGAIN

    * bundle index adjacency carries no naming signal: 03be10bb448c6133 sits at index
      121 of resource_packages/menu between `hud/hud_gold_bracket_left`,
      `menu/buttons/vertical_selection_over`, `menu/how_to_play/warrior_smash`,
      `equippables_icons/relic_illusion_ring`, `equippables_icons/eq_warrior_head_set03`,
      `menu/hero_coins/wizard_colored` and `hud/spells/lightning_burst`.  The order is
      not name-grouped, so a neighbour cannot be used to guess a name.
    * no byte-identical twin: md5 of all 1,525 named `.texture` bodies in the export
      against all 18 targets - 0.  The art is unique, so no copy is hiding under a
      second name.
    * no near-twin family either (`tools/twin_by_image.py`): the nearest *named*
      textures to the three valkyrie icons are `achievement_icons/*_locked` at L1
      distance 0.05-0.08 - far too far to be a family, and these are talismans, not
      badges.
    * package and size do not discriminate: all 18 targets live in
      `resource_packages/menu` and every one is 43,836 bytes, the same size as 275
      named icon textures.
    * the reference graph names only the three boards - `equippables_icons/equipment`,
      `equippables_icons/shop_action_icons`, `hud/action_icons`.  Nothing else in the
      catalogue draws them (they are UI-only, so no unit body carries a hint).
    * the store flavour does **not** transfer to the two ability plates:
      `hud/action_icons/<hero>_ultimate_<flavour>` over all flavours and 945 variants,
      0.  §61.3's wall - hand-authored ability words that no shipped file spells out -
      still stands for 402c8d6e26caf96f and 965dc9490a86221e.

  63.5 STATE

  17,880/20,004 rows named (89.38%), overlay 6,893, 915 unique unresolved hashes of
  which 38 are UNREACHABLE.  Still open in this family: the 16th board entry
  (`bcef7daa3a221c6c`, whose shop key matches no `shop_eq_<hero>_talisman*` spelling at
  all, so its item id is a third spelling), and the two ability plates above.
  `tools/gpu_talisman_battery.sh` is running the pairs-and-masks tail on the box.

  63.6 THE 16th ROW IS THE GHOST ORB'S ART, UNDER A SECOND NAME (identified by eye)

  The last unresolved row of the equipment board (`bcef7daa3a221c6c`) was identified
  visually - "this looks like the ghost orb relic" - and the pixels agree emphatically:

      bcef7daa3a221c6c  256x256   L1 0.0210  equippables_icons/relic_ghost_orb
                                   L1 0.1131  achievement_icons/eye_for_an_eye_locked
                                   L1 0.1146  achievement_icons/mysteries_in_the_dark_locked

  The nearest named texture is 5.4x closer than the next candidate, i.e. this is the
  ghost orb's art and nothing else.  It is not, however, the *same file*: the two bodies
  are both 43,836 bytes, they share their 128-byte DDS header byte for byte, and 53.5%
  of the payload differs - a re-encode (or a re-export) of one image, not a copy.  The
  named one is `equippables_icons/relic_ghost_orb` = `1930e1af0d70213f`; this row points
  at a second, unnamed export of the same picture.

  So the row is a *duplicate asset*, and its name is a spelling that no list holds:

    * not `equippables_icons/relic_ghost_orb` (that is 1930e1af, a different hash);
    * not any of 6,018 ghost/orb spellings across 14 directories, nor 3,852 slugged
      forms of the 113 localisation strings that mention ghost/orb/spirit;
    * not any of 122,160 relic/talisman/potion variants (`_tier1`, `_locked`, `_02`,
      `_ability`, ... over the 12 named relic basenames and the 15 recovered flavours);
    * not any item id the game can build.  This one is worth stating carefully, because
      it is a *proved* negative: `items.lua` builds item ids from `sprintf("eq_%s_%s",
      avatar_type, id)` for weapon/talisman, `eq_<avatar>_<eq_type>_<id>` for
      head/torso/legs/weapon_misc, `eq_<avatar>_shield_<id>` and `relic_<id>`, so an
      enumeration of that whole grammar (1,829 ids) reproduces **all fifteen** board keys
      we already know, exactly - and matches key `26e74ba4` not at all.  The same is true
      of the relic-tier keys the store builds directly (`shop_relic_tier_%d`,
      `avatar_loadout.lua:564`) for tiers 0-99, and of all 66 keys in the relics board.

  Two things follow.  First, that row's shop id is built by code the source repo does
  not contain (the readable Lua is a partial decompile), so labelling it means either
  finding the id that hashes to `26e74ba4` - a 32-bit oracle, but the engine only
  matches full 64-bit targets (`u64::from_str_radix` in `src/main.rs`), so it stays a
  Python-side search - or finding the leaf directly, which is what the running masks do.
  Second, the relics board is itself a fresh oracle: it has 66 entries but only 12
  distinct icons - **6 keys point at `equippables_icons/relic_ghost_orb` alone** - so
  the relic *item* names are mostly unrecovered even though their icons are named, and
  that board's 66 keys can be brute-forced to label them (the vocabulary to try is the
  shop's own relic flavour words, which the item ids clearly use).

  63.7 THE ABILITY LUA NAMES THE PLATE, AND THE BOARD KEYS ON THE ICON ID (*)

  The two shop plates that were still open (the warrior's potion-throw and the elf's
  forest-spirits) turned out to be reachable by a mechanism worth recording, because it
  is a *third* key formula and a clean oracle.

  Every talisman ability file carries an **`icon`** field:

      equipment/warrior/talisman01.lua:   icon = "warrior_ultimate_01",
      equipment/elf/talisman03.lua:       icon = "elf_ultimate_03"      (etc., 15 files)

  and the `hud/action_icons` board resolves that id with

      key == murmur64(<icon id>) >> 32          (validated 15/15)

  - i.e. that board is keyed on the *icon id*, **not** on `shop_<item id>` (which is the
  equipment board's rule, §61.20) and not on the texture name.  So the board is a
  complete oracle from the ability Lua to the plate texture, and all fifteen ids resolve:

      warrior_ultimate_01 -> 402c8d6e26caf96f  MISSING   <- potion_throw
      warrior_ultimate_02 -> hud/action_icons/warrior_ultimate_moreofthor
      warrior_ultimate_03 -> hud/action_icons/warrior_ultimate_hunger
      valkyrie_ultimate_01/02/03 -> ..._vengeance / ..._aegis / ..._valkyries
      wizard_ultimate_01/02/03   -> ..._arcaneblast / ..._polymorph / ..._barrier
      elf_ultimate_01/02         -> ..._hypersniper / ..._dryadssong
      elf_ultimate_03     -> 965dc9490a86221e  MISSING   <- wisps_cast
      necromagus_ultimate_01/02/03 -> ..._darkhunger / ..._souleater / ..._resurrect

  So the *shape* of the answer is certain: `hud/action_icons/warrior_ultimate_<flavour>`
  and `hud/action_icons/elf_ultimate_<flavour>`, with the flavour being the ability's own
  name glued, lower-case a-z, 6-11 characters.

  The **flow prefix is not the flavour** - `necromagus/talisman02.lua` flows as
  `ultimate_remnant_*` while its plate is `souleater` - so the obvious shortcut
  (`..._ultimate_throw`, `..._ultimate_wisp`) does not hold and both were tested anyway.

  What the flavour words are *not*, all verified:
    * 459,510 slug forms (glued / underscored / lower-cased) of all 12,157 strings in
      `game_strings.strings`, under every plate shape - 0.  "Improvise" and "Spirit
      Guardians" are real ability names in that pool and both were tested directly.
    * 12,645 hand-curated potion / wisp / spirit / guardian variants - 0.
    * on the GPU: `loc x loc` glued and underscored (356 M), `loc x ic_all` both orders
      (6.5 G) and `loc x loc x loc` glued (4.8 G) - 0.  Note this only became meaningful
      once the vocabularies were checked against each other: `spirit` and `guardians`
      are in `loc_slugs` but *not* in `ic_all`, so every earlier pair sweep that crossed
      `ic_all` with itself structurally could not have produced `spiritguardians`.
    * masks over a-z for 5, 6 and 7 letters (2 heroes x 26^5..26^7) - 0; 8 and 9 are
      running, 10-11 are left for later.

  Two of the *resolved* flavours are absent from the localisation pool as well
  (`hypersniper`, `souleater`), so a code-side word is the normal case here rather than
  an anomaly, and the mask is the honest tool for the last two - or the in-game display
  name of the two abilities, which the strings pool holds but which is not what the
  artist used for the other thirteen (`Resurrect` -> `resurrect` holds, but `Remnant`
  -> `souleater` does not).  `tools/gpu_plate_battery.sh` is the run.

  63.8 THE SECOND PATH - AND BOTH PLATES ARE NOW CONFIRMED AS THE STORE'S POTION ICONS

  §63.7's oracle came from the ability Lua's `icon` field.  There is a *second* path to
  the same two textures, and it is the one the store tooltip actually uses:

      lua/menu/avatar_loadout.lua:779   if ability_icon then icon = item.ability_icon or "item_empty"
      lua/settings/items.lua:780        item.ability_icon = sprintf("%s_%s_%s_ability", avatar_type, eq_type, id)

  i.e. `<hero>_<eq_type>_<id>_ability` - `warrior_talisman_talisman01_ability` for the
  warrior's first talisman.  That id resolves in the **`equippables_icons/shop_action_icons`**
  board by the same `key == murmur64(<id>) >> 32` rule, and both unresolved ids land on
  the two plates:

      warrior_talisman_talisman01_ability -> f1d32816 -> 402c8d6e26caf96f  (unresolved)
      elf_talisman_talisman03_ability     -> 7e190670 -> 965dc9490a86221e  (unresolved)
      warrior_talisman_talisman02_ability -> d9e0d8b4 -> hud/action_icons/warrior_ultimate_moreofthor
      elf_talisman_talisman01_ability     -> 9c69a6ee -> hud/action_icons/elf_ultimate_hypersniper

  So the two hashes are the **potion-ability icons** the store shows for those talismans,
  and that is now confirmed three ways: the ability Lua's `icon` field, `items.lua`'s
  `ability_icon` id, and the game's own screen (`TALISMANS` -> *Thor's Crummy Necklace*,
  `POTION ABILITY: Improvise`, "Throw the potion like a grenade..." - the blue potion in
  a hand; and *Enchanted Willow Branch*, `POTION ABILITY: Spirit Guardians`, "Summons
  three forest spirits that circle around the Elf" - the green wisps).

  A note on the shape, because it is now pinned: all 33 *named* textures in the
  `hud/action_icons` board are in the `hud/action_icons` namespace (2 are root), so the
  two missing ones are `hud/action_icons/<hero>_ultimate_<flavour>` - the namespace and
  the prefix are certain, only the flavour word is open.

  The display names are *not* the flavour words: `improvise` (9) and `spiritguardians`
  (15) were both tested directly (and singles over the whole 9,035-form pool), so the
  artist used something else for these two, as he did for `souleater` (whose flow name is
  `remnant`) and `hypersniper` (absent from the localisation pool).  What has now been
  swept clean, all 0: singles over the pool (9,035) and loc_slugs (13,346); pairs
  glued *and* underscored over pool x pool (163 M), loc x loc (356 M), pool x ic_all both
  orders (8.9 G, local CPU) and loc x ic_all both orders (13 G, GPU); 12,645 curated
  potion/wisp/spirit variants; masks over a-z at 5, 6 and 7 letters.  Queued next:
  *three* pool words glued (1.5e12) - the one phrase space no earlier sweep could produce,
  because `forest` is in the strings pool but in neither `loc_slugs` nor `ic_all`
  (`tools/gpu_plate_cube.sh`), then the 8 and 9 letter masks.


64. ANIMATIONS: EVERY TARGET HAS A CONTEXT, AND THE CORPUS IS SATURATED

  The 309 distinct unresolved animation hashes (545 rows) are the largest single bucket
  left, and they are the one family where the *context* of every target is known before
  the sweep starts.

  64.1 THE CONTEXT IS THE REFERRER, NOT THE CORPUS

  `extract_anim_refs.py` maps unresolved animations to their owning unit through
  `state_machine` bodies, and its output (`work/refs_animation.json`, 343 hashes) covers
  **all 309** of them: every unresolved clip is referenced by a *named* resource, normally
  its own unit file (`characters/boss_orox/boss_orox`, `gameobjects/spawners/spawner_beam`,
  `characters/necromagus_summoned_soldier/...`), so the character - and therefore the
  directory - of each target is known in advance, and a candidate only has to be right
  about the leaf.

  The directory list still has to be built with care.  The named corpus gives 47
  directories (92 with both `animations/` and `animation/` spellings, which the shipped
  data mixes: 156 clips under `skeleton_warrior/animation`, 53 under
  `avatar_necromagus/animation`), and it *misses* any directory whose clips are all
  unresolved (`cultist_zealot` has 11 unresolved and 0 resolved).  Adding
  `<referrer dir>/{animations,animation}` for each of the 309 gives 102
  (`data/vocab/anim_dirs_ctx.txt`) - a strict superset of the older
  `data/vocab/animation_dirs.txt` (46).

  Corpus shape: 2,276 animation rows, 1,731 named, 942 *distinct* names, 588 distinct
  leaves.

  64.2 SHARING ACROSS CHARACTERS IS NOT VERBATIM

  The obvious hope - the same clip spelled the same under another character - is false.
  92 directories x 588 resolved leaves = 54,096 candidates, and 0 of them match.  The
  corpus even contradicts itself between characters: `mummy_priest` has `move_walk_back`,
  `mummy_risen` has `move_walk_backwards`.  What is shared is the *vocabulary*, not the
  spelling, so the win has to come from products of tokens - which is what the engine is
  for, at 200 M candidates/s on the CPU path.



  64.3 MEASURE THE SHAPE BEFORE SPENDING THE BUDGET

  Every naive shape for this family is either tiny or enormous, so the shapes were
  *benchmarked* first: run each one against the **1,731 names we already hold** and count
  how many it can rebuild from tokens alone (`tools/anim_shape_bench.sh`).  Point
  `--matches` at a fresh file or the engine dedups the known names away and every shape
  scores 0.

    shape                                        rebuilt   candidates
    prefix_tail   %d/%p_%t   p=233 corpus prefixes, t=pool u tokens (9,394)
                                                 819  47%     2.2e8
    a_b           %d/%t1_%t2 (386 animation tokens)  358  21%     1.5e7
    a_b_c         %d/%t1_%t2_%t3 (386^3)             352  20%     5.9e9
    leaf_tail     %d/%l_%t  (588 leaves)             341  20%     5.6e8
    pool_anim     %d/%pool_t_%anim_t                 58           3.6e8
    a_b glued     %d/%t1t2                             3           1.5e7
    a_b_c glued   %d/%t1t2t3                           2           5.9e9
    leaf_suffix   %d/%l%s (258 suffixes)               1           1.5e7

  Two conclusions, both worth more than the 20 minutes the bench cost.  First, **a third
  token buys nothing**: 352 against 358 at 388x the cost, so the 1.2e11 anim-token and
  token-x-pool-x-token rounds queued earlier were cancelled rather than run.  Second,
  glued and suffix forms essentially do not occur in this family, so `attack_shieldbash`
  is one glued word and not a shape.  What is left is `<prefix>_<one word>` (47%), and the
  word has to come from outside the corpus.

  64.4 WHAT WAS FOUND

    characters/avatar_valkyrie/animations/move_jog_break_fwd   944beba296c3352d  mutate
    characters/avatar_wizard/animations/wind_offset             4b70cbb14031b301  pattern

  `move_jog_break_fwd` came from `mutate` (append a token plus a suffix to `move_jog`);
  `wind_offset` came from `%d/<pool word>_<animation token>` - and `wind` is *not* an
  animation token, it is in `strings_pool_slugs.txt`, which is exactly why the shapes that
  cross the corpus with the pool earn their budget.  Both were verified by round trip
  (`crack --candidates` against a fresh `--matches` file), which matters here because
  `check_names.py` tests the *unresolved* set: 0 hits for an already-merged name is the
  expected result, not a refutation.

  64.5 SATURATION: THE VOCABULARY IS THE BOTTLENECK

  With the corpus as the only vocabulary, the family is exhausted.  All of these are 0
  new against the residual: `%d/%l`; `%d/%p_%l`; `%d/%l_%p`; `%d/%l_%k`; `%d/%k_%l`;
  `%d/%p_%k`; `%d/%k_%k2`; `%d/%l_%l`; prefix^3 (1.3e9); corpus-token^3 (2.3e9);
  **leaf^3 (2e10)** - and, more tellingly, the bench's *best* shapes aimed at the residual
  rather than at the corpus: prefix_tail, tails x prefix, leaf x tails, tails x leaf
  (2.2e8-5.6e8 each).  A cascading `mutate` loop (`tools/anim_mutate_loop.sh`: seeds = all
  942 known names plus both new finds, tokens = their own tokens (338) and
  corpus + pool (9,335), two variants per pass) saturates on pass 1 with 0 new.  The
  control for that loop is the point: with a fresh matches file the *same* mutate rebuilds
  **819 / 1,731** known names, so the plumbing is sound and what is missing is words.

  The missing words can be shown directly.  `impact`, `summoned` and `grapple` - ordinary
  animation words, and the kind of thing a boss clip is called - appear in *none* of
  `strings_pool_slugs.txt`, `loc_slugs.txt` or `leaves/leaf.txt`.  No sweep over the
  vocabularies we hold can produce a leaf built from words we do not hold.

  State after this round: overlay 6,895 names, 17,882/20,004 rows named (89.39%), 913
  unique unresolved; animation targets 309 -> 307.  The 19 icon plates are unaffected
  (the box's `tal5`/`tal6` rounds are still the live line there).

  64.6 TOOLS

    tools/anim_battery.sh         dirs x (tokens, pool) products + mutate - the round that
                                  found `wind_offset`; logs to /tmp/anim_battery.log
    tools/anim_shape_bench.sh     the shape-vs-known-corpus measurement above
    tools/anim_corpus_battery.sh  corpus-only products (leaves/prefixes/tokens), incl.
                                  the 2e10 leaf^3; all 0 new
    tools/anim_mutate_loop.sh     cascading mutate, seeds and tokens both grow from the
                                  finds; saturates on pass 1

  What would break the saturation, in order of promise: (a) a tooltip or asset-browser
  view of any *one* remaining clip, which turns the vocabulary problem into a shape
  problem; (b) the unit/state-machine body itself, since clips are named after the state
  that plays them (§47.9) and `enumerate_anims.py`'s 55 M stream over the Lua event names
  is a true zero (its space *does* contain the names we hold, so the negative is real;
  `--candidates` was verified with a known pair for exactly this reason); (c) the same
  cross-build hunt the icons used - a 2014/2015 snapshot's animation names for hashes that
  are still live today.

  64.7 THE SHARED CLIPS: THE CONCEPT IS SHARED, THE SPELLING IS NOT

  61.2/64.2 asked the right question and the corpus answers it: an animation *is* shared
  between entities - the same walk, the same hit, the same die - but never as the same
  string.  Grouping the 590 resolved leaves into concepts makes the size of the problem
  visible: **452 concepts, 62 of them spelled more than one way**.

    hit_offset    hit_offset, hit_offset1, hit_offset2, hit_offset3, hit_offset01,
                  hit_offset02, hit_offset03, hit_offset_01, ..., offset_hit_01   (11)
    die / death   die_left_01, die_left1, die_left_2, death_left_01, death_left_02,
                  die_headshot01, death_headshot_02, die_decapitated
    move / walk   move_walk_backwards (mummy_risen) beside move_walk_back (mummy_priest),
                  move_run, walk_run, walk_default, walk_strafe_left

  So "does this entity have the clip the others have?" is not a string search, it is a
  search *per spelling convention* - which is what `tools/anim_shared_vocab.py` does: the
  corpus's concepts, respelled every way the corpus respells them (underscored number,
  glued number, `_default`/`_normal`/`_light`/`_heavy`, direction tokens with and without
  numbers, reversed token order so `offset_hit_01` is reachable), under the directories of
  the 37 entities that still own unresolved clips.

  It named four clips in one run - all on the **singular** `animation/` directory, all
  with the number **glued**:

    characters/cultist_novice/animation/die_headshot01   fdaf5c89e291301c
    characters/cultist_novice/animation/die_left01       23cc119b8b8ddb09
    characters/cultist_novice/animation/die_right01      910ca8a9e30041d7
    characters/cultist_novice/animation/die_slump01      0a9ed352aeb62d9b

  None of the four was reachable by anything built before, and the reason is structural,
  not budgetary: `mutate` substitutes a *token*, so it can turn `die_headshot_01` into
  `die_headshot_1` but can never drop the separator; `%p_%t` and `leaf_tail` always join
  with `_`; and the glued shapes (`%a%b`) glue *everything*, so the mixed form
  `<words>_<words><NN>` - which is what these are - has no expression in the pattern
  language at all.  Widening the conventions (26.8 M candidates: numbers 1-20 in four
  forms, more suffix families, both directions) added nothing, so the four are the whole
  of this family's spelling problem.

  Two other fingerprints were aimed at the same targets and both are recorded as
  negatives, because each would have been decisive had it held:

    * **identical bodies**: 0 of the 307 unresolved animation bodies are byte-identical to
      any of the 939 named ones.  An animation is rigged to its entity, so a shared clip
      does not share bytes and the name is not recoverable from them.
    * **size twins**: 116 unresolved rows (48 hashes) have a named animation of exactly the
      same `body_size`, and size is nearly a function of the clip (§43).  The twin is not
      the answer either - 9,086 candidates built from those twins (in the entity's own
      directory, respelled) are 0 - and the biggest clusters show why: size 444 is
      `stage1_idle`/`stage2_idle`/`stage3_idle`/`dormant` for `gameobjects/spawners`, and
      the unresolved clips at that size are *other* clips of the same shape, not those
      clips under another name.

  What the referrers do give, reliably, is the *directory* - and they have to be read as
  paths, not as unit names.  `gameobjects/spawners/spawner_beam` alone owns 141 of the 543
  unresolved rows, and the named spawner clips all sit in one shared directory
  (`gameobjects/spawners/animations/`: `stage1_idle`, `stage2_idle`, `stage3_idle`,
  `stage1_hit`, `stage2_hit`, `stage3_hit`, `death`, `appear`, `dormant`).  Deriving only
  the first-level unit collapses that name away, so `tools/anim_entity_guess.py` walks
  every progressive context (`<each prefix>/{animations,animation}`) crossed with the
  corpus leaves, the size twins and their respellings: 117,890 candidates, 0.  The spawner
  clips are not a stage matrix either (`stage<N>_<verb>` and `<spawner>_<verb>` over 50
  verbs, 9,870 candidates, 0).

  State after this round: overlay 6,899 names, 17,886/20,004 rows named, 909 unique
  unresolved hashes; the animation bucket 309 -> 305 distinct (543 unresolved rows, 141 of
  them spawner-owned).

65. UNITS: READ THE BODY, AND READ THE TREE AS IT IS

  The animation work kept stalling on one thing: the directory.  `characters/<unit>/animations`
  is only right when the unit *is* the entity - and 42 unresolved hashes are `unit`s
  themselves (54 rows), all in level modules (`modules/d01_crypt/resources`,
  `modules/d01_lava/resources`, `modules/menu/resources`).  These are the modular level
  geometry, and a unit's name *is* its animation directory, so naming a unit names a
  directory.

  65.1 THE BODY NAMES ITS OWN PIECES

  A Stingray unit body embeds its node names as plain ASCII - material nodes (`m_*`),
  sections (`s_*`), walkable zones (`z_*`).  Reading them out of
  `~/Documents/Coding/Gauntlet-Export/unresolved/unit/<hash>.unit` gives, for example:

    1f01e19947211248   m_crypt_railing_01, s_crypt_railing_straight_2c
    34ad7dab78fa1dbf   m_crypt_railing_01, s_crypt_railing_end
    05b95a956d2b2f06   m_lavastruct_floor_02, m_lavastruct_pillar_01, m_lavastruct_railing_01
    2e5618907cc69809   z_platform_walkable_01 ... _11
    71d81979402a0c01   m_lavastruct_goat_satan_statue
    93f1d4141e624ab2   m_lava_warterfall_01, m_lava_waterfall_02

  The `s_` strings are the *pieces*.  The `m_` strings are materials, and they sit close to
  the unit name without being it: `m_lavastruct_railing_01` belongs to the unit
  `blocks/d01_lava/railings/lavastruct_railing_2c`, so the trailing `_01` drops and a corner
  suffix (`_2c`/`_4c`, the corner count) appears.  (Note `warterfall` - the typo is in the
  game's own asset.)

  65.2 THE THREE NAMES

    blocks/d01_crypt/railings/crypt_railing_end          34ad7dab78fa1dbf
    blocks/d01_crypt/railings/crypt_railing_straight_2c  1f01e19947211248
    blocks/d01_crypt/railings/crypt_railing_straight_4c  b04f8e8773161d9d

  All three are the *literal* `s_*` strings under `blocks/<level>/<family>/`.  `railings`
  had never appeared in our known `d01_crypt` names - the family dirs we held for that level
  were only pillars/borders/stairs/rocks/gigameshes/floors/walls - because no *named*
  resource had ever put a file there.  So deriving the family list *per level from our own
  corpus* silently excluded the one directory the answer lived in: that trap is what made two
  passes of 1.6 M and 705 K candidates return 0.  What works is the union of families across
  every level (plus the real unit-directory tree read out of the catalogue), which is what
  `tools/unit_guess_by_body.py` now does.  Its control - a copy of the catalogue with those
  three names blanked - reproduces all three (3,309,852 candidates, 3 matches).

  65.3 THE NEGATIVES

    * `m_` strings are usually *not* the unit name: `crypt_floor_01` is a texture
      (`blocks/d01_crypt/crypt_floor_01_diff`) whose unit we still have not named.
    * Only ~30% of these units carry any `s_`/`z_` string at all (18 of 54 rows), so the
      method cannot reach the rest on its own.
    * The referrer route is empty here: `modules/d01_lava/room_lava_floor_adventure_2_template`
      and the `crypt_quilt_*` encounter templates reference their pieces **by hash**, and
      their bodies embed only markers and connectors (`north2_open`, `south2_closed`,
      `marker_unit`, `encounter_node_start`) - the pieces' names are not written down there.
    * `Gauntlet-install/Gauntlet-Source-DO_NOT_EDIT` is Lua-only (927 files: lua, foundation,
      gameobjects, gui, characters, equipment, ability_units, props) and has no `blocks/`
      tree, so the level geometry was never shipped as source text.
    * Stem x every known unit directory (10,270), stem x respellings x every known unit
      directory (705,312) and the literal `s_`/`z_` stems x every known unit directory
      (34,286) are all 0 - the directory, not the leaf, was the unknown.
    * The 141 spawner-owned animation rows survive the same treatment: the concept-spelling
      vocabulary (which named the four cultist clips, 64.7) crossed with every spawner
      directory context (`gameobjects/spawners/{animations,animation}` and
      `gameobjects/spawners/spawner_{beam,twister,t1,ghost}/{animations,animation}`) is
      4,346,808 candidates, 0.

  State after this round: overlay 6,902 names, 17,892/20,004 rows named, 906 unique
  unresolved hashes; unit bucket 42 -> 39.

66. THE BUCKETS THAT DID NOT MOVE, AND WHY

  66.1 MORE FINDS.  Re-running the standing battery (`sweeps/battery.sh`) after the three
  units were named has now produced **five** names - the batch of new names is fuel for
  phases that had been exhausted at the previous corpus size:

    fc65a8a7da42eacc  effects/trap_floor_blades_swipe
    7701c677b29b25e0  effects/wep_valkyrie_ultimate_vengeance_slash
    a0c3772f0de799e6  base:instanced:deferred_decals_group_level:diffuse_map:normal_map
    71f47829a45b0f5d  base:instanced:deferred_decals_group_other:diffuse_map:static_lighting
    75fe5327ac8d781b  base:instanced:deferred_decals_group_prop:diffuse_map:normal_map

  Two of them are effects and three are shader-library *flag chains*, so the chain
  enumeration of section 60.14 was not complete either - worth remembering before any
  future round is called closed.

  66.2 TEXTURES (252 distinct unresolved).  All of these are 0:

    * byte-identical bodies - 0 of 252 against 1,540 named texture bodies, so the same art is
      never shipped twice under the same bytes in this build.
    * pixel-identical PNG twins (`tools/twin_by_image.py`, the oracle that caught the ghost
      orb).  Exactly one exact match, `72363b5263f238e0` == `textures/white`; the 47
      near-twins under 0.02 are almost all flat `_norm` maps (all flat normals look alike),
      and filtering the twins to diffuse art leaves 36 hints whose family mutation is 0.
    * the map-set rule - an owner's *known* textures give the channel set and the unresolved
      sibling is another channel: 162,871 candidates, 0.  Owners reference generic shared art
      (`textures/outline`, `menu/black`, `effects/textures/alpha_smoke_thin_01`) while their
      own art is a word we do not hold.
    * the node names inside the *owner's* body (`m_`/`s_`/`t_` stems) x the owner's directory
      x channel suffixes: 12,860, 0; the same with every known texture directory as the dir
      side: 890,807, 0.
    * `crack_siblings.py --type texture`: 31 M candidates, 0.

  66.3 MATERIALS (91).  Owner bodies plus every material directory: 2,137,379 candidates, 0.
  The known materials are named after their *units* (`blocks/d01_caves/caverock_set`,
  `ability_units/spawners/spawner_exploding_burning`), so an unresolved material is the
  material of a unit we have not named - the dependency runs unit -> material, not the other
  way round.

  66.4 `tools/twin_by_refs.py --write` over the whole residual: 661 targets had a twin,
  1,484,090 candidates, 0.

  66.5 THE COMMON CAUSE is the one from 64.5: these words are in no vocabulary we hold, and
  every method above can only reshuffle a name we already have.  What breaks the wall is a
  *body* (65.1, three units) or a *spelling convention* (64.7, four clips) - both live in the
  assets and not in our word lists.

  66.6 NEW SWEEP.  `sweeps/find_new.sh` runs this session's four finders in sequence
  (`anim_shared_vocab`, `unit_guess_by_body`, `anim_twin_by_size`, `anim_mutate_loop`) and
  merges into the overlay.  Like the standing battery, it is meant to be re-run after every
  batch of new names.

  66.7 WHICH BODIES ACTUALLY CARRY NAMES.  The "read the body" route (65.1) only exists where
  a body stores names as plain ASCII, so every type was audited for it - bodies that contain
  at least one `name`-shaped string (lower-case, `_` or `/` separated):

    type            bodies  with names   strings   what they are
    shader_library     362         357     7,084   parameter names (ambient_top_color), not
                                                    resource names
    shader              64          64       825   the same
    unit                48          48       119   node names - these *are* the names (65.1)
    texture            672          40        80   DDS header noise
    timpani_bank        37          28       135   Ogg stream noise
    level               12          12        75   references, i.e. real names - but only of
                                                    resources we already hold (0 hits over the
                                                    679 level bodies, all 60 distinct strings
                                                    already resolved)
    flow                 4           4        12
    animation          539           0         0
    particles          161           0         0
    material           207           0         0
    package              2           0         0

  So the route is closed for animations, particles, materials and packages - their bodies are
  rigged motion, DXBC, shader parameter blocks and hashed tables, none of which spell a name.
  It is open exactly where it paid: units.

  66.8 SHADER LIBRARY PARAMETERS DO NOT GIVE THE CHAIN.  A library body does carry its
  parameter names, and those correlate with the flags in its name (`diffuse_map` ->
  `diffuse_map`, `billboard` -> `c_billboard`, `static_lighting` -> `static_lighting`), so
  the flags were recovered from the parameters and scored against the 453 known libraries:
  recall 0.831, but only **5 of 453** libraries are recovered *exactly*, and an unresolved
  body proposes ~62 flags - the marker sets are shared globals (`ambient_top_color`,
  `c_per_object`) rather than discriminators.  Too broad to build a chain from; closed.

67. THE VFX AND WEAPON HUNT: THE EFFECT SENTENCE IS BUILT FROM ABILITIES

  67.1 WHERE THE GAPS ARE.  The grouped view of section 66.6 showed the residual to be VFX
  and hero equipment - exactly as it looks from the type totals:

    resource_packages/all_effects               171 rows  material 69, particles 68, texture 32, shader 2
    resource_packages/heroes/necromagus_ability  39       animation 14, material 7, particles 7, shader 2, texture 9
    resource_packages/heroes/wizard_base         39       animation 26, material 3, particles 3, texture 7
    resource_packages/heroes/warrior_base        38       animation 25, material 3, particles 3, texture 7
    resource_packages/heroes/necromagus_base     34       animation 17, material 5, particles 5, texture 7
    resource_packages/heroes/valkyrie_base       29       animation 15, material 3, particles 3, texture 8
    resource_packages/heroes/elf_base            28       animation 15, material 3, particles 3, texture 7
    resource_packages/heroes/warrior_equipment   15       material 6, particles 6, shader 1, texture 2
    resource_packages/heroes/*_capes             3 each   texture 3

  Reading the bundles rather than counting them gave two corrections that matter.  In
  `all_effects` the **material and the particles share one name and one hash** - 69 against 68
  is the same resources counted in two type slots, so one effect name pays twice.  And the
  three unresolved textures in *each* of the five cape packs are the **same three hashes**
  (2,796,372 / 699,220 / 699,220 bytes): one cape set, shipped once, referenced by every hero.

  67.2 THE EIGHT NAMES.  An effect name is a sentence -
  `effects/<prefix>_<faction>_<ability>_<phase>` -

    effects/cha_cultist_sorcerer_beam_windup
    effects/boss_circle_turn_outer

  and `tools/effects_family.py` already sweeps that shape with the **unit** vocabulary (its
  stems are unit directories).  That is the wrong half of the sentence: it scored 0 across
  all ten prefixes, ~2.7 M candidates each.  Driving the same shape with the **ability** names
  we already hold - every leaf under `characters/*/abilities/`, `*/animations/`, `*/weapons/`
  plus every prefix and suffix of those leaves - named eight in one run
  (`tools/effects_by_ability.py`):

    7d18b518b2650be4  effects/wep_elf_arrow_snipershot_pierce_trail
    1df09ec7da3a8fd2  effects/wep_necromagus_heavy_shadowbolt_hit
    8e05da3e161ce35e  effects/wep_necromagus_heavy_shadowbolt_trail
    ab620c86ce595d70  effects/wep_necromagus_ultimate_remnant_spawn
    a3656cd886df16ea  effects/wep_necromagus_ultimate_souleater_trail
    d62a9c93e91c2b9d  effects/wep_necromagus_ultimate_remnant_idle
    35565841792929ea  effects/wep_necromagus_ultimate_remnant_explode
    48f1d20eb9fa6cdc  effects/cha_spider_queen_egg_projectile_trail

  Eight names cleared 34 rows: 17,912 -> 17,946 named (89.71%), 900 -> 892 unique.  The
  rounding is the material/particles doubling and the several packs each effect ships in, and
  the referrer graph confirms the shape from the other side - `effects/wep_necromagus_ultimate_souleater_trail`
  is referenced by `characters/avatar_necromagus/abilities/ultimate_souleater`.

  67.3 CLOSED, WITH NUMBERS.  Every one of these is 0 against the residual:

    * the *item* half of the sentence: `effects/<p>_<faction>_<item>_<tail>`,
      `effects/<p>_<item>_<tail>` and `effects/<faction>_<item>_<tail>` with 113 weapon and
      equipment stems - the weapon VFX are not named after the weapon.
    * a second tail token (`%t_%t` and `%t%t`) on the five heroes, and ability-before-faction
      (`effects/<p>_<ability>_<faction>_<tail>`).
    * all 19 `effects/<p>_` prefixes rather than the main ten - the space is exhausted.
    * every texture derivation from the *referrer*: `<referrer stem>_<channel>`, the
      referrer's own directory, `effects/textures/<stem>`, `<hero>/abilities/<stem>_<channel>`
      and `equipment/<hero>/<stem>_<channel>` (65,535 candidates).  An effect's texture is
      *shared art*, not a derivative of the effect's name.
    * channel completion over every equipment and effects name (70,859); the cape *meshes*'
      stems (they are `cape_s0N_r0M` and their textures are not `<stem>_<channel>`); the known
      effect-texture prefixes crossed with the known words and the pool; and
      `shaders/<effect>` / `effects/meshes/<effect>` (2,935).
    * the size-family rule on textures: 142 of the unresolved textures are exactly 349,684
      bytes, which is the size of the known `*_norm` maps - they are normal maps whose base
      names we do not hold either.  Size says *what kind* of art a texture is, never which.

  67.4 WHAT IS LEFT.  `all_effects` 155 rows (61 effect names + 32 shared textures + 2
  shaders); the five `*_equipment` packs (15/9/7/5); the five `*_capes` (3 each - three names
  would clear all fifteen); five hero `*_banks`, one audio bank each; and the hero `*_base`
  packs, which are animations (26/25/17/15/15) and belong to the animation bucket, not to VFX.

  State after this hunt: overlay 6,916 names, 17,946/20,004 rows named (89.71%), 892 unique
  unresolved hashes.

68. THE SHADER-CHAIN NEIGHBOURHOOD IS WORTH FIFTEEN NAMES

  68.1 WHY THE CHAINS ARE WORTH CHASING.  The grouped view (§66.6) puts 350 of the missing
  rows in three bundles - `core/rendering/renderer` 137, `mobile_renderer` 108,
  `emulate_renderer` 105 - and they are the *same* chains in all three, so one name pays three
  times.  A chain name is `<technique>:<flag>:<flag>...` with the flags alphabetically sorted
  (§41), so the space is enumerable: 43 techniques, 110 flags, chain lengths 2-10 (the corpus
  holds 88 chains of length 2, 66 of 3, 61 of 6, 56 of 5, 49 of 4, 41 of 7, 25 of 8).

  68.2 WHAT FOUND THEM.  `tools/make_chain_candidates.py` - the project's own one-flag
  neighbourhood (insert, drop or swap one flag, re-sorted) - run **greedily**: its seeds are
  the chains we hold, so every find widens the next pass.  Four passes of ~5.3 M candidates:

    pass 1   6    character:double_sided:enemy:one_bit_alpha:skinned_3weights, ...
    pass 2   7    base:instanced:diffuse_map:material_map:normal_map, base:instanced:diffuse_map, ...
    pass 3   2    base:instanced:diffuse_map:self_illumination_map, ...
    pass 4   0    converged

  Fifteen names, and because each is live in three renderer bundles they cleared 39 rows:
  17,946 -> 17,985 named (89.91%), 892 -> 877 unique.

  68.3 CLOSED AFTERWARDS, WITH NUMBERS.  With the fifteen in the corpus the space was re-swept
  and everything reachable from here is exhausted: the same tool to convergence (pass 4); the
  same flag set under every other technique (`<tech>:<flags of a known chain>`, 141,581
  candidates - how a renderer variant would be found); the whole sorted-subset space of every
  technique's own flag vocabulary at lengths 2-4 (38,455); and the decal family with two free
  flags (`base:instanced:deferred_decals_group_*:<f1>:<f2>`, 141,581).  What is left of the
  shader bucket is 5+ flag chains, where a technique's size band (§60.14) is the only
  constraint - `hunt_shader_subsets.py`'s territory, already swept.

  68.4 NOT FOR WANT OF TRYING: THE ACHIEVEMENT ICONS.  The thirteen unresolved 43,836-byte
  textures are exactly the size of the `achievement_icons/*` pairs (`x` and `x_locked`), and
  achievement names are English titles (`as_luck_would_have_it`, `besting_the_beast_of_orox`,
  `captains_special`).  The *known* ones are only sporadically present in our mined strings
  (`bombardier` in `loc_words`/`strings_pool`/`bundle_strings`, `artful_dodger` in
  `bundle_strings` alone), and slugging every harvested string - 1,026,408 slugs, 3,079,204
  candidates, with apostrophes dropped the way the game drops them (`Captain's Special` ->
  `captains_special`) - finds none of the missing thirteen.  The achievement block is not in
  the strings we harvested.

  State: overlay 6,931 names, 17,985/20,004 rows named (89.91%), 877 unique unresolved.

69. MUTATE, SIZED RIGHT, AND WHAT THE LAST LOOK ESTABLISHED

  69.1 MUTATE'S COST MODEL, WHICH IS EASY TO GET WRONG.  `mutate` does `tokens x suffixes`
  *per seed* - not per run - so a "big pool" run can be astronomically large before the
  engine says anything (`--tokens` 245,000 with `--suffixes` 10,661 against 1,432 seeds is
  1.5e11 candidates and never returns; the right-sized version, 2,000 x 200, is 5.7e8 and
  finishes).  All of these are 0: the package-scoped `pkg_mutate.sh` over the biggest
  packages, a new `sweeps/mutate_from_refs.sh` that builds **referrer-placed seeds** in the
  target's own namespace (1,432 seeds - effect textures under `effects/textures/`, ability art
  under `characters/<hero>/abilities/`, shaders under `shaders/`, per type) with the
  anim-token, strings-pool, tokens_big, `all_names` and `ic_all` pools and a wide `all_tails`
  suffix pool (10,661 tails mined from every leaf token we hold), and the self-feeding global
  mutate (all 6,329 held names as seeds).  The placement was right; the *stems* are the wall.

  69.2 THE ANIMATION RESIDUAL IS ONE FAMILY.  Grouping the unresolved animations by their
  referrer shows where they really live: 184 of them are referenced out of the `debug` bundle
  (the audit falls back to the bundle name when the referring resource is itself unnamed),
  then the hero base packs.  And their sizes say which family: 332 bytes x40, 696 x17, 444 x16.
  Exactly one known animation is 332 bytes - `equipment/elf/animations/aim` and `drawn` - and
  444/696 are the spawner clips (`stage1_idle` ... `stage3_idle`, `dormant`, `stage6_hit`).
  So the largest unresolved animation cluster is aim/draw-class clips and spawner stage clips
  for entities whose *names* we do not hold.

  69.3 MY DIRECTORY LIST HAD A SCOPE GAP - AND FIXING IT DID NOT HELP.  Every animation sweep
  so far used directories derived from the *referrers* (`anim_dirs_ctx.txt`), which is why
  `equipment/<hero>/animations/` was never tried even though `equipment/elf/animations/aim`
  is a name we hold.  The fix - every prefix of every known name crossed with `animations`
  and `animation`, times the corpus leaves, the ability stems and number respellings - is
  0.  The gap was real; it just was not the obstacle.

  69.4 WHAT THE BLOCKS AND PROPS ARE MISSING.  `modules/d01_crypt/resources` (116 rows) and
  its sibling level packs are the same story from the other side: 349,684-byte textures x22
  in one bundle, and every known texture of that size in the corpus is a `*_norm` map.  They
  are normal maps of bases we do not hold, and note the corpus spells the channel both ways
  (`blocks/d01_lava/lavastruct_lavaflow_normal` beside `..._norm`), so a channel sweep has to
  carry `normal`, `norm`, `diff`, `diffuse`, `spec`, `hm`, `m`, `illum`, `normal_aware`.

  68.5 WHY THE SUBSET SWEEPS ARE MOSTLY EMPTY - AND WHAT THAT MEANS.  A subset sweep only
  works if its flag vocabulary matches the chains.  Per technique it does not: `base` has 31
  flags, `character`/`mobile_filter`/`filter` 7 each, `billboard` 13 - so `base`'s own
  sorted-subset space at length 5 is only 169,911 candidates and is 0.  Yet the chains found
  here mix vocabularies (`character:double_sided:enemy:one_bit_alpha:skinned_3weights` is
  filter-flavoured flags under `character`; `base:instanced:diffuse_map` is instancing under
  `base`).  The follow-up therefore enumerates `<technique>:<sorted subset of the *global*
  152-flag vocabulary>` - `tools/chain_global_subsets.py`, chunked by technique and k because
  C(152,4) is 21 M and C(152,5) is 124 M.  Recorded so the next reader does not re-run the
  per-technique version and conclude the space is closed.

  State after this round: overlay 6,903 names, 906 -> 905 unique unresolved hashes.
